# Project Mu: Automated Media Engine & Simulation Network

## 1. System Overview
An automated, event-driven platform and media ecosystem for a simulated NFL universe. The architecture combines:
1. **Canonical Temporal PostgreSQL Database**: Real-time snapshot ledgers storing game states, team standings, player stats, cultural metrics, and fantasy projections.
2. **FastAPI Backend (`server/api.py`)**: High-performance RESTful API powering schedules, box scores, team hubs, player profiles, and fantasy analytics.
3. **Advanced Quantitative Analytics**:
   - **Excitement Index ($E$)**: Scores games (0–100) based on pace, margin, and star performances.
   - **Team Watchability Index ($TWI$)**: Measures continuous consumer entertainment value and chaos volatility.
   - **Franchise Popularity Index ($POP$)** & **Player Popularity ($Pop_{\text{player}}$)**.
   - **Fantasy Defensive Strength vs. Position**: Quantile-balanced 5-star matchup and Strength of Schedule (SOS) ratings.
4. **Agent Swarm Architecture (192+ Agents)**: Autonomous journalists, beat reporters, and pundits generating contextual articles and social posts.
5. **Interactive UI (`Mu_Network`)**: A React 19 + TypeScript + Vite frontend featuring Gridiron Newsroom, Agent Wire Social, and Mu Fantasy Analytics.

### 1.1 Repository Layout
The project root is the Python import root. Every backend folder is a package, and modules import each other by absolute package path (e.g. `from db.db_handler import DatabaseHandler`, `from newsroom.swarm_dispatcher import SwarmDispatcher`).

```
Project Mu/
├── PROJECT_SPEC.md            # This document (system-level spec)
├── CLAUDE.md                  # Claude Code session rules (read this spec first; keep it updated)
├── GEMINI.md                  # Same session rules for Gemini agents
├── NOTES.md                   # Feature documentation & engineering notes (draft prospects & OCR pipeline)
├── madden_capture.py          # Remote Play capture (macOS + chiaki-ng): drives Madden franchise menus with held keypresses,
│                              #   checks every screen via Apple Vision OCR, screenshots Season Stats (NFL scope, all categories;
│                              #   Madden lists the top ~270 per category; Passing/Defense/Kicking: each page also captured scrolled right at the same row position, *_right_NNN.png;
│                              #   confirm_end=True prevents dropped Down presses from prematurely aborting wide lists like Defense; prior weekly capture page verification (`prior_category_pages`): a category more than 2 pages short of last week is deleted and re-captured once after `list_to_top()`; if the retry is still short, `capture_category` raises Abort so the category is never written to stats/done.txt)
│                              #   Team Stats tabs Conversions / Red Zone Efficiency / Penalties (NFL scope, 32 teams),
│                              #   the previous week's League Schedule (week picked via the held-L2 dropdown, ordered Season Weeks 1-18 → Wild Card → Divisional → Conference → Super Bowl → Preseason Weeks 1-4; on Pro Bowl week Madden opens the schedule to Preseason Week 1 because there are no games that week, recognized as POST SEASON PRO BOWL so select_week steps Up to the Super Bowl and Conference Championship preview), and the 32-team Injury Report
│                              #   (Manage Roster > Injury Report, cycled via single L2 presses, no vertical scrolling; recognized with table or when empty via NO INJURIES TO REPORT banner) → captures/<YYYY-MM-DD_HHMM>/{stats,team_stats,schedule,injuries,transactions}/
│                              #   → League > LEAGUE TRANSACTIONS (team management step 2; `LEAGUE_TRANSACTIONS` screen, list headed `2036 SEASON - WEEK 4`; never Cross on it):
│                              #   transactions/page_NNN.png (3-row page steps, 4 rows show; no title, opens on NFL / ALL, Down moves a highlight, no Cross on rows: JR 2026-10-19), stopping after the first page whose every heading orders before `--since "<heading>"`
│                              #   (the newest heading already loaded; weekly_capture passes `<season> SEASON - WEEK 1` when nothing is loaded; without it, the whole list), plus meta.json (since, first/last heading, pages, reached_end) and done.txt;
│                              #   `capture_list(stop=...)` is the cutoff hook. In the weekly and playoff runs the phase is optional (`run_transactions_optional`): a failure is logged and its folder
│                              #   removed so the week goes on. `--only transactions [--since ...]` runs it alone (a weekly resume starts the list over; a backfill resume scrolls back to its last page).
│                              #   The League-menu Cross guard is by name (`LEAGUE_CROSS_OK`: STATS & AWARDS, SCHEDULE, LEAGUE TRANSACTIONS; RELOCATE OR REBUILD and LEAGUE HISTORY refused, D7).
│                              #   Cross on the League Schedule list only opens a played game's box score (week before the current one, BOX SCORE button, no ACTIONS, FORCE WIN only as the column header, highlighted row has a final score)
│                              #   `--only games [--week ...] [--games N]`: every game of a week (default the previous one): League Schedule → guarded Cross → box score
│                              #   (box_top.png, box_bottom.png: 17 team-stat rows) → R1 player stats (TEAM column, 10 categories via R2, 6-row page steps; Kicking/Defense/Defensive
│                              #   captured at both edges, end judged on the named half; traverse_game supports both NAME and PLAYER column headers across franchise and postgame screens) → Back → next game; games/<NN>_<away>_at_<home>/{box_*.png,stats/}, games/games.txt. ~7 min a game (wide tables crossed by holding Left/Right; lists stop at all-zero pages), so it's only for recovering a skipped week
│                              #   `--only postgame` (or `--only superbowl`): in-game postgame stats capture right after playing (e.g. Super Bowl): handles starting on POSTGAME_MENU (FINISH GAME / PLAYER STATS / TEAM STATS), BOX_SCORE (postgame TEAM STATS tab: box_top.png, box_bottom.png), or GAME_STATS (postgame PLAYER STATS tab: R1/L1 tab navigation, R2 category cycle) → games/01_super_bowl/{box_*.png,stats/}, writes week.json with POST SEASON SUPER BOWL; VIEW HIGHLIGHTS captures all quarters (4TH down through 1ST) via resolution-independent normalized underline detection (active_highlight_quarter) with Left-press retries into games/01_super_bowl/highlights/; GAME_WIDE_CATEGORIES includes DEFENSE, DEFENSIVE, and KICKING, normalizing postgame DEFENSIVE to defense filenames; the postgame table pins TEAM/PLAYER, so traverse_game judges a wide table's edges by its horizontal scrollbar thumb (game_scroll_thumb: thumb touching the track ends, same geometry on the franchise box score), falling back to headers when there is no scrollbar; postgame categories: Defensive, Kicking, Punting, Kick Return, Punt Return, Passing, Rushing, Receiving, Blocking (no General)
│                              #   Every mode survives a frozen, crashed or closed chiaki-ng stream (`recover_stream` also relaunches when the chiaki-ng process is gone): on a failure, a harmless test press (Down/Up on lists, Right/Left on tiles, R1/L1 on the hub) that doesn't
│                              #   change the picture means frozen → force-quit + relaunch (8 restarts per run, shared by all modes): Season Stats resumes per category (discarding partial
│                              #   screenshots of the failed category; stats/done.txt tracks finished categories; a re-captured category takes the first unused index, so Defense keeps `05_`), rosters resume per team (discarding partial folders; roster/done.txt),
│                              #   injuries resume per team (seeding from existing captures), games resume per game (games/<NN>/DONE), season scan resumes per phase (skipping completed season_schedule via done.txt/18 weeks, rosters via done.txt, team_overalls via done.txt, injuries via done.txt, contracts via contracts/done.txt, and draft prospects via .prospects_done), team/schedule from scratch; contracts resume per team (contracts/done.txt, targets.txt). Cross guard: on Manage Roster only ROSTER, INJURY REPORT, SCOUTING and TEAM SALARY (`ROSTER_CROSS_OK`); TRADE, FREE AGENCY, CONTRACTS and Cross on the Team Salary table are refused
│                              #   `--only season`: start-of-season cadence run: captures the 18-week schedule (~3 pages a week), all 32 team rosters (~80 s a team), all 32 Team Salary screens (contracts, right after rosters so every contract row matches a player; skipped on --resume once contracts/done.txt lists 32 teams), the 32 team overalls and rankings cards, the 32-team injury report, and the college draft board row-by-row, in that exact order (draft prospect scan after schedule, rosters, team overalls, and injuries)
│                              #   `--only contracts [--team-names PANTHERS,SAINTS]` (team management step 1, docs/team_management/01_CONTRACT_SCAN_PLAN.md): Manage Roster > TEAM SALARY (read-only; TEAM_SALARY classified by its cap strip ROLLOVER + CAP SPACE + ROOKIE RES/TOTAL SPEND, before ROSTER_LIST), teams cycled with L2 from the one it opens on; every team's list is scrolled to the top first (L2 can keep the previous team's row; it wraps VIKINGS → BEARS), then `capture_wide_list` (12 rows show, pages step 10; `settle=True` waits after every scroll until two frames match, since Madden can apply a burst of Down presses seconds late; `pair_check=salary_pair_aligned` compares both halves' BONUS column with Apple Vision and re-shoots a pair that isn't at the same row, twice at most; `salary_traverse`: left edge = NAME header, right edge = NO TRADE CLAUSE, confirm_end) → contracts/<NN>_<team>/salary_NNN.png + salary_right_NNN.png; contracts/targets.txt (the run's teams, written on the first run; a resume without --team-names keeps it), contracts/done.txt (`NN TEAM`, written once a team's pages are complete; unfinished folders removed on start and after a failure); a team whose list ends before 3 pages raises (the list didn't scroll) and is retried once, then the run stops; stops if L2 wraps to the first team before every target is done. ~40 min for 32 teams, ~1.5 min a team
│                              #   `--only season-schedule`: League Schedule weeks 1-18 via the week dropdown (~3 pages a week) → season_schedule/
│                              #   `--only roster`: Manage Roster > Roster for all 32 teams (team via the held-L2 dropdown, starting at the user's team);
│                              #   per row the player card is read on the Mac (Apple Vision on an in-process window grab, settled = two agreeing reads,
│                              #   each step checked against the list, by name or by the neighbouring line's position and age: a skipped row is stepped back, an unverifiable step stops the run; a card that stops changing anywhere but the last row stops the team, since a frozen chiaki-ng stream looks like the end of the list; when a team or team switch fails and a harmless test press doesn't change the screen, the stream is force-quit and relaunched (CHIAKI_HOST / CHIAKI_HOST_IP from env or .env, up to 8 times per run) and the team redone; the team chip must read as one of the 32 teams; a team's rows reach cards.csv only once it is complete) → roster/cards.csv (team,row,first,last,number,
│                              #   position,archetype,role,dev,age,height,weight,experience,flags,card_png; experience 0 = ROOKIE; flags name missing or
│                              #   implausible values, e.g. Vision drops the digit 1) + roster/<NN>_<team>/card_NNN.png (card crop) and list_NN.png (one full
│                              #   screenshot per 14-row list page, for Gemini). ~80 s per team
│                              #   `--only team-overalls` (or `--only team-ratings`): captures all 32 team overalls and rankings cards from Weekly Hub → OPTIONS tab → USER TEAMS tile → Triangle into Choose Team grid (4 cols x 8 rows wrapping grid in alphabetical order by nickname / city: row 0 BEARS, BENGALS, BILLS, BRONCOS ... row 7 STEELERS, TEXANS, TITANS, VIKINGS; aligns to Row 0 Col 0 BEARS, traverses row-by-row) → captures/<YYYY-MM-DD_HHMM>/team_overalls/NN_<team>.png (full screenshot per team card displaying team OVR, OFF, DEF, 2036 NFL RANKING for passing, rushing, blocking, pass rush, coverage, team speed, franchise info division, status, salary cap, and dev trait counts); returns safely via Back to Weekly Hub
│                              #   `--only draft-results` (or `--only draft-recap`): captures completed Madden Draft Recap screen from Weekly Hub → DRAFT RECAP tile → permitted Cross → held-L2 dropdown switch to NFL; paged mode (default, scrolls 10 rows per page across all rounds via capture_list) captures all 7 rounds (224 picks); `--row-by-row` captures picks row-by-row reading player cards (pick_NNN.png)
├── extract.py                 # Gemini vision pass (gemini-3.8-flash, JSON schema) over a captures/ run (a row read two ways on overlapping pages - same NAME+POS differing in 1-2 cells, or equal numbers under names one letter apart - keeps the copy seen highest above a page's bottom edge, where rows are faded or mid-scroll; a tie is re-read twice and voted; table columns cleaned of sort marks and majority-voted across pages; General category maps OVR to DP) → stats_<category>.csv (wide tables: left/right pages paired by number, rows joined by position since NAME/POS scroll away; shared columns cross-checked), team_conversions/team_redzoneefficiency/team_penalties.csv, schedule.csv, season_schedule.csv (all 18 weeks, same columns), prospects.csv (draft board: projected pick, name, pos, college, ht, wt, age, ovr), transactions.csv (League Transactions rows newest first via `pipeline/extract_transactions_ocr.py`: ORDER, PAGE, HEADING, SEASON, WEEK, TEXT, TYPE, TEAM, TO_TEAM, POS, NAME, YEARS, AMOUNT, PARSE_OK, FLAGS), injuries.csv (weekly injury report via on-device Apple Vision OCR; postseason weeks filter extraction to teams playing that week), team_overalls.csv (32-team overalls, ratings OVR/OFF/DEF, rankings passing/rushing/blocking/pass_rush/coverage/team_speed, and franchise info via on-device Apple Vision OCR); games runs → games/<NN>/box_score.csv (LABEL, away, home) and stats_<category>.csv, plus week-level game_scores.csv (quarters + totals; QUARTERS_OK = n when the quarters don't add up to the total, e.g. digits washed out by a light team colour) and game_stats_<category>.csv (GAME column; rows aligned by column name across games; wide pages read through each page's own headers, cut-off edge columns mapped to their full name, all-empty columns dropped); roster runs → roster_list.csv (TEAM + list columns from the list pages; CONV/DEV icons blank; a page with fewer than 14 rows is re-read; rows de-duplicated on NAME+POS, differing repeats printed as CONFLICT; per-team counts compared with cards.csv) and roster_cards.csv (cards.csv with AGE/HT/WT/EXP re-read by Gemini from the card crops, 20 numbered strips per request, since Apple Vision drops the digit 1 and misreads heights; vision_* columns keep Vision's values, vision_disagrees names the fields that differ) (GEMINI_API_KEY, env or root .env); Team Salary (team management step 1): contracts/<NN>_<team>/salary[_right]_NNN.png read with Gemini (SALARY_LIST_BOX crop without the player card, SALARY_NOTE, a short page re-read like the roster), each page pair checked row by row on the BONUS column both halves show (a pair that never lines up after 2 re-reads stops the extract), merged with merge_wide + vote_misreads → contracts.csv (TEAM + NAME, POS, AGE, OVR, LEN, REM, TOTAL, BONUS, SAVINGS, PENALTY, five year columns, INCENTIVE, NO TRADE CLAUSE; raw strings) and the cap strip (CapStrip, SALARY_HEADER_BOX) read from salary_000 + salary_right_000, a third read of salary_001 on disagreement or a C10 identity missed by more than $3M (Madden rounds), majority kept, no majority stops → team_cap.csv (TEAM, CAP_LABEL, CAP, ROLLOVER_LABEL, ROLLOVER, ADJUSTED_CAP, CAP_SPACE, SALARIES, CAP_PENALTY, TOTAL_SPEND, ROOKIE_RES_LABEL, ROOKIE_RES)
├── README.md                  # Setup & weekly usage for madden_capture.py / extract.py (permissions, App Nap, safety rules)
├── captures/                  # madden_capture.py output (screenshots, scope_debug/ + week_debug/ L2-dropdown frames, stop_debug/ frame that caused a stop, extracted CSVs; roster/ from --only roster; team_debug/ team-dropdown frames; team_overalls/ from --only team-overalls; contracts/<NN>_<team>/salary[_right]_NNN.png + contracts/{done,targets}.txt from --only contracts and the Season scan; <date>_explore/ holds --step mapping frames); gitignored
├── server/                    # FastAPI backend
│   ├── api.py                 #   REST API + /static mount (serves ../static) + the built site (Mu_Network/dist) at /, mounted last
│   ├── social_api.py          #   Agent Wire read queries behind /api/social/* (feed, post, profile, trends, search, Team Hubs)
│   ├── debug_api.py           #   /api/debug/*: the #debug page (league state, newsroom article cleanup & rerun, table browser + cell edits, endpoints, docs, run jobs; §4.5)
│   └── napper_api.py          #   Napper interactive fantasy platform endpoints behind /api/napper/* (profiles, matchups, waivers, trades, chat; §4.4b)
├── pipeline/                  # Video ingestion & OCR extraction (Section 6, step 1)
│   ├── main.py                #   End-to-end pipeline entrypoint (--file / --stream, --db, --workers)
│   ├── weekly_capture.py      #   Weekly run, started from #debug or by hand: connect, capture, extract, load, backfill + engines, articles, shrink;
│   │                          #   --no-articles, --mode games (last week's games, loaded with --replace), --mode season (schedule, rosters, contracts, team overalls, then draft board), --mode contracts [--teams CAR,NO] (Team Salary capture → extract → load, no engines or articles; `contracts_phase(folder, log_path, teams)` is the lock-free capture/extract/load step), --mode transactions (League Transactions alone: capture, extract, load; no engines or articles); weekly/playoff runs pass `--since` (newest loaded heading); nothing re-scans Team Salary automatically, --mode postgame (Super Bowl postgame screen), --mode team-overalls (team overalls scan); stops + notifies on a failed step; --resume
│   ├── notify.py              #   ntfy phone notices (NTFY_TOPIC / NTFY_SERVER from env or .env); env() reads .env settings
│   ├── ocr_extractor.py       #   Gemini box-score extraction + Pydantic stat models
│   ├── load_transactions.py   #   League Transactions load (§3.3e): expand rows to legs + keys, pure plan_effects (state in, ops out), read_state / execute
│   │                          #   inside load_capture's transaction; trade rows and non-moves are skipped
│   ├── load_capture.py        #   Loads an extracted captures/<run> into the DB, one transaction, checks first (mode from the folder; `transactions` mode = transactions.csv
│   │                          #   without stats/games; weekly/playoff runs load transactions.csv before Season Stats; roster built so far; monotonic lower-bound row count check with 2-row margin and 30%/floor 20 upper-bound expansion check; unmatched passing/rushing/receiving players only: crops 700x120 row snippets to unmatched_crops/, prompts interactively if tty or dumps to unmatched_players.json and raises LoadError; load_team_overalls updates team_seasons team_ovr, off_ovr, def_ovr, pass_off_rank, rush_off_rank, pass_rush_rank, coverage_rank); `contracts` mode (contracts.csv alone; in season mode `load_season` runs it right after `load_roster`): year check (the cap strip's `36 CAP` = 2036 must equal the season, the year columns must be that year + 4, ROOKIE RES the next year, all teams agreeing; an offseason scan stops here by design), teams = contracts/targets.txt (else all 32), 45–56 rows a team, every money cell through parse_money, 1 ≤ LEN, 0 ≤ REM ≤ LEN, rows matched by `match_contract_rows` (any unmatched/ambiguous row stops the load, nothing written), then `supersede_active` + `insert_contract` + `upsert_team_cap` with the run's capture id (`report['capture_id']`); C10 identity misses over $3M and year-0 cap hits vs SALARIES are `cap_warnings`, not fatal; `incentives` lists every player whose INCENTIVE cell is YES (stored as `YES`; NO → NULL; the incentive's name is only on the player card) for JR to look up; `fuzzy_matches` lists rows matched across a one-letter OCR slip
│   ├── resolve_player.py      #   Interactive CLI to resolve unmatched players from capture extraction (shows candidate matches from players, links or creates players and player_seasons, removes resolved entries from unmatched_players.json)
│   ├── money.py               #   parse_money(str) -> int | None: Madden money cells ($16.8M, $200K, -$29.5M, ($1.2M)) to whole dollars via Decimal; blank/-/N/A → None, OCR junk raises ValueError (team management §2.1)
│   ├── contracts.py           #   Contract/cap writes shared by the Team Salary load and step 2's transactions load: supersede_active (team's + listed players' ACTIVE rows → SUPERSEDED), insert_contract (+ one player_contract_years row per year, source SCAN | TRANSACTION), upsert_team_cap (latest wins)
│   ├── capture_matching.py    #   Name keys (initial + last name; `Q.St. Louis` keeps its multi-letter `St.` as part of the last name, while `T.J.` initials collapse to the first), roster matching in passes (list name first; rows whose full_name belongs to another player don't count),
│   │                          #   match_contract_rows (Team Salary rows → players: team + name key, then position, AGE, OVR; a name no teammate has matches only the team's single player of the same position and age whose name is one OCR slip away (`one_slip`), noted; 0 or >1 candidates or two rows on one player is a problem, never guessed)
│   │                          #   Season Stats history assignment for shared names
│   ├── transactions_parse.py  #   League Transactions rows, pure (§3.3e): heading clean-up/ordinal/week, `is_move` (rows with no move verb and no (POS) NAME are skipped), row grammar (sign, release, re-sign incl. "RE-SIGN (QB) NAME TO A 4-YEAR, $120M EXTENSION",
│   │                          #   extension, tolerant trade legs, else OTHER), row_key, seq (oldest first per heading), heading-order fixes
│   ├── template_classifier.py #   Screen-state classifier (TEMPLATE_DIR = pipeline/templates/)
│   ├── templates/             #   Reference screenshots for each Madden UI state
│   ├── ingest_stream.py, sort_video.py, batch_historical.py, batch_menus.py
│   ├── extract_team_overalls_ocr.py # Apple Vision OCR extraction for Madden Team Overalls and Rankings cards (team_overalls/*.png -> team_overalls.csv)
│   ├── extract_prospects_ocr.py # Apple Vision OCR extraction for draft board row captures (prospect_*.png -> prospects.csv, ordered by on-screen Round/Pick); HT/WT read glyph by glyph
│   ├── assets/prospect_htwt_digits.png # 0-9 digit templates (18x34 each) for the card's HT/WT glyph reader
│   ├── extract_injuries_ocr.py  # Apple Vision OCR extraction for weekly injury report captures (injuries/*.png -> injuries.csv; in postseason weeks, filters extraction to only teams playing games that week)
│   ├── extract_transactions_ocr.py # Apple Vision OCR for League Transactions pages (transactions/page_*.png -> transactions.csv): rows = heading + body lines
│   │                          #   (wrapped bodies joined), a row = its heading + the one line under it (half-visible rows at the bottom edge, the News Center ticker and text left of the list are ignored), non-move rows dropped, pages lined up on the longest overlap that fits (1–4 rows: Down moves a highlight before the list scrolls; rows match by key or ≥ 92% text similarity, one garbled row allowed in an overlap of 3+; identical rows kept; no overlap flags gap?),
│   │                          #   heading-order fixes, Gemini re-read of rows that fail the grammar / break order / lack terms / name an unknown team
│   ├── extract_draft_results.py # Apple Vision OCR extraction for draft recap page captures (draft_results/page_*.png -> draft_results.csv & draft_results.json across all 7 rounds)
│   ├── ingest_prospects.py    #   Draft prospect CSV parsing and validation (strict, or lenient: bad cells → None with warnings)
│   ├── draft_board.py         #   draft_prospects / mock_draft_picks reads and writes (board, rookie link, saved mock drafts)
│   ├── draft_results.py       #   Round 1 actual draft results ingestion and 32-pick validation engine (§3.3c)
│   └── roster_scanner.py, schedule_scanner.py, heal_roster.py
├── engines/                   # Deterministic analytics (Sections 2 & 6, steps 2–3)
│   ├── game_stat_generator.py #   CPU-sim backfill (offense, defense, kicking, punting, blocking, returns) → culture engines (no swarm; see newsroom/weekly_run.py);
│   │                          #   a released player (player_seasons.released_week) gets no line from his release week on (`_player_weights`); trades need nothing (lines deducted across teams)
│   ├── backfill_team_stats.py
│   ├── team_box_estimator.py  #   Per-game team conversions, red zone, penalties from team_seasons totals → team_stats (§2.6)
│   ├── gotw_engine.py         #   Game Excitement Index (E) → games.excitement_index; E components, player matching, 70–100 display scale
│   ├── excitement_projection.py #  Projected E for scheduled games → games.projected_excitement (+ --backtest)
│   ├── twi_engine.py, popularity_engine.py, player_popularity_engine.py
│   ├── market_constants.py    #   B / L / D / N / Legacy Floor per franchise + SB hardware bumps derived from games
│   ├── game_credit_engine.py  #   Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit (SRS) + Trajectory Multiplier → team_seasons.opp_adj_credit, sos_rating, expected_wins, trajectory_multiplier
│   ├── team_efficiency_engine.py # Pythagorean wins, team pace/production → team_seasons
│   ├── coty_engine.py         #   Coach of the Year race (§2.11) → coty_scores
│   ├── coach_profile.py       #   Head-coach profiles, coordinator staff, and staff matchups for writer tools (§3.3a; computed live, cutoff-aware)
│   ├── coordinator_seeder.py  #   Seeds head coach slants (OFFENSIVE/DEFENSIVE/CEO) and baseline OC/DC coordinator tenures (§3.3a)
│   ├── tendency_engine.py     #   Observed pass lean and fourth-down aggressiveness per team-season (§2.12) → team_seasons
│   ├── player_splits.py       #   Player splits by kind of defense faced, real game lines only (§2.13; computed live)
│   ├── playoff_clout_engine.py #  Team & player Playoff Clout → team_seasons.s_playoff, player_seasons.s_playoff
│   ├── matchup_edges.py       #   Matchup Edge / Trench Edge math for writer tools (computed live, not stored)
│   ├── mock_draft_engine.py   #   Round 1 Mock Draft simulation (order + team needs + draft_prospects board) → mock_draft_picks
│   ├── draft_order.py         #   Round 1 draft order with playoff-exit brackets and locked/provisional picks (§2.10)
│   ├── fantasy_adp_engine.py  #   Preseason value board (draft_score) + runs the simulated drafts → fantasy_projections week 1 adp_rank
│   ├── fantasy_league_sim.py  #   100 simulated 12-team PPR leagues: strategy drafts → ADP; weekly rostered/start %, standings, 6-team playoffs → fantasy_sim_*
│   ├── records_engine.py      #   Record book: checks a season against franchise records and the NFL top 10s (§2.9)
│   ├── game_script_engine.py  #   Game scripts (§2.14): one canonical, seeded story per completed game → game_scripts
│   ├── clutch_engine.py       #   Clutch profile (§2.15): comebacks, game-winning drives, blown leads from game scripts (computed live)
│   ├── fantasy_projection_engine.py # Weekly PPR projections, matchup stars, pos/ROS ranks → fantasy_projections (+ --backtest)
│   ├── napper_seeder.py       #   Seeds reference user @jrereyi2000, 3 reference leagues, rosters, week 6 matchups, and chat cards with 100% authentic simulation players (§3.4b)
│   ├── napper_trade_engine.py #   AI trade proposals and decisions on offers to AI teams: positional scarcity weights, bench depth safety (net incoming/outgoing starter check), IR players excluded, stochastic acceptance; trade deadline end of week 11 (TRADE_DEADLINE_WEEK; later pending offers EXPIRED); run weekly by napper_weekly.py, CLI --league N | --all-active (§4.4b)
│   ├── napper_chat_engine.py  #   Autonomous league chat banter (4 manager personas) and weekly bot award cards (benchwarmers, high scorer) (§4.4b)
│   ├── napper_injuries.py     #   Napper injury status per week (IR / O from the latest injuries report, counted down) and IR-slot eligibility
│   ├── napper_lineup_engine.py #  Weekly AI Napper lineups: IR moves, roster-limit drops (to waivers), greedy starters by projection; matchup projection refresh; trade slot refill (fill_empty_starter_slots); shared roster helpers (roster_capacity, release_player, add_player)
│   ├── napper_waiver_engine.py #  Weekly Napper waiver run: user claims + AI pickups (NEED / STREAM / UPGRADE) in reverse-standings order; waiver wire
│   ├── napper_playoffs.py     #   Napper fantasy playoffs: 6-team bracket in weeks 15–17 (seeds 1–2 bye), round matchups written as weeks go final, champion + trophy (§4.4b)
│   ├── napper_weekly.py       #   Weekly Napper step per active league: playoffs sync → AI lineups → waiver run (week 2+, once per week) → AI trade proposals (once per week, through week 11) → offers to AI teams decided (from week 12: pending offers expire instead) → lineups again → current_week
│   ├── napper_draft_engine.py #   Interactive league snake drafts; standalone mock drafts run client-side and are persisted only when saved as a league
│   ├── gm_personas.py         #   GM agents (team management step 3, §3.3f): 32 seeded GM personas (stratified traits, template or Gemini bio), gm_team_seasons rows, draft_picks seed for the next 3 drafts; CLI seed / backfill-bios
│   ├── gm_direction.py        #   Team direction (CONTEND/RETOOL/REBUILD) and positional needs/surplus per team-season (§2.16) → gm_team_seasons; CLI --season --week --dry-run
│   ├── trade_value.py         #   Trade value model (§2.17): market worth fit, aging fit, player surplus by team, pick curve; --report
│   ├── gm_trade_engine.py     #   Weekly GM trade proposals (§2.17): expiry, injury triggers, budget pacing, two-team deal search, legality, acceptance, steps text, store; --dry-run, --replace, --simulate-season
│   ├── gm_rationale.py        #   Trade rationale: fact sheet, template headline + each GM's two sentences (§2.17)
│   └── gm_llm.py              #   The GM agents' Gemini seam: make_llm(label) → metered prompt→text callable or None (template fallback); tests pass fakes

├── newsroom/                  # Agent swarm (Section 7)

```

### 1.2 Running the System
All Python commands run **from the project root** (relative data paths such as `data/...` and `static/...` resolve against it). Configuration comes from the environment: `DATABASE_URL` (defaults to the local `madden` database; the backfill, every engine, the newsroom, the capture loader and the API all read it, so pointing it at a restored test copy runs everything there) and `GEMINI_API_KEY` (the swarm also reads a root-level `.env`).

| Task | Command |
|---|---|
| API server | `python3 -m uvicorn server.api:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --reload` |
| Frontend | `cd Mu_Network && npm run dev` (Vite on port 8443) |
| Video pipeline | `python3 -m pipeline.main --file data/video/<clip>.mp4 --db` |
| Stat backfill + culture engines | `python3 -m engines.game_stat_generator` (season from `league_state.current_season`; `--season N` overrides) |
| Publish the weekly newsroom slate | `python3 -m newsroom.weekly_run` (next week; skips if already published). `--week N` for a specific week (Week 1 preseason and Week 23 Super Bowl are manual), `--force` to publish again, `--dry-run` to print prompts with no Gemini calls or writes. The Mu Fantasy slate runs in the same call after the national slate (W1–18, own already-published check); `--fantasy-only` / `--skip-fantasy` |
| Commission a custom article | `python3 -m newsroom.custom_commission --prompt "..." [--season N] [--week N] [--desk NATIONAL\|FANTASY] [--dry-run]` (classifies prompt into article type and qualified writer via Gemini Flash, runs ReAct swarm, routes to National Newsroom or Mu Fantasy Desk; also interactive Two-Step Flow via `#debug` Control tab) |
| Clear generated articles/tweets | `bash db/clear_news_feed.sh [--week N] [--season S] [--articles-only] [-y]` (backs up `news_feed`, tags, `power_rankings` and the agent-memory tables to `db/backups/` first, asks to confirm; deleting articles cascades to their takes and predictions, and agent season summaries are cleared; `--articles-only` keeps tweets; also manageable from the `#debug` Control tab with week targeting and instant rerun) |
| Re-score Excitement Index (E) | `python3 -m engines.gotw_engine --season 2035` (then refresh the culture indices below). Every engine's season (CLI `--season` and the Python `season` argument) defaults to `league_state.current_season`, as does `newsroom.weekly_run` |
| Game scripts (§2.14) | `python3 -m engines.game_script_engine` (season from `league_state`; `--season N`, `--week N`, `--game ID`; builds missing scripts and rebuilds only games whose recorded facts changed; `--force` rebuilds anyway, except scripts that an article or post published after them may cite, which also need `--unlock-published`; `--print` prints them as writers see them). The weekly run's engines step runs it after $E$ |
| Refresh culture indices (in this order) | `python3 -m engines.twi_engine` → `python3 -m engines.popularity_engine` → `python3 -m engines.player_popularity_engine` |
| Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit + Trajectory | `python3 -m engines.game_credit_engine --season 2035` (independent of the culture indices) |
| Team efficiency profile | `python3 -m engines.team_efficiency_engine --season 2035` |
| Coach of the Year race | `python3 -m engines.coty_engine --season 2035` |
| Playoff Clout | `python3 -m engines.playoff_clout_engine --season 2035` |
| Project E for scheduled games | `python3 -m engines.excitement_projection --season 2035` (`--backtest` scores accuracy, no writes) |
| Preseason ADP (runs the 100 simulated drafts once per season) | `python3 -m engines.fantasy_adp_engine --season 2035` (`--redraft` replaces a stored draft) |
| Simulated fantasy leagues (rostered %, start %, standings) | `python3 -m engines.fantasy_league_sim --season 2035` (re-simulates from the stored draft through the next unplayed fantasy week; `--summary` prints results, no writes) |
| Weekly fantasy projections | `python3 -m engines.fantasy_projection_engine --season 2035 --week 18` (no `--week`: league_state current week; `--all-weeks` backfills 1–18; `--backtest` scores accuracy, no writes) |
| Napper AI lineups | `python3 -m engines.napper_lineup_engine --all-active` (or `--league N`; `--week W`, default league_state current week; `--dry-run` prints the moves and rolls back) |
| Napper waiver run | `python3 -m engines.napper_waiver_engine --all-active` (or `--league N`, repeatable; `--week W`, default league_state current week; `--dry-run`). Normally run by the weekly step; once per league and week |
| Napper weekly step (lineups + waivers + trades) | `python3 -m engines.napper_weekly` (runs automatically after the simulated leagues; default: current season, first week with scheduled games, all active leagues; `--week W`, `--league N` (repeatable), `--no-trades`, `--no-waivers`, `--dry-run`). Trades alone: `python3 -m engines.napper_trade_engine --all-active` (or `--league N`; `--week` defaults to league_state current week; `--process-ai` decides pending offers to AI teams) |
| GM personas (team management step 3, §3.3f) | `python3 -m engines.gm_personas seed [--season S] [--replace TEAM] [--no-llm] [--dry-run]` (seeds any team without an open GM tenure, the season's `gm_team_seasons` rows and every team's own `draft_picks` for the next three drafts; idempotent; `--replace TEAM` closes that GM's tenure and seeds a new one; Gemini writes name/bio/voice when a key is set, else the template); `backfill-bios` rewrites template bios with Gemini |
| Team direction & needs (§2.16) | `python3 -m engines.gm_direction [--season S] [--week W] [--force-direction] [--dry-run]` (prints the 32 teams by D with direction and top needs; writes `gm_team_seasons`: direction only at checkpoint weeks or for a team without one, needs every run) |
| Trade value report (§2.17) | `python3 -m engines.trade_value --report [--season S]` (read-only: market fit per group, aging curve, top 40 players by league value, pick chart) |
| GM trade proposals (§2.17) | `python3 -m engines.gm_trade_engine [--season S] [--week W] [--dry-run] [--replace] [--no-llm]` (one engine run for league_state's week: expires stale proposals, writes direction/needs, raises injury triggers, works out the budget and stores 0–4 proposals; `--dry-run` reads only and prints the proposals with values, cap effects, flags and Madden steps; `--replace` removes this week's PROPOSED engine proposals first). `--simulate-season [--approval 0.6] [--seasons 2000] [--first-week 1]` replays the in-season budget and prints the mean executed count (≈12.8 for a full season at 60% approval: the 4-a-week cap binds in the 3× deadline weeks; ≈8.7 from week 4) |
| Round 1 Mock Draft simulation | `python3 -m engines.mock_draft_engine [--season N] [--limit 24] [--save] [--file captures/<run>/prospects.csv] [--json]` (Round 1 from the draft order, team needs and the `draft_prospects` board; `--save` stores it as the current week's `mock_draft_picks` run; `--file` reads an unloaded board leniently and can't be saved) |
| Debug page (league state, tables, endpoints, docs, run buttons) | Open the site's LIVE badge (`#debug`); from a phone on the home network `http://<Mac's IP>:8000/#debug`: the API also serves the built site (`Mu_Network/dist`, mounted at `/` after every route; rebuild with `cd Mu_Network && npm run build` after frontend changes), because the macOS firewall allows Python but not `node` (Vite's 8443). Edits and runs need `DEBUG_TOKEN` from `.env`, typed once on the page. See §4.5 |
| Weekly run by hand / resume a stopped week | `python3 -m pipeline.weekly_capture` (connect → capture → extract → load → backfill + engines → articles → shrink); `--resume <run>` continues `captures/<run>` from the first step not done; `--no-articles` (Backfill previous week); `--mode games` (Game scan: `madden_capture.py --only games`, extract, load with `--replace`, engines, shrink; never articles); `--mode season` (Season scan: start-of-season cadence run capturing the 18-week schedule, all 32 team rosters, all 32 Team Salary screens (contracts, after rosters, loaded right after the roster load), the 32-team injury report, and the draft board line-by-line in that exact order with prospects after schedule, rosters, and injuries; automatically rolls over `league_state` to `(current_season + 1, 1)` if `current_week != 1`; extracts, loads schedule, players, prospects, and Week 1 injuries; generates media-day headshots for incoming rookie draft class via `step_rookie_headshots`; runs preseason calibration engines: 100-league simulated ADP drafts, scheduled game excitement projections, and Week 1 fantasy projections, then Napper week 1 AI lineups (`run_napper_week(season, 1, trades=False)`; a failure only logs a warning); publishes Week 1 preseason magazine unless `--no-articles`, shrinks media); `--mode roster` (Roster scan: season cadence scan of all 32 team rosters, extracts cards, loads players, generates rookie headshots; `--no-articles`); `--mode transactions` (League transactions scan: `madden_capture.py --only transactions` from the newest heading loaded this season, or `<season> SEASON - WEEK 1` the first time; extract; load (§3.3e); shrink; no engines or articles); `--mode contracts [--teams CAR,NO]` (Contract scan: Team Salary for all 32 teams, or a by-hand re-scan (nothing triggers one automatically) of the named abbreviations resolved through `teams.abbreviation` (an unknown one stops before the console is touched), `madden_capture.py --only contracts [--team-names …]`, extract, load; no engines or articles; ~100 min, ~3 min a team, capture time limit `CONTRACTS_TIMEOUT` 150 min; started again without `--resume` (by hand or from #debug) it continues the most complete contracts run from the last 24 h whose capture was cut off and whose targets match, `unfinished_contracts_run`); `--mode prospects` (Draft prospect scan: scans draft board line-by-line, extracts prospects via Apple Vision OCR, loads the board into `draft_prospects` (§3.3c); `--no-articles`); `--mode draft-results` (Draft results scan: captures every pick across all 7 rounds, default 224 picks, extracts, loads into `draft_prospects` (§3.3c, §7.10), no articles); `--mode playoff` (chosen automatically when `league_state.current_week` is 19+; at week 23 the capture names the Super Bowl week with `--week "POST SEASON SUPER BOWL"`, since Madden is in the offseason by then, i.e. the round being captured is a playoff round, or a resumed folder holds both schedule and games screenshots: `madden_capture.py --only playoff`, a stop in the games resumes with `--only games --resume`, extract, load as one `playoff` transaction, engines, articles, shrink); Postgame scan (`run_postgame` / `--only games`): captures Team Stats, player stats across categories (wide categories Kicking, Defense, and Defensive captured at both edges with `traverse_game`, which reads each edge from the table's horizontal scrollbar thumb (`game_scroll_thumb`; the postgame table pins TEAM/PLAYER so headers can't tell its edges apart; held Left/Right in 1.5 s chunks, up to 20, since the postgame screen auto-repeats only ~1.3 columns a second) and falls back to `NAME`/`PLAYER` headers when no scrollbar shows; cleans partial captures per category prefix and normalizes defensive filenames to `05_defense_*.png`), and in-game `VIEW HIGHLIGHTS` (`capture_highlights`) across all quarters 4TH down through 1ST into `games/<NN>/highlights/` (the highlights list wraps — the stats tables don't — so a quarter ends when the top clock drops between pages or the first page's top play, once scrolled off, comes back; a Down batch that crosses the last play wraps before the bottom page shows, so `highlight_bottom_page` then steps Up one row at a time (Up from the top row wraps to the last row) and saves the quarter's bottom page; clocks are read from the TIME column in frame fractions (`highlight_clocks`, `HIGHLIGHT_CLOCK_BOX`, so it works on the 3024x1888 stream); a page that didn't move is a dropped input and gets up to `HIGHLIGHT_SCROLL_TRIES` Down batches before it counts as the end; active quarter tabs are detected via resolution-independent normalized underline coordinate analysis, with Left keypress retries, and a quarter that won't change stops the run (Abort) rather than keeping a partial set; `highlights/DONE` marks a complete set). Game stats lists (`capture_list` / `capture_wide_list` with `confirm_end`) give a repeated page one more batch of Down presses before calling it the end, since the lists never wrap and a stream restart can drop a whole batch. `box_score_bottom` allows 40 Downs (the postgame TEAM STATS table is longer) and checks the frame after the last; a postgame run that starts on TEAM STATS holds Up back to the first row before `box_top`. After a stream restart `clear_postgame` drops only unfinished parts (stats without `games/01_super_bowl/DONE`, highlights without `highlights/DONE`). Whenever the League Schedule opens on a postseason week (where Madden renames the schedule tile from LEAGUE SCHEDULE to PLAYOFF SCHEDULE and places it in the first slot; open_league_schedule dynamically adapts to the card name and tile order), the schedule step first captures that week's list into `schedule_next/` (the coming round's matchups and kickoff times; `week.json` gets `next`); on Pro Bowl week (`POST SEASON PRO BOWL`, between the Conference round and the Super Bowl; Madden's postseason order then matches `games.week` 19–23) it steps to the Super Bowl week and records that instead (`next_round`; JR confirmed Oct 14 that the Super Bowl matchup shows on its week list during Pro Bowl week). After the Super Bowl Madden is in an offseason stage with no week label (JR confirmed the League Schedule menu opens the same way there): `--week` names the week to capture, and JR's Cross rule treats the Super Bowl week as played when no current week is readable (row final score, BOX SCORE button, no ACTIONS and FORCE WIN only in the header still required); JR approved this exception, Oct 14, and confirmed the Super Bowl box score opens in the offseason, so the Week 18 run records the Wild Card matchups) (`--games N` stops after N games for testing; `--only roster` captures all 32 team rosters; `--teams N` stops after N teams for testing; `--only prospects` captures draft board prospects line-by-line; it first opens the Draft Board from anywhere in the franchise menus (`open_prospects()`, mapped Oct 15: Manage Roster > SCOUTING ✕ > Scouting menu PROSPECTS ✕ > L3 (EDIT PLAYERS; chiaki-ng key 5) > the Edit Draft Class prompt, which opens on DOWNLOAD FROM DOWNLOAD CENTER, so Down to EDIT DRAFT CLASS ✕ (JR's OK; Madden posts a notice to the league transaction log) > the board, opened on Round 1, Pick 1; screens `SCOUTING`, `SCOUT_PROSPECTS`, `EDIT_CLASS_PROMPT`, `DRAFT_BOARD`; the Prospects list's PROJ column reads "Round 1", so `DRAFT_BOARD` needs ALL ROUNDS or ADJUST ORDER; Cross is refused on the board (EDIT PLAYER), on any Scouting item but PROSPECTS and on any prompt button but EDIT DRAFT CLASS). Leaving the board (`close_draft_board()`, at the end of the capture and whenever `go_hub()` meets the board): Back opens a Draft Class Edited prompt over the board (`EXIT_CLASS_PROMPT`, checked before `DRAFT_BOARD`) that opens on EXPORT LOCAL FILE; Down twice to SAVE AND EXIT ✕ (the only button Cross is allowed on) returns to the Scouting screen; `--prospects N` stops after N prospects, `--all-prospects` captures the full board) (roster, season-schedule and games are not in the default run yet; season-schedule = League Schedule weeks 1-18 → season_schedule/season_week_NN_NNN.png; `--resume <run>` continues a stopped roster or games run in captures/<run>, skipping the teams in roster/done.txt or the games with a games/<NN>/DONE marker), `--week "SEASON WEEK 12"` (the schedule step writes `week.json` with the week the schedule opened on and the week captured, for the loader), `--test` (read screen only), `--scope-only`, `--probe-l2` (records the held-L2 dropdown from Player Stats or the Manage Roster list), `--all-rows`, `--foreground`. Playoff` → 20 and `Conf. a Super Bowl load ends the season at 23 whatever the offseason screen showed; the next round must be the week it moves to, or the Super Bowl when it moves to Pro Bowl week 22; a folder without one gets the loaded week + 1; never moves back), scores → `games` COMPLETED (a postseason week's games aren't in the season schedule, so the load first creates them from schedule.csv with their kickoff time and `game_type`, `add_playoff_games`), team tabs → `team_seasons`, Season Stats resolved by name + position through the roster (shared names settled by earlier counting-stat totals, then by team-scoped lists `stats_scope_<team>_<cat>.csv` where one team's list settles a pair; else it stops and prints the `--scope` commands to run; a drop in a total settled by a scoped list is reported as a correction, other drops stop the load; CSV headers lose Madden's sort mark, and a mapped column missing from a CSV stops the load; afterwards it removes stale copies, i.e. season_stats rows it didn't write that repeat a written row's list name, position and offense numbers under another player), offense → `season_stats`, other categories → `season_stat_*`, GP/DP → `season_stat_general`; stops on unmatched passing/rushing/receiving rows (unmatched player handling crops 700x120 row snippets to `unmatched_crops/`, prompts interactively if terminal tty or dumps to `unmatched_players.json` and raises `LoadError` instructing user to run `python3 -m pipeline.resolve_player <folder>`), a row read two ways, a counting stat going down, a monotonic lower-bound row count decrease against last week (`now[f] < n - 2`, flagging an incomplete capture) or upper-bound expansion exceeding 30% / floor 20 (`now[f] > n + max(20, int(0.30 * n))`). Games (recovery): box scores → `team_stats` (the box score has yards but no attempts, completions or TDs, so `team_line_totals` sums `passing_completions`, `passing_attempts`, `passing_tds`, `rushing_attempts` and `rushing_tds` from the team's `player_stats` lines loaded for that game), lines → `player_stats` / `game_stat_*` (team + list name; teammates sharing a name settled by category), scores → `games` with `excitement_index` reset so the engines re-score the game from its real box score; a matchup stored twice in the season (a playoff rematch with the same home team) is settled by the stored final score; `highlights.json` upserted into `game_highlights` table; refused while the backfill's rows exist unless `--replace`. Playoff (a folder with both `schedule.csv` and `game_scores.csv`, from Week 19): the weekly schedule load without Season Stats or Team Stats (regular season only), the coming round's games from `schedule_next.csv` (SCHEDULED; the round must be the week the league moves to), then the round's box scores and lines, in one transaction; a weekly load also reads `schedule_next.csv` when there is one (the Week 18 run: the Wild Card games) |

---

## 2. Core Mathematical Indices & Formulations

### 2.1 Game Excitement Index ($E$)
Evaluates single-game entertainment value ($0 \text{ to } 100$):
$$E = 0.35(C) + 0.25(P) + 0.20(S) + 0.20(U)$$
- **$C$ (Competitiveness)**: Final score margin and overtime drama: $\min(100, \max(0, 100 - 4 \cdot |\text{Score}_A - \text{Score}_B|) + \text{OT Bonus})$.
- **$P$ (Production & Pace)**: Combined points and offensive yardage volume.
- **$S$ (Star Impact & Outliers)**: Top-3 player OVR averages and standout single-game milestone box scores (e.g. 400+ pass yards, 4+ TDs).
  - **Defensive & special-teams outliers** (`defensive_outlier_points`, JR Oct 14): from a game's captured lines in `game_stat_lines` (`fetch_game_defense_rows`; captured games only, Week 17 onward in 2035), per player: 3+ sacks +12, 2+ interceptions +12, +10 per defensive TD, +10 per kick or punt return TD, a safety +6, 2+ forced fumbles +6, a made field goal of 55+ yards +6. They add to the offensive milestones (400+ pass yards +15, 4+ pass TDs +15, 150+ scrimmage yards +12, 2+ rush/rec TDs +8) before the 100 cap, so a 3-sack game counts like a 150-yard day. Games without captured lines keep offense-only outliers. Week 17 of 2035 was given only the defensive increment (six games, +0.6 to +1.6 raw; Steelers–Vikings, already the week's top game, 83.65 → 85.25), not a full re-score: re-scoring those games with today's inputs would also have moved them up to ±5.6 from rating and box-score drift since they were scored.
  - Each stat line's player is its stored `player_stats.player_id` (abbreviated names like `A. Wright` collide across players), and OVR is that player's rating in the game's season; lines with no rating that season are skipped (`fetch_game_player_rows` in `engines/gotw_engine.py`). TWI's $Y_{\text{prod}}$ ages, Playoff Clout's "played in", the record book's single games and the fantasy projections' lines join the same way.
- **$U$ (Upset & Narrative Leverage)**: Pre-game talent differential (`team_seasons.team_ovr` for the game's season) and heavyweight clashes.
- Computed by `engines/gotw_engine.py` and stored in `games.excitement_index`. A game's $E$ depends only on that game's data, so once scored it is **not** re-scored automatically (re-scoring would let it drift as player/team ratings change during the season, which would in turn shift TWI and popularity).
  - **Pipeline (Section 6, step 3):** `batch_evaluate(season, only_missing=True, game_ids=<games just backfilled>)` scores only completed games with no $E$ yet plus the games whose box scores were inserted in that run.
  - **Manual re-scoring** (e.g. after a box-score correction): `python3 -m engines.gotw_engine --game-ids 805 832`; `--season 2035 --missing-only` fills gaps; no flags re-scores every completed game in all seasons (`--season` limits it to one).
- **Projected Excitement (scheduled games, `engines/excitement_projection.py`)**: the expected value of each component before kickoff, on the same raw scale as $E$, stored in `games.projected_excitement` (migration `db/migrations/2026_10_13_projected_excitement.sql`) and refreshed for every scheduled game by the pipeline (§6):
  - $C$: $\mathbb{E}[\max(0, 100 - 4|m|)]$ with margin $m \sim N(\mu, \sigma)$, $\mu = k \cdot (\text{OVR}_\text{home} - \text{OVR}_\text{away})$. The slope $k$ (≈2.3 points per OVR) and the residual spread $\sigma$ are fitted from the season's completed games.
  - $P$: the league-average game. Team scoring and yardage barely persist week to week in this league, so team-specific $P$ only added noise.
  - $S$: the teams' average top-3 OVR and milestone-outlier points per game (offensive plus captured defensive and special-teams outliers), shrunk toward the league average (4 pseudo-games).
  - $U$: the OVR favourite's win probability, blended over the chalk and upset outcomes.
  - **Accuracy (2035 backtest, weeks 3+, earlier weeks only):** correlation ≈ 0.23 with actual $E$, and mean error 9.25 vs. 9.38 for always guessing the league average. Pre-game excitement is only weakly predictable, so projections are always labelled as projected. Check with `python3 -m engines.excitement_projection --season 2035 --backtest`.
  - This replaces the former display-only formula $\min(98, \max(85, \lfloor \text{Matchup TWI} \times 1.45 \rfloor))$. That put 207 of 224 backtested games at exactly 85, and correlated −0.25 with actual $E$.
- **Display scale (70–100)**: stored and projected $E$ stay raw (TWI and popularity use raw $E$). Everything shown to readers and writers (API, assignment brief, writer DB tools) uses `display_excitement()` in `engines/gotw_engine.py`: $\text{Display} = \min(100, \max(70,\ 70 + 30 \cdot (E - 40) / 60))$. A dull game (raw 40, about the 5th percentile) shows as 70, a typical game (≈58) about 79, the 2035 best (83.7) about 92, and a perfect 100 shows as 100.

### 2.2 Team Watchability Index ($TWI$)
Rolling, opponent-adjusted metric defining how entertaining a franchise is to watch over time:
$$TWI = \min\left(100, 0.40(\mu_{\text{adj}}) + 0.20(\sigma_{\text{top}}) + 0.25(P_{\text{draft}}) + 0.15(Y_{\text{prod}}) - \text{Penalty}_{\text{purgatory}}\right)$$
- Attributed through production share $\alpha$ and opponent baseline correction $\delta_{\text{opp}}$.
- Exponential recency decay with $\lambda = 0.15$.
- Ceiling & chaos volatility bonus ($\sigma_{\text{top}}$) from high-entertainment games.
- Stored permanently in `team_seasons.watchability_index`, along with its components `watchability_sigma_top` ($\sigma_{\text{top}}$), `p_draft`, `y_prod` and `purgatory_penalty` so writer tools read the same values TWI used.

### 2.3 Fantasy Defensive Matchup Stars & Strength of Schedule (SOS)
- **Even 5-Tier Quantile Distribution (`NTILE(5)`)**: To eliminate artificial clustering, defensive strength against each offensive position (QB, RB/HB, WR, TE) is ranked across all 32 NFL teams:
  $$\text{Matchup Stars} = \text{NTILE}(5) \text{ OVER } (\text{PARTITION BY } \text{season}, \text{position} \text{ ORDER BY } \text{total\_allowed} \text{ ASC})$$
  - **1 Star (Top 6–7 Defenses)**: Toughest matchups (allow fewest fantasy points).
  - **2 Stars (6–7 Defenses)**: Above-average defenses.
  - **3 Stars (6 Defenses)**: Average defenses.
  - **4 Stars (6 Defenses)**: Below-average defenses.
  - **5 Stars (Bottom 6–7 Defenses)**: Easiest matchups (allow most fantasy points).
- **Season SOS & Playoff SOS**:
  - **Season SOS**: 18-week schedule difficulty bucketed into 1 to 5 stars via `NTILE(5)`.
  - **Playoff SOS**: Fantasy playoff weeks (Weeks 15–17) opponent schedule difficulty bucketed into 1 to 5 stars via `NTILE(5)`.

### 2.4 Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit (`engines/game_credit_engine.py`)
Schedule-adjusted team strength in **points per game relative to a league-average team** (0 = average), fitted as a Simple Rating System over the season's completed games:
- **Capped margin** $m$: each game's margin clamped to ±24 (`MARGIN_CAP`).
- **Home field** $H$: the league's mean capped home margin this season, clamped to $[0, 4]$. `SUPER_BOWL` games are neutral (site sign $s = 0$; home $s = +1$, away $s = -1$).
- **Game credit** for a team in one game: $c = m - sH + R_{\text{opp}}$, the margin an average team would need at that site against that opponent to match the result.
- **Rating** (`opp_adj_credit`): $R_i = \dfrac{\sum_{g} c_g}{n_i + 2}$ (shrunk toward 0 by 2 pseudo-games), solved iteratively and re-centred so ratings average 0.
- **Recent form** (`opp_adj_credit_recent`): recency-weighted mean of $c$ with TWI's decay $w_t = e^{-0.15(T - t)}$.
- **Strength of schedule** (`sos_rating`): mean $R_{\text{opp}}$ faced; positive means a harder schedule.
- **Trajectory Multiplier** (`trajectory_multiplier`, $T$): $\Delta = \bar c_{\text{last 4}} - R$; $T = \text{clamp}(1 + 0.5\,\Delta / 20,\ 0.80,\ 1.20)$, the 0.5 shrink damping the noisy 4-game mean. $T = 1.00$ before 6 games. Labels: ≥ 1.08 Surging, ≥ 1.03 Rising, > 0.97 Steady, ≥ 0.92 Fading, < 0.92 Sliding.
- **Expected wins** (`expected_wins`): $\sum_g \Phi\!\left((R_i - R_{\text{opp}} + sH)/\sigma\right)$, where $\sigma$ is the RMS residual margin (default 14 with fewer than 64 games). `wins − expected_wins` ("win luck") flags paper tigers (positive) and unlucky teams (negative); ties count as half a win.
- Written to `team_seasons` by `GameCreditEngine().calculate(season)`. Regular-season games only (`fetch_completed_games`): the credit, expected wins and luck sit next to the regular-season record, so a playoff game has no game credit. Writer agents read it live (recomputed from `games`, so it is never stale) through `get_opponent_adjusted_credit`.
- This is distinct from TWI's opponent baseline correction $\delta_{\text{opp}}$, which adjusts *excitement* for how watchable an opponent is, not team strength.

### 2.5 Popularity Indices (`engines/popularity_engine.py`, `engines/player_popularity_engine.py`)
- **Franchise POP** $= 0.35B + 0.30H + 0.35\,TWI$, with $H = 0.85\,S_{\text{recent}} + 0.15\,S_{\text{all-time}}$ and $S_{\text{recent}} = \min(100,\ 100\cdot\text{Win\%} + \text{hardware bump})$. Written to `team_seasons.popularity_index`.
  - The hardware bump is +25 per Super Bowl win and +5 per runner-up over the 5 seasons before the current one, derived from `games.game_type = 'SUPER_BOWL'` (`market_constants.recent_hardware_bumps`).
  - $B$ (Base Market Gravity) and $S_{\text{all-time}}$ (Legacy Floor) are constants in `engines/market_constants.py`, transcribed from `docs/models/base_market_gravity_specification.md` and `docs/models/legacy_floor.md`. The $L$/$D$/$N$ breakdown of $B$ is only published for eight franchises.
  - `docs/models/legacy_floor.md` lists the Giants as 2031 and 2033 runner-ups; the recorded results (and the Season Recap video) have the Lions and Falcons. The Legacy Floor values were not recomputed.
- **Player Pop** $= (0.15F + 0.20E + 0.20P + 0.15R + 0.15A + 0.15T)\cdot M_{\text{pos}}$ (`player_seasons.popularity_index`). Stored components: SpikeIndex, $R_{\text{rating}}$, $F_{\text{platform}}$, $T_{\text{success}}$, $A_{\text{awards}}$, $E_{\text{exposure}}$ (`spike_index`, `r_rating`, `f_platform`, `t_success`, `a_awards`, `e_exposure`).
  - **Awards Equity** $A = \min\left(100,\ \sum_{k=1}^{3} e^{-0.35(k-1)} A_{\text{pts}}(Y-k) + 0.25\,A_{\text{career}}\right)$, where $A_{\text{pts}}$ is MVP 50, OPOY/DPOY 35, First Team All-Pro 30, SBMVP 25, OROY/DROY 18, Second Team All-Pro 15 (COTY 0; All-Pro tracked from `OFFICIAL_AP_ALL_PRO_ROSTER`) and $A_{\text{career}}$ is all award points before season $Y$. Matched on `awards.player_id`. Before 2026-10-12 it compared full award names with abbreviated `players.player_name`, so $A$ was 0 for everyone.
  - **Performance** $P = (0.60\,\text{prod} + 0.40\,\text{SpikeIndex})\cdot d$, $d = 0.85$ on a team at or under .350, else 1; SpikeIndex $= \min(100,\ 25\cdot\text{spike games})$ (`player_seasons.spike_index`). Offense: prod $= \min(100,\ 3.5\cdot$ average PPR per game$)$ and a spike game is 24+ PPR, from `player_stats` matched by `player_id` (matching `vw_fantasy_scores` on the abbreviated name gave 33 players, defenders among them, a namesake's fantasy games until Oct 14, e.g. CB "T. Davis" had SpikeIndex 100). Defenders listed in Madden's Season Stats (`season_stat_lines` with a defensive line; JR, Oct 14): prod $= \min(100,\ 12.5\cdot$ defensive points per game$)$ with 0.25 per tackle, 1 per TFL, 4 per sack, 6 per INT, 1.5 per pass deflection, 3 per forced fumble, 2 per recovery, 6 per defensive TD, 2 per safety (the 12.5 scale puts the 10th-best defender at the 10th-best skill player's 74; 2035: 25th 66 vs 62, 50th 56 vs 56); a spike game has 2+ splash plays (sacks, INTs, forced fumbles, recoveries) or a defensive TD, counted from `game_stat_lines` where they exist (Week 17 on in 2035) and estimated for the season's other games as each remaining defensive TD plus, per game, the Poisson chance of 2+ splash plays at the remaining per-game rate, so a defender's SpikeIndex can be fractional. A player with both keeps the higher $P$. No line at all (unlisted defenders, linemen, specialists): prod $= 3.5\cdot 15\cdot \text{OVR}/100$, no spikes. `engines/player_popularity_engine.defensive_performance`; `tests/test_player_and_playoff_metrics.py`.
  - **Position multiplier** $M_{\text{pos}}$: QB 1.15; WR, HB 1.05; edge rushers (LEDG, REDG), outside linebackers (SAM, WILL), CB 0.90; TE, MIKE, FS, SS 0.75; OL, DT, K, P, LS 0.50; anything else (FB) 0.75. The roster's LEDG/REDG/SAM/WILL/MIKE codes and LS were missing until Oct 14 (only the old LE/RE/LOLB/ROLB/MLB were listed), so those players got the 0.75 default; long snappers averaged 25.4 popularity, above kickers (18.2) and punters (17.6), and now average 17.0.
  - **Exposure** $E = \sum_k w_k E_k (1 + 0.25\,\mathbb{I}_{\text{GOTW},k}) / \sum_k w_k$ over games the player has a stat line in, $w_k = e^{-0.12(T-k)}$, GOTW = the week's highest raw $E$. The appearance flag stands in for the spec's SnapShare. Players with no stat lines (all defenders) get 50. Previously a flat 60.
  - **Team Success** $T = 0.60\cdot 100\,\text{Win\%} + 0.40\,S_{\text{post}}$, where $S_{\text{post}}$ is 30 for last season's Super Bowl winner and 15 for the runner-up, from `games`.
  - Data note: the 2033 SB MVP is recorded as "Cade Klubnik" but `player_id` 1477 is `players.full_name` "Cade Loop".


### 2.6 Team Efficiency Profile (`engines/team_efficiency_engine.py`)
- **Regular season only** (`fetch_team_game_rows`), matching the record and standings; playoff games stay out.
- **Pythagorean wins** (`pythagorean_wins`): $G \cdot PF^{2.37} / (PF^{2.37} + PA^{2.37})$. "Pyth luck" $=$ wins $-$ Pythagorean wins. It ignores schedule; compare with `expected_wins` (2.4).
- **Team pace & production** (`pace_production`): $\min\left(100,\ \frac{PF/G}{35}\cdot 60 + \frac{\text{Yds}/G}{425}\cdot 40\right)$, the game-level $P$ benchmarks halved to one team. True tempo needs `team_stats.total_plays`, which is not captured (all 0).
- Also returned live by `get_team_efficiency_profile`: pass and rush yards per game and giveaways per game (the team's own offense).
- **Box-score caveat:** for CPU-simmed games, `game_stat_generator.py` splits season totals across games in proportion to each game's combined score (see Section 6, step 2). A team's own season totals are real, but per-game box scores, and anything attributed to opponents game by game (takeaways, yards allowed, TDs vs. points in one game, game-to-game usage changes), are artifacts of that split. Metrics are built only on season totals, scores and ratings for that reason; turnover margin and a TD-finishing-rate stand-in for red-zone efficiency were considered and rejected.
- **Per-game team conversions, red zone and penalties** (`engines/team_box_estimator.py`, `python3 -m engines.team_box_estimator [--season N] [--dry-run]`; JR, Oct 14): every NULL `team_stats` cell of third/fourth-down and two-point conversions and attempts, red-zone trips/TDs/FGs/percentage, penalties and penalty yards gets the team's season remainder (`team_seasons` total from the Team Stats capture minus the cells already stored), split across the games that need it. A captured box score supplies conversions, red-zone TDs/FGs/percentage and penalty yards (red-zone trips follow from the percentage); attempts and penalty counts are always estimated. Stored cells (captured or estimated earlier) never change, so in a weekly run each new game gets exactly that week's change; only the first run (Weeks 1–16 of 2035, plus Week 17's attempts and counts) spread a backlog. The split: each game's score is decomposed into TDs and FGs (about one extra point per TD, closest to the team's season red-zone TD share) and caps red-zone TDs and FGs; red-zone trips are balanced against the offense's remainder and the opponent's red-zone defense remainder by iterative proportional fitting, then rounded to the offense exactly (defense lands within a few trips; 98 off in total across all teams and the three stats on the 2035 backlog); third-down conversions follow points scored and failures the opposite, both scaled by the game's combined score; fourth-down attempts rise with the deficit; two-point tries follow TDs and the deficit and never exceed the game's TDs; penalties follow captured penalty yards or the combined score, and yards follow the count. Filled columns are listed in `team_stats.estimated_fields`; a cell that was its team's only missing cell for the column (normal weekly runs from Week 18 on) is the real value and is also listed in `exact_fields` (`red_zone_pct` when its TDs and trips are captured or exact). A clamped remainder (stored cells already over the season total) is never marked exact. A team's conversions allowed in a game are the opponent's row for that game: real where that row is captured or exact, an estimate in the backlog (Madden's Conversions screen has no defense side, so nothing anchors third-down defense the way red-zone defense is anchored). Regular season only, and only weeks the latest loaded weekly capture covers. These per-game values are estimates under the same caveat: nothing reads them yet, and season-level metrics should keep using `team_seasons`. `tests/test_team_box_estimator.py` covers the split.


### 2.7 Playoff Clout (`engines/playoff_clout_engine.py`)
- **Team** (`team_seasons.s_playoff`): $\min\left(100,\ \sum_{y \le Y} e^{-0.15(Y-y)}\left[100\cdot\text{SB win} + 50\cdot\text{SB loss} + 20\cdot\text{playoff wins}\right]\right)$. Super Bowls are `games.game_type = 'SUPER_BOWL'`; playoff wins are completed wins in any other non-`REGULAR_SEASON` game.
- **Player** (`player_seasons.s_playoff`): $\min(100,\ 40\cdot\text{SB MVPs} + 25\cdot\text{SB wins played in} + 8\cdot\text{other playoff wins played in})$. "Played in" = a `player_stats` row for the winning team in that game, which only exists from 2035. Before then only SB MVPs (by `awards.player_id`) count, so veterans' clout is understated.


### 2.8 Matchup Edge & Trench Edge (`engines/matchup_edges.py`)
Computed live by `get_matchup_edges`; nothing is stored. For team A's offense against team B's defense, with $z$ = z-score across the 32 teams:
- **Pass edge** $= z(A\ \text{pass yds/g}) + z(B\ \text{coverage\_rank})$. Madden ranks run 1 = best, so a high rank is a weak secondary.
- **Rush edge** $= z(A\ \text{rush yds/g}) - z(B\ \text{def\_ovr})$.
- **Ball-security edge** $= z(B\ \text{giveaways/g}) - z(A\ \text{giveaways/g})$.
- **Trench edge** = mean OVR of A's top 5 offensive linemen (LT/LG/C/RG/RT) − mean OVR of B's top 4 pass rushers (LEDG/REDG/LE/RE/DT), in OVR points, shown with B's `pass_rush_rank`.
- The largest positive edge is A's best edge and the most negative is its biggest vulnerability. Defense uses Madden ratings, not yards allowed or takeaways (see the box-score caveat in 2.6).
- **Season touch share** (in `get_player_season_stats`, RB/FB/WR/TE only): the player's share of the team's season carries + receptions and scrimmage yards, from `season_stats`. Receptions stand in for targets, which aren't recorded.

### 2.9 Record Book (`engines/records_engine.py`, `db/migrations/2026_10_26_nfl_records.sql`, `2026_10_28_league_record_book.sql`, `2026_11_12_defense_kicking_records.sql`, `2026_11_23_postseason_records.sql`)
JR, Oct 14, 2026: a record book the league can be measured against. Not on a screen yet.
- **What's in it:** 17 regular-season categories with real NFL history (`record_categories`, `season_type = 'REGULAR'`), plus the record book's BOOK categories, and 39 national postseason categories (`season_type = 'POSTSEASON'`, `2026_11_23_postseason_records.sql`). Single season: passing yards and TDs, rushing yards and TDs, receptions, receiving yards and TDs, rushing + receiving TDs (no return TDs), team wins, team points scored and fewest points allowed (seasons of 16+ games). Single game: passing yards and TDs, rushing yards, receiving yards, receptions, rushing + receiving TDs. Defense and kicking, single season (`2026_11_12_defense_kicking_records.sql`, `coverage = 'BOOK'`, sort order 37–41, plus the record book's existing `season_sacks`): sacks, interceptions (`season_def_interceptions`), tackles (`season_tackles`), defensive TDs (`season_def_tds`), field goals made (`season_fgm`) and longest field goal (`season_fg_long`). Real NFL history (`2026_11_15_defense_kicking_history.sql`, JR Oct 15): every franchise's record and the NFL top 10 for sacks (official, 1982 on: Myles Garrett's 23 in 2025 is the record, which the record book's Arvell Reese tied in 2029; Al Baker's unofficial 23 in 1978 is left out), interceptions and field goals made, and the top 10 plus the 22 franchises with a 61+ yard kick for the longest field goal (Cam Little's 68 in 2025); those four are `coverage = 'FULL'`. Sources: Pro-Football-Reference's single-season leader lists (deep enough that every franchise's best is on them; the Texans', Jaguars' and Panthers' interception records checked on StatMuse) and Wikipedia's list of the longest NFL field goals. Tackles and defensive TDs stay BOOK: tackles aren't an official NFL stat and there is no season list of defensive TDs, so they are measured only against the record book and later league seasons and produce no events until then. On the 2035 data, Cooper DeJean's 8 interceptions break the Texans' record (Marcus Coleman's 7, 2003).
- **Postseason Record Book (`2026_11_23_postseason_records.sql`):** National / all-time NFL postseason leaderboards only (no franchise postseason records). Distinguished by `season_type = 'POSTSEASON'` on `record_categories` (`REGULAR` default); `scope` allows `GAME`, `SEASON` (single-postseason tournament runs), and `CAREER`; `subject` allows `PLAYER`, `TEAM`, and `COACH`. Seeded with official NFL history through the 2025–26 postseason from Pro-Football-Reference (top 10 entries per category, 390 rows):
  - **Single-game postseason (GAME):** passing yards (Brady 505), passing TDs (Brady/Young/Lamonica 6), rushing yards (Dickerson 248), rushing TDs (Watters 5), receiving yards (Moulds 240), receptions (Sproles/White 15), receiving TDs (Gabe Davis 4), sacks (McGinest 4.5), defensive interceptions (Vernon Perry 4).
  - **Single-postseason tournament runs (SEASON):** passing yards (Eli Manning 1,219), passing TDs (Montana/Warner/Flacco/Mahomes 11), rushing yards (John Riggins 610), rushing TDs (Terrell Davis 8), receiving yards (Larry Fitzgerald 546), receptions (Cooper Kupp 33), receiving TDs (Fitzgerald 7), sacks (Richard Dent / LaMarr Woodley 6.0).
  - **Super Bowl single-game (GAME):** passing yards (Brady 505), passing TDs (Steve Young 6), rushing yards (Timmy Smith 204), rushing TDs (Terrell Davis / Jalen Hurts 3), receiving yards (Jerry Rice 215), receptions (James White 14), receiving TDs (Jerry Rice 3), sacks (L.C. Greenwood 4.0).
  - **Super Bowl wins (CAREER):** player (Tom Brady 7, Charles Haley 5), head coach (Bill Belichick 6, Chuck Noll 4), franchise (Patriots/Steelers 6, 49ers/Cowboys 5).
  - **Career playoff records (CAREER):** passing yards (Brady 13,049), passing TDs (Brady 88), rushing yards (Emmitt Smith 1,586), rushing TDs (Emmitt Smith 19), receiving yards (Jerry Rice 2,245), receptions (Travis Kelce 165), receiving TDs (Jerry Rice 22), sacks (Willie McGinest 16.0), interceptions (Ronnie Lott / Bill Simpson / Ed Reed / Charlie Waters 9), QB playoff wins (Brady 35, Montana 16, Mahomes 15), head coach playoff wins (Belichick 31, Reid 26).
- **Seed (real NFL history through 2025, from Pro-Football-Reference, pulled Oct 14, 2026):** every franchise's single-season record holders in all 11 season categories (400 rows, ties included; franchise histories follow Pro-Football-Reference, so the Rams include St. Louis and Cleveland, the Titans the Houston Oilers, the Raiders Oakland and Los Angeles, the Cardinals Chicago and St. Louis, and the Ravens start in 1996), and the NFL's all-time top 10 in the 14 player categories (160 rows). Single-game records exist only as NFL top 10s: Pro-Football-Reference has no franchise single-game lists. Known limits: Pro-Football-Reference's rushing + receiving TD single-game list has a data error at the top and leaves out Ernie Nevers (1929), so that list was set by hand from the NFL record book (6: Nevers, Dub Jones, Alvin Kamara); 38 games are tied at 6 passing TDs, of which the six most recent are kept; and for the oldest franchises the source pages were cut off before their earliest seasons, which were checked by hand only where they could hold a record (the 1963 Giants' 448 points, the 1961 Oilers' 513).
- **The league's own record book, 2026–2035 (JR, Oct 14; `2026_10_28_league_record_book.sql`, source `BOOK`):** the records set in this league between 2026 and 2035, from the game's record book: franchise records for single seasons, single games, rookie seasons and rookie games (119 rows, including Crawford's 2034 rookie season, which the NFL-wide rookie list implies was also a Panthers record), and the same seasons and games on the NFL lists (75 rows; the views rank them, so Ty Simpson's 592 passing yards (2034) and Jordyn Tyson's 399 receiving yards (2032) are the single-game records, and Trey'Mane Davis's 2,406 receiving yards (2034) the season record). Short names were matched to full names where the roster had exactly one player with that initial and surname, or the player is a well-known current NFL player; A. Lowery and B. Gore were later matched to Antoine Lowery and Ben Gore on the 2035 roster (`2026_11_16_record_book_names.sql`); B. Ojulari, M. Fields and M. Wilson, who aren't on it, were named by JR as Bayo Ojulari (retired before 2035), Malachi Fields and Michael Wilson (`2026_11_17_record_book_names_2.sql`). Isaiah Carson's rookie receiving-yards game was listed as 2036 and is stored as 2035. New categories: sacks in a season, rushing TDs, receiving TDs and interceptions in a game, and rookie seasons and games; they are `coverage = 'BOOK'` (only what the record book lists, no real NFL history), so `league_records` gives them a league-wide record only where the record book named one (the rookie passing, receiving-TD and receptions records). The record book only lists records broken, not ties, and has no team or combined-TD records. Known gap: seasons from 2026–2034 that made an all-time top 10 without breaking a franchise record aren't in it.
- **Updating:** `RecordsEngine().calculate(season)` runs automatically in the post-week analytics pipeline (`engines/game_stat_generator.py`); `python3 -m engines.records_engine --season 2035` reports from the CLI, and `--apply` stores them. Season values come from `season_stats` (players), `season_stat_lines` (defense and kicking: `season_defense_values`, only players Madden lists in the category; an unlisted player's value is unknown, so he gets no entry rather than a zero) and completed regular-season games (teams); a category not yet in `record_categories` (migration not applied) is skipped; single games from `player_stats` (regular season, the line's `player_id`). A season is measured against every earlier season (NFL, record book and league rows) and against its own other entries: only a team's best of the season can hold its franchise record, and top-10 ranks count the season's other entries. Fewest points allowed waits for a full 17-game season. `--apply` deletes and rewrites that season's `LEAGUE` rows, so it can run every week. The record book is the authority for its seasons: where it already has the season's row for a team and category, `--apply` adds nothing, and the report ends with where the record book and the stats disagree (a record-book entry the engine could have found but didn't, or a broken record in a category the record book tracks that it doesn't list). Old holders are never deleted: the views pick the best value, so the history of who held each record stays.
- **In-week record breaking detection (`find_records_broken_in_week(cur, season, week)`):** detects any all-time NFL single-game or single-season records broken or tied during a specific week (`week - 1` recap week). Checks single-game player records set in that week against all-time leaderboards; cumulative single-season player totals crossing an all-time threshold in that week (`prev_total <= old_best < curr_total` or tying `prev_total < old_best == curr_total`); team cumulative points and wins crossing all-time records in that week; and final-week (Week 18) defense/kicking records from `season_stat_lines`. Feeds dynamic editorial slate expansion in the media swarm (§7.3).
- **League-wide records** are computed (JR): the best franchise record across the 32 teams (`league_records`; single-game categories, which have no franchise records, take the top of the NFL list).
- **2035 check (Oct 14, through Week 17, on a copy of the live data, with the record book loaded):** the record book's 2035 entries and the stats agree. NFL records: the Panthers' 654 points (2013 Broncos, 606) and 16-0 (ties the 2007 Patriots with a game left), Crawford's 67 passing TDs (Manning, 55) and 7 in Week 6 (ties the single-game record), Davis's 27 receiving TDs (Moss, 23). Top 10: Davis's 1,949 receiving yards (3rd) and 27 combined TDs (3rd), Hansen's 19 receiving TDs (5th), Carson's 18 (8th), Carson's 16 catches in a game (9th). Franchise: Panthers 6 (passing TDs, Franklin's rushing yards, Davis's receiving and combined TDs, wins, points), Steelers 4, Texans 2 ties, Bears 1 (points). Without the record book the check had wrongly credited Crawford's 5,121 yards, Davis's 1,949 yards, and Bears, Browns and Lions records that 2026–2034 seasons had already passed.
