- **Tests:** `tests/test_records_engine.py` (franchise and NFL records, ties, the season's best only, top-10 ranks with same-season entries, the full-season rule, single games, the report, the record book confirming the stats, disagreements both ways, `--apply` leaving record-book rows alone, defense and kicking seasons against the record book, unlisted defenders getting no value, the seed covering every team and category, `find_records_broken_in_week` detecting Crawford W6/W14/W18, Davis W15/W16, and Panthers W17/W18 records).

### 2.10 Mock Draft Simulation Engine (`engines/mock_draft_engine.py`, `engines/draft_order.py`, `pipeline/draft_board.py`, `pipeline/ingest_prospects.py`, `db/migrations/2026_11_19_draft_board.sql`)
Simulates an analytical Round 1 NFL Mock Draft by marrying three inputs:
1. **Draft Order** (`engines/draft_order.py`, through `get_draft_order`): reverse regular-season record (win% with ties counting half, then regular-season point differential, worst first), with playoff exits in their brackets: missed playoffs 1–18, Wild Card losers 19–24, Divisional 25–28, Conference Championship 29–30, Super Bowl loser 31, winner 32. A pick is `locked` once its team is out and that round is fully played; teams still alive hold the remaining picks provisionally. Before the Wild Card games exist the whole order is provisional. While only the Wild Card round exists, each conference's best record is taken as its bye (#1 seed), since byes appear in no game yet. A past season stored with only its Super Bowl is complete: loser 31, winner 32, the rest by record, all locked. Playoff games are read from `games.game_type` (`WILD_CARD`, `DIVISIONAL`, `CONFERENCE`, `SUPER_BOWL`; weeks 19/20/21/23 for untyped rows). Only games before the run's cutoff week count.
2. **Team Positional Needs:** Ranked roster deficiencies calculated via `get_team_draft_needs`, factoring in starter baseline rating deficits (sub-75 OVR bridge starters vs. sub-80), age cliffs (starters aged 30+ without under-25 developmental successors), and phase ranks.
3. **Prospect Board:** the draft class (league season + 1) in `draft_prospects` (§3.3c), read with `pipeline/draft_board.read_board`. Each prospect has `projected_pick` (overall, from the Draft Board's PICK column; undrafted prospects numbered after the last pick), `projected_round` / `round_pick`, `full_name`, `position`, `college`, `height`, `weight`, `age` and `overall_rating` (scouted OVR, 40–99), with optional `archetype` and `notes`. Any field but the pick and name can be blank. A prospect without an OVR is valued at `UNRATED_OVR` = 50 rather than dropped.
   - **Draft Prospect Scan:** Scanned once per season as part of the start-of-season cadence alongside the schedule and roster scans, specifically after both have run (via `python3 madden_capture.py --only season` or debug job `season_scan`, or standalone `--only prospects` / `draft_scan`), which captures prospects line-by-line, and extracts via `extract.py` into `prospects.csv`. `pipeline.load_capture` loads it leniently into `draft_prospects` for class season + 1, replacing an earlier scan of that class and its saved mock drafts. A blank or implausible cell is stored as NULL and listed in the report's `prospect_warnings`; next season's roster scan has the real values. A row with no name or pick is skipped, and only an empty board stops the load. It also writes an export copy at `data/draft/{season+1}_prospects.csv`, which nothing reads. Running the draft prospect scan after the roster scan ensures that a roster load first links each rookie (`years_of_experience` 0) to his prospect row (`draft_prospects.player_id`, matched by full name + position group; ambiguous names are left unlinked) before the incoming class replaces it. A roster or season folder that also holds `prospects.csv` loads the board as well, and a bad board no longer fails the roster.
   - **Line-by-Line Console Capture:** Captured via `python3 madden_capture.py --only prospects [--prospects 32 | --all-prospects]`, which navigates to the Draft Board on its own (Manage Roster > Scouting > Prospects > L3 > EDIT DRAFT CLASS), leaves it afterwards through Back > SAVE AND EXIT (starting on the board, it leaves and reopens it so Pick 1 is highlighted, e.g. on a redo after a stream restart), and steps down row-by-row saving `prospects/prospect_NNN.png` because full names, heights, and weights are revealed only on the active highlighted line (`--all-prospects` or `--prospects 0` steps through all ~300–450 prospects across all rounds until the end of the board is detected via `same_table`; when a frame looks unchanged it looks again after 0.6 s before pressing Down again, up to `PROSPECT_RETRIES` = 3 times, since a second Down after a late redraw skips a row; a board that stops moving anywhere but the Undrafted rows raises, so `run_mode` can check for a frozen stream (`DRAFT_BOARD` is in `LIST_SCREENS`), restart it and redo the board). Extracted by `extract.py` via local Apple Vision OCR (`pipeline/extract_prospects_ocr.py`). Card height and weight are read glyph by glyph, not by Vision, which flips digits from run to run (1↔7, 3↔8, 5'11" as 5'1"). `read_ht_wt` takes the value band after `HT/WT:` (`HTWT_BOX` on a 3024×1888 frame), splits it into characters by inked columns, and matches each digit against `pipeline/assets/prospect_htwt_digits.png` (L1 distance, best over ±2 px shifts). It settles 0/3/6/8/9 by their loops (8 two, 3 none, 0 one tall, 6 one low, 9 one high). Vision's text fixups stay as the fallback when the band isn't `d'dd'',ddd` or the values are implausible: `6'T"` → `6'1"`, `5'1"` → `5'11"`, weights over 500 less 500, letters read in place of digits (`3U2 LBS` → 302, `E'11"` → `5'11"`). Names: the wide-tracked first name is re-read from its band alone (`FIRST_NAME_BAND`; 2×, then 1×). That read replaces the full-frame one when it only restores letters (`JAM` → JAMAL, `SAIA` → ISAIAH), fixes one letter at the same length (`AMIF` → AMIR), or the frame read was garbled (overlapping pieces `RC`+`ONALD`). A band read backwards (`NOD` for DON) is refused. The last name stands unless its band read and the table's short name (`J.Samuel`) agree on other letters (`SAMUEI` → SAMUEL, `RUTSON` → HUTSON); the table also supplies its spacing (`THO MAS` → THOMAS, VAN BUREN kept). The table row is matched by initial + last name, then last name, then one letter off (`N.Croshy`), then badge POS/OVR. Badges read with or without the bar (`CB #17 OVR 57`, `TE #1 1 OVR61`). Diacritics are stripped and HT/WT text read as Cyrillic is kept out of the name. Age comes from the matched table row. Output: `prospects.csv`, with targeted Gemini vision fallback.
   - **Board order comes from the screen, not the file names:** frames sort by frame number (resumed runs kept in the same folder are numbered from 1000, 2000, ...; as text `prospect_1000` would sort between `prospect_100` and `prospect_101`). Each frame's highlighted table row is found by first initial + last name (`D.Vogel`; a bare last-name match can hit `T.Whitehead` for WHITE), then by last name, then by the one row whose POS and OVR equal the card badge; its PICK cell (`Round 2, Pick 4` or `Undrafted`) fills `PROJECTED_ROUND` (1–7 or `UDFA`) and `ROUND_PICK`. Repeated frames (a resumed run's first row) are dropped. `fix_pick_sequence` then mends PICK cells: the capture steps one row per frame, so a cell that breaks the consecutive run (Vision 3↔8, 5↔6) or is unreadable is set to the one pick that fits both neighbours (the next in the round or the next round's first). A break no single pick mends is printed as a possible skipped row and left alone. Drafted prospects sort by (round, pick), and `PROJECTED_PICK` is the overall pick (earlier rounds' on-screen sizes + pick in round; Madden shows 7 × 32 = 224). Undrafted prospects follow in board order, numbered on from 225. `prospects.csv` columns: `PROJECTED_PICK, PROJECTED_ROUND, ROUND_PICK, FULL_NAME, POSITION, COLLEGE, HEIGHT, WEIGHT, AGE, OVR, ARCHETYPE, NOTES`.
- **Matching Algorithm:** Evaluates available prospects per pick:
  $$\text{FitScore} = 0.45 \cdot (\text{NeedScore} \cdot 1.5) + 0.40 \cdot (\text{BPA}) + 10.0 \cdot (M_{\text{pos}} - 1.0)$$
  where BPA is centered on scouted OVR with a draft-slot value delta $((i - \text{projected\_pick}) \cdot 0.5)$, and positional premium $M_{\text{pos}}$ prioritizes Round 1 cornerstone positions (QB 1.25, OT/EDGE 1.12, CB 1.10). A team already set at QB receives a severe penalty to prevent unwarranted early QB hoarding.
- **Output:** Assigns unique prospects to the slots (all 32, or the first `limit`), each with `team_id`, `locked`, `selection_status`, `fit_score` and a rationale. A run from the database is saved to `mock_draft_picks` for its (season, week). `get_mock_draft` returns that saved run, so every article in a week cites the same picks, and runs and saves the engine only when the week has none. Run via `python3 -m engines.mock_draft_engine [--season N] [--limit 24] [--save] [--file …] [--json]`. Tests: `tests/test_mock_draft_engine.py`, `tests/test_draft_order.py`, `tests/test_draft_board.py`, `tests/test_mock_draft_tools.py`.

### 2.11 Coach of the Year Race (`engines/coty_engine.py`, `db/migrations/2026_11_06_coty_scores.sql`, `2026_11_24_coty_historical_records.sql`)
Scores every head coach of a season from completed **regular-season** games (playoff games are ignored; award voting covers the regular season). The coach is the one whose `coach_tenures` row covers the season (Carolina 2035 = Marcus Whitfield). Four base inputs, each turned into a z-score across that season's coaches who have it:
- **Win%** (ties count half), weight 0.30.
- **Wins above expectation** = wins − expected wins, weight 0.20. Expected wins come from Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit (§2.4), which is regular season only, so they match `team_seasons.expected_wins`.
- **Roster outperformance** = `team_ovr` rank − win% rank (1 = best, tied values share the mean rank), weight 0.20. Positive = winning more than the roster's rating says. `team_ovr` is the stored roster OVR from the latest capture; no preseason rating snapshot is kept. Skipped for a team with no `team_ovr`.
- **Improvement** = win% − the team's previous regular-season win% (from `games`), weight 0.30. Only a **full** previous season counts: the team must have at least 17 regular-season games that season (`FULL_SEASON_GAMES`, `full_season_win_pcts`), so a partly loaded backlog season (a few weeks) never stands in for last year's record. This is the normal case: the weekly pipeline records every regular season from 2035 on, so improvement switches on by itself in the 2036 race, once the 2035 regular season is complete (Week 18 played). The credit goes to the team's current coach, even after a coaching change, since a new coach turning a team around is the classic COTY case. Skipped for a team without a full previous season, which today is every team in 2035, because 2026–2034 regular seasons aren't in `games` (only their Super Bowls). Backlog seasons are never estimated for this.

**Historical Rarity & Team Records Bonuses (`evaluate_team_historical_rarity`):**
In real NFL history, historic coaching milestones command unanimous honors. The engine awards additive bonus points directly to the score:
- **Undefeated Regular Season:** $+15.0$ points for completing a full undefeated regular season (16-0 or 17-0, 0 losses; in-progress undefeated seasons with $\ge 8$ games receive $\min(15.0, \text{wins} \cdot 0.9)$). There is no precedent in football history for an undefeated coach losing Coach of the Year (Don Shula 1972, Bill Belichick 2007).
- **9+ Win Turnaround:** $+6.0$ points for a historic turnaround ($\ge 9$ win increase over the previous season, $\Delta\text{win\_pct} \ge 9/17$); $+3.0$ points for a major $\ge 7$ win turnaround.
- **All-Time NFL Team Records Broken:** $+5.0$ points per NFL team single-season record broken ($+2.5$ if tied) across wins (`season_team_wins`), scoring (`season_team_points`), or scoring defense (`season_team_fewest_points_allowed`).
- **Franchise Team Records Broken:** $+2.0$ points per franchise team record broken ($+1.0$ if tied) across wins, scoring, and scoring defense.

**COTY score** $= 50 + 10\cdot\dfrac{\sum_i w_i z_i}{\sum_i w_i} + 3\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\text{first season with team}} + \text{HistoricalBonus}$, summing over the base inputs the coach has, so a missing input is re-weighted away rather than counted as zero. 50 = league average, about 10 points per standard deviation. Stored in `coty_scores` alongside `historical_bonus` and `historical_notes`.
- **Stored:** `CotyEngine().calculate(season)` deletes and rewrites the season's `coty_scores` rows (§3.3a) after the team efficiency engine in the weekly run (§6, step 3). Writers read it live through `get_coach_of_the_year_race` (§7), recomputed from `games` under the article's week cutoff, so it is never stale.
- **2035 Regular Season:** Marcus Whitfield (CAR, 17-0, 93.6 score, $+29.0$ historical bonus: undefeated 17-0, broke all-time NFL wins and scoring records, broke franchise wins and scoring records) #1; Peter Gaffney (DEN, 14-3, 70.0 score, $+1.0$ tied franchise wins record, $+16.5$ roster outperformance) #2; DeMeco Ryans (HOU, 13-4, 66.9 score, $+4.0$ broke franchise wins and scoring records) #3; Sean McVay (LAR, 10-7, 59.7) #4; John Harbaugh (NYG, 12-5, 58.6) #5.
- `engines/player_popularity_engine.py` still gives COTY 0 award points (§2.5).
- **Journalistic Data Citation Rule:** While `coty_scores` computes numerical index scores and bonuses internally to rank the coaching field, writers and editorial prompts must never explicitly quote "COTY score", "coach scores", "Coach of the Year metric", "historical bonus", or internal point tallies (e.g. +29.0). Instead, published copy must describe the real-world football achievements: the 17-0 undefeated regular season, all-time and franchise records broken, margin of victory, win turnaround, and roster overperformance.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_coty_engine.py` (tied ranks, z-scores skipping missing values, regular season only, ranking and centring, roster outperformance, improvement when recorded, only a full 17-game previous season counting, re-weighting when it isn't, a team without `team_ovr`, the first-year bonus, coachless teams, undefeated season historical bonus, 9+ win turnaround and broken team records bonuses).

### 2.12 Observed Tendencies (`engines/tendency_engine.py`, `db/migrations/2026_11_13_observed_tendencies.sql`)
JR, Oct 15: the real-world 2026 coach tiers (§3.3a) were checked against the 2035 season totals. Pass lean had no relation to how often teams passed (rank correlation 0.01, also after allowing for point differential). Fourth-down aggressiveness pointed the right way but weakly (0.16, p ≈ 0.4). Blitz rate couldn't be tested: listed defenders carry only 640 of the league's 1,268 sacks, and their totals don't follow pass-rush rating. Blitz rate is therefore left out of everything writers see (JR, Oct 15). So pass lean and fourth down are measured from this league instead:
- **Pass lean** `pass_rate_oe` = pass share of plays (`season_stats` pass / (pass + rush) attempts, Season Stats capture) minus the share a league-wide least-squares line on point differential per game predicts. Teams that trail pass more, so a lean is passing beyond what the score explains.
- **Fourth down** `fourth_down_oe` = fourth-down attempts per game (`team_seasons.fourth_down_att`, Team Stats capture) minus the rate the same kind of line on point differential predicts.
- **Tiers** `observed_pass_lean` / `observed_fourth_down`: ranked across teams with at least 4 regular-season games, top 11/32 High, next 10/32 Medium, rest Low. Teams with fewer games get values but no tier. `tendency_games` is the number of games behind them.
- **Stored** on `team_seasons` by `TendencyEngine().calculate(season)` after the team efficiency engine in the weekly run (§6, step 3); `python3 -m engines.tendency_engine [--season N]`. These are season totals, so they aren't cut off by an article's week, like the other `team_seasons` values (§7).
- **Used by** the writer tools through `engines/coach_profile.py` (§3.3a): this season's tier, else last season's when the same coach ran the team, else the coach's real-world tier, each labelled with its source.

### 2.13 Player Headshot Generation Architecture (`headshots/`, `docs/HEADSHOT_PIPELINE.md`)
Fictional players in Madden lack official broadcast photography. The headshot subsystem creates photorealistic, broadcast-grade media-day player portraits across all 1,499 players in `players` and serves them as transparent WebP cutouts.
- **Deterministic Appearance Synthesis (`headshots/headshot_appearance_generator.py`):** Uses independent SHA-256 seed streams (`name + team + version`) to deterministically sample physical traits: position-weighted skin tones (`TONE_W` across 5 tone brackets), authentic hair textures, facial hair distributions (skews clean-shaven for QBs/kickers/punters), BMI-derived physical build from database `height` and `weight`, age-dependent hairline recession, and micro-aging fine lines. Attractiveness and facial asymmetry (jawline, nose, eyes, natural skin blemishes) are jittered along a Gaussian stream to avoid synthetic airbrushed artifacts.
- **Official Franchise Staging & Prompts (`headshots/make_headshot.py`):** Combines the synthesized appearance line with an authoritative 32-team home jersey color palette (`_color_cache`, primary/secondary hex values). Formulates a high-fashion sports media-day prompt: 85mm portrait lens at $f/5.6$, key/fill/rim lighting, natural fabric mesh folds, and a seamless pure white backdrop (`#FFFFFF`).
- **Batch API Pipeline (`headshots/make_headshot.py`, `headshots/gen.py`):**
  - Compiles all 1,499 active players into `data/headshots/batch_headshot_requests.jsonl` using `build_batch_request`, handling duplicate same-team name collisions by suffixing position or ID.
  - Submits the batch payload to Gemini Batch API targeting `gemini-nano-banana-2.1` with `responseModalities: ["IMAGE"]` and 3:4 portrait aspect ratio.
  - Downloads completed batch artifacts into `data/headshots/batch_results.jsonl`.
- **Background Removal & WebP Processing (`headshots/process_headshots.py`):**
  - Executes across 6 concurrent multiprocessing workers with an in-memory queue.
  - Uses `rembg` with the edge-preserving ONNX `silueta` model ($\approx 0.35\text{s}$ inference) to cleanly segment players from the white background.
  - Encodes the RGBA cutout into WebP (`quality=90`, `method=4`, $\approx 110\text{–}180\text{ KB}$).
  - Re-maps request keys to each player's primary key `players.id`, saving directly to `static/players/{player_id}.webp`.
- **Seasonal Rookie Headshot Pipeline (`headshots/rookie_headshots.py`, `pipeline/weekly_capture.py`):**
  - Automatically runs after `step_load` in start-of-season cadence runs (`--mode season`) and roster scans (`--mode roster`) via `step_rookie_headshots(season)`.
  - Queries all rookies entering the league on team rosters (`player_seasons.years_of_experience = 0` / linked `draft_prospects` rows) that do not yet have headshots in `static/players/{player_id}.webp`.
  - Generates media-day headshots concurrently across 5–6 worker threads using `gemini-nano-banana-2.1`, strips backgrounds with thread-local `rembg` silueta sessions, archives raw cutouts to `data/headshots/players_backup/{player_id}.webp`, and standardizes transparent cutouts to 700×508 WebPs at `static/players/{player_id}.webp`.
  - Fully idempotent: resumes seamlessly across stops and network drops. Standalone CLI: `python3 -m headshots.rookie_headshots [--season N] [--workers 5] [--drafted-only] [--dry-run]`.
- **Frontend Serving:** Served statically at `/static/players/{player_id}.webp` by FastAPI (`server/api.py`). The React frontend (`Mu_Network/src/App.tsx`) loads headshots with cache-busting `?v=${HEADSHOT_VERSION}` across player modals, roster views, and social cards, falling back to team-colored initialed avatars if missing. Full architectural spec in [`docs/HEADSHOT_PIPELINE.md`](docs/HEADSHOT_PIPELINE.md).

- **2035 (Week 17):** most pass-leaning New Orleans (+7.1 points of pass share), Chargers and Bengals; most run-leaning Buffalo (−8.6), Miami and Baltimore. Most aggressive on fourth down Baltimore (+0.59 attempts a game), Philadelphia and Miami; least Rams, Browns and Packers.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_tendency_engine.py` (residuals, the 11/10/11 split, a lean that is only the score being none, per-game fourth downs, no tier before 4 games, missing inputs).

### 2.13 Player Splits by Defense (`engines/player_splits.py`, `db/migrations/2026_11_14_player_stats_line_source.sql`)
JR, Oct 15: how a QB, running back, receiver or tight end produces against different kinds of defenses. The real-world blitz/man tiers didn't show up in this league (§2.12), so defenses are grouped by measured data instead:
- **Groups** (per season of the game, each Strong / Average / Weak = the best 11 of 32, the next 10, the rest): scoring defense (points allowed per regular-season game, from final scores, games the article may see), pass rush (`team_seasons.pass_rush_rank`) and coverage (`coverage_rank`), both Madden ratings.
- **Real lines only:** `player_stats.line_source` CAPTURED (box-score capture) or WEEKLY (the backfill filled the team's only missing game, so the line is that week's exact change in the captured season totals). DISTRIBUTED lines (a remainder spread over several games by score) say nothing about the opponent and are skipped; the tool reports how many.
- **Per group:** games, per-game averages blended toward the player's overall average with 3 pseudo-games, and the change from his average (`vs_his_average`, e.g. `-18%`). A group needs 3 real games. Stats: QB completions, attempts, yards, TDs, INTs, rush yards, fantasy points, completion % and yards per attempt; RB/FB carries, rush yards and TDs, receptions, receiving yards, fantasy points; WR/TE receptions, receiving yards and TDs, fantasy points.
- **Span:** this season and last by default (staffs and playbooks are stable, so a player's history against a kind of defense carries over).
- **2035:** only Week 17's captured box scores are real (277 lines); weeks 1–16 were spread from the first weekly capture, so no player has 3 real games against any group yet and the tool's `verdict` says so. Weekly runs add one real line per player per week.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_player_splits.py` (the 11/10/11 groups, position groups, strong vs. weak splits with blending, too few games and ungrouped opponents, quarterback rates).

### 2.14 Game Scripts (`engines/game_script_engine.py`, `db/migrations/2026_11_18_game_scripts.sql`)
JR, Oct 15: writers may invent what the data doesn't record, but every writer has to tell the same story about a game. The engine decides that story once per completed game, deterministically (no model call; ~5 s for a whole season) and stores it in `game_scripts`. Invented detail must never contradict recorded data.
- **Honoured (recorded):** the final score; each team's captured quarter scores and overtime (`team_stats.q1`–`ot`, used only when they add up to the score); every touchdown in the game's player lines (rush TDs to the runner, pass TDs paired passer → receiver, defensive and return TDs from captured lines); captured kicking (field goals made, extra points made and the longest field goal, which is one of the kicks); captured two-point conversions and fourth-down results where real (not estimated, as in `get_game_box_score`); each player's yards and longest play (no touchdown play is longer than his longest or his total); interceptions thrown and lost fumbles (the team's captured turnovers minus interceptions, else the lines' fumbles); captured defensive lines (who sacked, intercepted, forced fumbles); captured highlights in `game_highlights` when present. A defender is never credited with a takeaway or sack the data doesn't record: without captured defensive lines a takeaway names only the quarterback ("interceptor not recorded"), and the defensive standouts are the team's top-rated defenders, marked to be praised in words only.
- **Anchoring to captured highlights (`_script_from_highlights`):** when a game has recorded highlights in `game_highlights` (from Madden's in-game `VIEW HIGHLIGHTS` postgame screen; `db/migrations/2026_11_23_game_highlights.sql`), the script's scoring timeline, clocks, quarters, and key moments are built directly from the real highlight plays rather than synthesized. Scoring plays, go-ahead/tying tags, lead for good, and key takeaways/sacks/fourth-down stops follow the exact sequence of the game. `quarter_scores_source` is set to `"captured highlights & box score"`, and the complete highlight reel is preserved in `script["highlights"]`. Games without captured highlights continue to use the backtracking partition search and sampling.
- **How the score is made (`solve_points`):** the recorded touchdowns plus the cheapest mix of extra points, two-point tries, field goals and safeties that reaches the final score (missed tries, two-point tries, safeties and unusually many field goals cost more; recorded kicking fixes its counts). When a team's defense and returns weren't captured, up to two unrecorded defensive touchdowns may fill a score that would otherwise need many field goals; synthetic lines whose touchdowns can't fit the score lose touchdowns at a high cost. With captured quarters, the next-cheapest mixes are tried until one splits into the quarter scores (`_partitions`, a backtracking search).
- **Ordering and drama:** 240 orderings that fit the data are sampled (periods weighted toward the 2nd and 4th quarters, scores at least 75 s apart; with captured quarters, only the order inside each quarter varies). Margins of 3 or 6 can become overtime wins (a walk-off field goal or try-less touchdown), and ties are overtime ties. Each ordering gets a drama score (lead changes, ties, the winner's largest deficit, how late the winning score came, overtime, minus the winner's largest lead). The engine keeps the ordering at the game's excitement quantile, `((E − 35) / 45)^1.6` clamped to 0–1 (raw $E$, §2.1), with a small seeded jitter. The 1.6 curve corrects random orderings swinging more than real games: 2035 lands at about 17% 10-point comebacks, 9% late winners, 21% wire-to-wire and 3% overtime.
- **Invented (not recorded):** the clock of each score, touchdown play lengths within the player's yards, field-goal distances, the try result, drives (plays, yards and time inside the clock gap since the previous score, carrying over from the 1st into the 2nd or the 3rd into the 4th quarter, a shorter field after a takeaway, total scoring-drive yards at most 90% of the team's captured yards), when takeaways, sacks, missed field goals, fourth-down stops and big non-scoring plays (captured longest plays) happened (never inside a scoring drive; a takeaway may set up the next score), halftime adjustments (a defense that allowed 14+ before halftime and 7 or fewer after, worded from its coach's front and coverage philosophy; an offense held to 7 or fewer that scored 14+ after, worded from its top runner, receiver or passer), and a blown lead of 10+.
- **Script fields:** `matchup`, `shape` (`overtime`, `comeback` (winner down 10+), `late_winner` (winning score in the last 5:00), `back_and_forth` (3+ lead changes), `garbage_time`, `rout` (21+), `pulled_away`, `wire_to_wire`, `took_control`, `tie`), `storyline` (one sentence), `quarter_scores` with `quarter_scores_source` (`captured highlights & box score` / `captured box score` / `generated`), `halftime`, `end_of_regulation` (OT), `lead_changes`, `ties`, `largest_lead`, `winner_largest_deficit`, `scoring` (period, clock, team, play with try, drive, `points`, `score` (text) and `score_after` (points by team abbreviation), tags: opening score, go-ahead, tying, lead for good, set up by a takeaway, drive kept alive on fourth down), `key_moments`, `adjustments`, `defensive_standouts` (`recorded` true/false), `highlights` (the full highlight reel if captured). `render_script` turns it into prompt lines; `script_summary` gives the storyline, halftime, the winning score and a few moments.
- **Stability:** seeded by game id and `SCRIPT_VERSION`. `facts_hash` hashes only the recorded facts (scores, quarters, lines, kicking, defense, turnovers, two-point and fourth-down results, highlights, $E$), not ratings, coaches or rosters, so a stored script is rebuilt only when its game's facts change (e.g. a games capture replacing the backfill's lines, which happens before that week's articles; a corrected box score always rebuilds) or with `--force`. **Publish lock:** `--force` skips a script once anything in `news_feed` from a later week of that season was created after the script (`published_since`), since articles and posts may cite it and the clutch counts (§2.15) build on it; `--unlock-published` overrides. Bumping `SCRIPT_VERSION` doesn't rebuild stored scripts by itself.
- **Pipeline and readers:** the weekly run's engines step (`engines/game_stat_generator.py`) runs `game_script_engine.run(season)` right after $E$. `get_game_box_score` ends with `game_script` (the rendered script); the assignment brief's `recap_slate` games carry `game_story` (`script_summary`, 2 moments); Agent Wire game facts (`Week.game_facts`: reactions, beat notes, memes) carry `how_it_happened`. Any of them builds a missing script on the spot (`get_script`, `get_scripts`, `ensure_script`).
- **Tests:** `tests/test_game_script_engine.py` (score decomposition, the captured overtime game's quarters, walk-off and kicking, weekly games' scorers and yardage limits, determinism, clock order and spacing, excitement → drama, anchored highlights game scripts, rendering); the publish lock in `tests/test_clutch_engine.py`.

### 2.15 Clutch Profile (`engines/clutch_engine.py`)
JR, Oct 15: late-game records for teams and quarterbacks ("blows a lot of leads", "lives for fourth-quarter comebacks"), counted from the game scripts (§2.14). Computed live, nothing stored. In captured games the quarter scores behind them are real; elsewhere the order of scoring is the script's, so the counts are canon (shared by every writer, contradicted by no recorded data) rather than measured: nothing measured (ratings, record book, popularity) reads them.
- **Per team per game (`team_situations`):** result and final; halftime margin and margin after three quarters (from the script's quarter scores); the margins at the start of the fourth quarter and after each fourth-quarter or overtime score; **fourth-quarter comeback** = a win after trailing by one score (1–8) at one of those points; **game-winning drive** = a win whose lead-for-good score was the team's, in the fourth quarter or overtime; **blown double-digit lead** = a loss after leading by 10+; **blown fourth-quarter lead** = a loss after leading at one of those points; overtime; largest lead and deficit; points by quarter.
- **Profiles (`profile`, `team_profile`, `qb_profile`):** games, record, comebacks, game-winning drives, both blown-lead counts, records leading / trailing / tied at halftime and leading / trailing after three quarters, overtime record, points per game by quarter, largest comeback win and largest lead lost, and one line per comeback, game-winning drive and blown lead (week, opponent, result, deficit or lead, the winning score). Regular season and postseason are separate blocks. A quarterback is credited with the games he started: the team's QB with the most pass attempts in that game (`player_stats`).
- **League leaders (`league_leaders`):** regular-season QBs by comebacks and by game-winning drives, teams by blown double-digit and fourth-quarter leads, teams by wins after trailing at halftime (top 8 each).
- **Writer tool:** `get_clutch_profile(team_identifier, quarterback, season)` (§7), week-cut like the other tools.
- **2035:** comebacks and game-winning drives lead with Christopher Burr (HOU, 6 and 6); the most blown double-digit leads are 3 (seven teams). Weeks 1–16 credit touchdowns from distributed lines, so team counts are steadier than quarterback counts until weekly runs replace them.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_clutch_engine.py` (comeback, blown lead and rout situations, the one-score rule, team and quarterback profiles with the playoff split and week cutoff, league leaders, tool registration).

---

### 2.16 Team Direction & Needs (`engines/gm_direction.py`, team management step 3)
Every GM works from its team's **direction** (`CONTEND` | `RETOOL` | `REBUILD`) and ranked **positional needs**, stored in `gm_team_seasons` (§3.3f). Inputs come from `team_seasons` (S1 columns; the prior season's `wins`), `team_cap_seasons.cap_space`, rosters (`player_seasons`, `released_week IS NULL`, the `DEF` row left out), the latest `injuries` week and `player_contracts`.
- **Score:** `g = wins + losses`, `form = (pythagorean_wins + expected_wins)/(2g)`, `prior = 0.5·wins₋₁/17 + 0.5·pct_rank(team_ovr)`, `p = g/(g+6)·form + 6/(g+6)·prior`, `proj_wins = wins + p·(17 − g)`. `D = z(proj_wins) + 0.5·z(team_ovr) + 0.25·z(opp_adj_credit_recent) + 4·(trajectory_multiplier − 1) − 0.3·max(0, starter_age − 27.5) + 0.2·(aggressiveness − 0.5)` (z across the league; `starter_age` = mean age of the team's healthy starters by slot).
- **Bands** by rank of D: top 10 CONTEND, bottom 8 REBUILD, the rest RETOOL (edges = midpoints between the 10th/11th and the 24th/25th D). **Hysteresis:** with a previous direction, a team changes only when D crosses an edge by more than 0.3. **Overrides** (last): `proj_wins ≥ 10.5` → CONTEND; `cap_space < 0` can't be REBUILD (→ RETOOL).
- **When:** the direction is set the first time a team has none, then re-checked only at checkpoint weeks `{1, 6, 7 (deadline − 1), 24 (offseason)}` (`--force-direction` overrides); needs are recomputed every run.
- **Needs:** starter slots QB1 RB1 WR3 TE1 OT2 IOL3 EDGE2 DT2 LB2 CB3 S2 K1 P1 (mock draft group map, K and P apart). A team's slot OVRs are its top-n healthy `overall_rating` in the group (out ≥ 3 weeks or IR doesn't count; an empty slot is 40). `slot_gap = Σ max(0, league 60th-percentile slot OVR − team slot OVR)`; `injury` = the part of the gap the injuries cause; `expiring` = for each starter on an expiring deal (`years_remaining = 1`, no extension) 0.5 × the gap his slot would have if he left (the next healthy player steps up), ×1.5 for REBUILD and RETOOL. `score = (slot_gap + expiring) × TRADE_POS_WEIGHT[group]` (QB 1.25, OT/EDGE 1.12, CB 1.10, WR 1.02, DT 1.00, TE 0.90, IOL 0.88, LB 0.85, S 0.82, RB 0.80, K 0.30, P 0.25). **Surplus:** depth players within 3 OVR of the group's weakest starter.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_gm_direction.py` (band counts, hysteresis, overrides, early-season prior, injured starter, expiring weight, surplus, DEF rows, checkpoint behaviour).

### 2.17 Trade Value (`engines/trade_value.py`, team management step 3)
Everything is in **dollars of surplus**, so players and picks compare directly. The fits are recomputed from live data every run (pure functions over rows).
- **Market worth** `W(ovr, group)`: `ln(cap_hit) = a_group + b·(ovr − 75)` over this season's `ACTIVE`, `SCAN` contracts of players with ≥ 4 years' experience (one slope, group intercepts; a group with < 15 rows uses the league intercept), × `MARKET_PREMIUM` 1.3 (replacing a player costs more than the average existing deal; without it a star on a fair deal would be worth nothing in trade).
- **Aging**: the mean same-player OVR change from last season by age band (≤23, 24–26, 27–29, 30–31, 32–33, 34+) and group, shrunk (20 pseudo-observations) toward the league band mean, itself shrunk toward a generic curve (+1.5 … −3.0). Dev trait adds +1/+2/+2 OVR a year up to age 26 (Star/Superstar/X-Factor; blank = Normal).
- **Player value for team T**: `V_T = Σ_y d_T[y]·(W(ovr_y)·u_T·youth − cap_hit_y)` for each season through the contract's end (a re-signing's `projected` years extend it and are flagged); 0 afterwards (he re-signs at market). `u_T` = 1 + 0.5·(need share) if he'd start in a slot T needs, 1.0 if he'd start, 0.3 as a backup, × `position_bias`. `youth = 1 − 0.05·youth_bias·(age − 28)` clamped 0.7–1.3. Discounts `d_T`: CONTEND `[1, .55, .35, .25, .2]`, RETOOL `[.8, .75, .6, .5, .4]`, REBUILD `[.35, .8, .9, .9, .85]`. The neutral **league value** `V_L` uses RETOOL discounts, `u = 1` and no persona.
- **Pick value**: expected OVR (the rookie's roster OVR, else the scouted one) and year-1 rookie cap hit by overall pick from the latest completed draft in `draft_prospects`, each smoothed (±4 picks) and made non-increasing (pool-adjacent violators). Per rookie-deal year k < 4: OVR grows `ROOKIE_GROWTH` (+2.5, +1.5, +1.5), `E[W]` includes the outcome spread `σ_k = 5 + 1.5k` (`× exp(b²σ²/2)`), `u_k = 0.3 + 0.7·Φ((ovr − 74)/4)` (the chance he starts), rookie hits rise 5% a year and count at `ROOKIE_COST_WEIGHT` 0.25. **Calibration note:** Madden's rookie scale is steep (pick 1 ≈ $13.8M), so pure surplus made the first picks the least valuable; these three knobs are for JR's `--report` review. Next year's pick uses the original team's projected slot; later drafts the middle of the round with one more year of discount per year out. Team value × `pick_affinity` × 1.2 REBUILD / 0.8 CONTEND.
- **Not tradeable**: no position group (the `DEF` row), no `ACTIVE` contract, a no-trade clause (C6), any injured player (C7), a player acquired by trade this season (no flipping). Players signed or re-signed this season can be traded (C7).
- **Proposal engine** (`engines/gm_trade_engine.py`): runs for `league_state.current_week` while the window is open (`IN_SEASON`: weeks 1–`TRADE_DEADLINE_WEEK` 8, JR 2026-10-20; the NFL deadline, not Napper's fantasy week 11). Order: expire stale proposals (PROPOSED/APPROVED whose week has passed, or past the deadline) → direction (checkpoints) and needs (§2.16) → re-check open proposals (illegal now → EXPIRED with the failed checks) → injury triggers → budget → search → legality → acceptance → choice → rationale → store.
  - **Budget:** `IN_SEASON_TARGET = 17` (JR), scaled to the weeks the engine runs (the first engine proposal's week; 2036 starts at week 4 → 12.75). Weekly shape flat, the two weeks before the deadline 3×. `e_w = (target − executed) × shape_w / Σ remaining shape`; `p_exec = (3 + executed)/(5 + decided)` (Beta(3,2), decided = EXECUTED + REJECTED); new proposals `n ~ Poisson(e_w / p_exec)` from `random.Random(f"{season}:{week}")`, minus any already made this week; caps: 4 new a week, 6 open (PROPOSED + APPROVED), each team in at most one new proposal a week. A week with no budget may still get one urgent-injury proposal (probability 0.5).
  - **Injury trigger:** a CONTEND team's starter by slot (full lineup) in a group weighted ≥ 1.0 or at QB, on IR or out ≥ 4 weeks, sets `gm_team_seasons.urgent_need = {group, player_id, weeks_remaining, is_ir}`; that team initiates first with probability `0.35 × (0.5 + aggressiveness) × weight`. It clears when he is back under 2 weeks, the group stops being a need, or the team stops contending.
  - **Search:** initiators drawn without replacement by `need score (top two trade needs, K/P left out) × aggressiveness × (CONTEND 1.5, RETOOL 1.0, REBUILD 0.6)`; a REBUILD initiator sells half the time (its 28+ starters to non-REBUILD teams needing the group). Buying: for each of the top two needs, players on other teams who'd start for the buyer and whom the buyer values above their own team (top 6 by the gap); packages = the target plus up to 2 of the seller's non-starters/picks (top 7 by the buyer's value) against 1–3 of the buyer's non-starters and owned picks (top 8 by the seller's value), exhaustive, two-team only. User-controlled teams with suggestions off are left out; proposals involving a user-controlled team are flagged `advisory:<ABBR>` (its GM advises JR).
  - **Legality** (`check_legality` → `{check: {ok, detail}}`, stored in `legality`): `cap` (each team's `cap_space − incoming 2036 hits + outgoing hits − dead money ≥ $1M`, `$3M` when the cap row is derived; dead money = the sender's `cut_penalty`, `TRADE_DEAD_MONEY`, C4), `ntc`, `roster` (46–53 in season, 65 offseason; a team already outside the range may trade as long as the deal doesn't move it further out), `slots` (no team left without a QB, K or P), `deadline`, `picks` (the sender owns each unused pick), `booking` (no asset in another open proposal), `tradeable` (§2.17 exclusions), `fairness` (`|V_L(A gets) − V_L(B gets)| ≤ max(25% of the larger, $4M)`). A derived cap row with under $3M of headroom after the deal adds flag `rescan:<ABBR>` (run Contract scan `--teams` first).
  - **Acceptance:** both GMs: `Δ_T = Σ V_T(in) − Σ V_T(out) − dead money paid (× this season's discount) ≥ θ_T = $1.5M × (1 − aggressiveness)`; a CONTEND team receiving a player in its urgent-need group accepts down to `−$2M × risk_appetite`. Choice: seeded softmax (temperature $1M) over the five best `min(Δ_A, Δ_B)`. `deal_hash` (teams + sorted assets) is unique per season, so a re-run never duplicates.
  - **Stored** (`gm_trade_proposals` + `gm_trade_assets`, §3.3f): `expires_week` = the week made, values, cap before/after, dead money, roster after, derived flags, legality, rationale, `rumor_ok` (seeded per deal, ~40%; step 5 may use it only when the proposal isn't executed), `flags`.
  - **Steps** (`steps_text`, shown for APPROVED proposals): switch team A to user control (or "you already control"), trade with team B (send / receive), submit and switch to team B to accept (user control or CPU trades on just for this trade, C2/C10), Couldn't make it if Madden refuses, switch back, Made it.
  - **Rationale** (`engines/gm_rationale.py`): `build_fact_sheet` (assets with position, name, OVR, age, dev, cap hit, years left; both teams' direction, needs, cap before/after, GM, voice) → template headline + each side's two sentences.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_gm_trade_engine.py` (each legality check on its own fixture, the over-max one-for-one exception, acceptance needs both sides, dead money against the sender, injury trigger for a CONTEND starter only, seeded/capped budget and the season mean, deal_hash re-run guard, expiry, a full search, user team with suggestions off); `tests/test_trade_value.py` (W monotone, small-group intercept, aging shrinkage, surplus ends at contract end, projected years flagged, REBUILD vs CONTEND on a veteran, need/backup utility, pick curve monotone and R1 > R2, exclusions).

## 3. Database Schema & Architecture (PostgreSQL)

The database utilizes a relational temporal model in PostgreSQL (`dbname=madden`). `db/init_db.sql` reproduces the full live schema (tables, constraints, generated columns, views, functions, triggers) on an empty database and is safe to re-run; it does not add columns to existing tables, so schema changes to the live DB ship as migrations under `db/migrations/` and must also be folded into `init_db.sql`.

### 3.1 Core Entity & State Tables
- **`teams`**:
  - `id` (PK, int), `name` (varchar), `city` (varchar), `abbreviation` (varchar, e.g. 'CAR', 'TB', 'KC'), `conference` (varchar), `division` (varchar), `team_color` (varchar hex), `stadium_name` (varchar). `UNIQUE(name)`.
- **`players`**:
  - `id` (PK, int), `full_name` (varchar), `player_name` (varchar, e.g. 'G. Crawford'), `player_number` (int - jersey number, default 0), `height` (varchar), `weight` (int lbs), `espn_id` (int, nullable).
- **`league_state`**:
  - `id` (PK, int, default 1 — single row), `current_season` (int), `current_week` (int). Column defaults are 2024 / 1; the live league row is currently season 2036, week 4 (2026-10-19). `current_week` is Madden's current (next unplayed) week in `games.week` numbering, set by each weekly capture load from the captured week label (`pipeline/load_capture.py`); `current_season` is set by hand at a new season or automatically incremented (with `current_week` reset to 1) when starting a start-of-season cadence run (`--mode season`). The backfill, `pipeline/main.py`'s trigger and `DatabaseHandler.get_current_league_state()` read both and never infer or write them.

### 3.2 Temporal Snapshot Ledgers
- **`team_seasons`**:
  - `id` (PK), `team_id` (FK), `season` (int), `wins` (int), `losses` (int), `team_ovr` (int), `off_ovr` (int), `def_ovr` (int), `pass_off_rank` (int), `rush_off_rank` (int), `pass_rush_rank` (int), `coverage_rank` (int), `watchability_index` (float8), `popularity_index` (float8), `opp_adj_credit`, `opp_adj_credit_recent`, `sos_rating`, `expected_wins` (float8, nullable; see 2.4, added by `db/migrations/2026_10_14_opp_adj_credit.sql`), `watchability_sigma_top`, `p_draft`, `y_prod`, `purgatory_penalty` (float8, nullable; TWI components, see 2.2, added by `db/migrations/2026_10_15_stored_index_components.sql`), `trajectory_multiplier` (2.4), `pythagorean_wins`, `pace_production` (2.6) (float8, nullable; added by `db/migrations/2026_10_16_trajectory_and_efficiency.sql`), `s_playoff` (2.7; added by `db/migrations/2026_10_17_playoff_clout_awards_exposure.sql`); Team Stats tabs from the capture (nullable, `2026_10_23_capture_loader.sql`, not read yet): `third_down_conv`/`_att`/`_pct`, `fourth_down_conv`/`_att`/`_pct`, `two_point_conv`/`_att`/`_pct`, `red_zone_att`/`_td`/`_fg`/`_pct`, `red_zone_def_att`/`_td`/`_fg`/`_pct`, `penalties`, `penalty_yards`; observed tendencies (§2.12, `2026_11_13_observed_tendencies.sql`): `pass_rate_oe`, `fourth_down_oe` (float8), `observed_pass_lean`, `observed_fourth_down` (`Low` / `Medium` / `High`), `tendency_games` (int). `UNIQUE(team_id, season)`; `wins`/`losses` are maintained by `trigger_update_team_records` (see 3.6).
- **`player_seasons`**:
  - `id` (PK), `player_id` (FK), `team_id` (FK), `season` (int), `position` (varchar), `age` (int), `overall_rating` (int), `years_of_experience` (int; 0 = rookie, 1 = second-year), `popularity_index` (float8), `spike_index`, `r_rating`, `f_platform`, `t_success`, `a_awards`, `e_exposure` (float8, nullable; player Pop components, see 2.5), `s_playoff` (float8, nullable; 2.7); roster card fields `dev_trait`, `archetype`, `role` (varchar, nullable; `2026_10_23_capture_loader.sql`; `overall_rating` is the roster list OVR, the card's base OVR and morale aren't stored); `released_week` (int, nullable; `2026_12_08_transactions.sql`: set when a League Transactions release makes him a free agent, NULL = on the team's roster; `team_id` keeps his last team, §3.3e). `UNIQUE(player_id, season)`.

### 3.3 Games & Statistics Tables
- **`games`**:
  - `id` (PK), `season` (int), `week` (int), `home_team_id` (FK), `away_team_id` (FK), `home_score` (int), `away_score` (int), `status` (varchar: 'COMPLETED' | 'SCHEDULED' | 'BYE'), `game_time` (varchar, e.g. 'Sun 1:00 PM'), `game_type` (varchar, default 'REGULAR_SEASON'; playoff games `WILD_CARD` (week 19), `DIVISIONAL` (20), `CONFERENCE` (21), `SUPER_BOWL` (23), created by the weekly capture load from Madden's schedule, `pipeline/load_capture.PLAYOFF_GAME_TYPES`), `excitement_index` (float8), `projected_excitement` (float8, nullable; pre-game projected $E$), `completed_at` (timestamp). `UNIQUE(season, week, home_team_id, away_team_id)` (constraint `games_season_week_home_away_key`).
- **`player_stats`**:
  - `id` (PK), `game_id` (FK), `team_id` (FK), `player_name` (varchar), `position` (varchar), `completions`, `pass_attempts`, `pass_yards`, `pass_tds`, `interceptions`, `rush_attempts`, `rush_yards`, `rush_tds`, `receptions`, `rec_yards`, `rec_tds`, `fumb`, `fantasy_points` (numeric(6,2), **generated column**); from `2026_10_23_capture_loader.sql` (nullable, written only by the capture loader's games mode, not read yet): `player_id` (FK; keyed in a later migration), `pass_comp_pct`, `pass_rating`, `pass_ypa`, `pass_long`, `pass_sacks`, `rush_avg`, `rush_btk`, `rush_yac`, `rush_long`, `rec_avg`, `rec_rac`, `rec_rac_avg`, `rec_long`; `line_source` (`2026_11_14_player_stats_line_source.sql`): `CAPTURED` (written by the capture loader's games mode and the legacy `db_handler` loader), `WEEKLY` (the backfill filled the team's only missing game: exact) or `DISTRIBUTED` (spread over several games: synthetic); existing rows were set from whether the game has captured category lines (2035: Week 17 captured, the rest distributed). `UNIQUE(game_id, player_name, position)` and, from `2026_10_24_player_id_keys.py`, unique `(game_id, player_id)` where `player_id` is set; trigger `player_stats_fill_player_id` fills `player_id` on inserts that only name the player (the backfill), from players + player_seasons (same team and position, list name).
  - `fantasy_points` is a PostgreSQL `GENERATED ALWAYS ... STORED` column computed from the row's box score with full-PPR scoring: 0.04/pass yd (1 per 25), 4/pass TD, −2/INT, 0.1/rush & rec yd (1 per 10), 6/rush & rec TD, 1/reception, −2/fumble; NULL stats count as 0. It recomputes automatically on every insert or stat correction and is read-only: ingestion and backfill code must **not** insert or update it (Postgres rejects writes). All fantasy surfaces (`vw_fantasy_scores`, `vw_fantasy_defense_vs_position`, game logs, rankings, player popularity) read this column.
  - Introduced by `db/migrations/2026_10_12_fantasy_points_generated.sql` (self-verifying; rolls back on any value change). Restoring a database dump taken before 2026-10-12 brings back the old plain column; re-apply the migration afterwards. Fresh databases built from `db/init_db.sql` already have the generated column.
- **`team_stats`**:
  - `id` (PK), `game_id` (FK), `team_name` (varchar), `score`, `first_downs`, `total_offense`, `total_plays`, `rushing_attempts`, `rushing_yards`, `rushing_tds`, `passing_completions`, `passing_attempts`, `passing_yards`, `passing_tds`, `third_down_attempts`, `third_down_conversions`, `fourth_down_attempts`, `fourth_down_conversions` (int, no default: NULL = unknown until the estimator fills them, 2026_10_29), `turnovers` (all int); box-score fields from `2026_10_23_capture_loader.sql` (nullable): `q1`–`q4`, `ot` (NULL when the digits were unreadable), `punt_return_yards`, `kick_return_yards`, `total_yards_gained`, `two_point_conversions`, `red_zone_pct`, `red_zone_tds`, `red_zone_fgs`, `penalty_yards`, `time_of_possession_s`; from `2026_10_29_team_box_estimates.sql`: `red_zone_attempts`, `two_point_attempts`, `penalties` (int, nullable) and `estimated_fields` (text[], default `{}`: the columns `engines/team_box_estimator.py` filled rather than a capture); from `2026_10_31_team_box_exact_fields.sql`: `exact_fields` (text[], default `{}`: the estimated columns that are exact, i.e. a team's only missing cell for that column; the 2035 Weeks 1–17 backlog has none). `UNIQUE(game_id, team_name)`.
- **`season_stats`**:
  - `id` (PK), `season` (int), `player_id` (FK), `player_name` (varchar), `team_id` (FK), `position` (varchar), the same box-score counters as `player_stats`, `total_fantasy_points` (numeric(7,2)), `ppg_fantasy_points` (numeric(5,2)); offense extras from `2026_10_23_capture_loader.sql` (nullable, not read yet): `pass_comp_pct`, `pass_ypg`, `pass_rating`, `pass_ypa`, `pass_long`, `pass_sacks`, `rush_avg`, `rush_ypg`, `rush_btk`, `rush_yac`, `rush_long`, `rec_avg`, `rec_ypg`, `rec_rac`, `rec_rac_avg`, `rec_long`. `UNIQUE(season, player_name, team_id, position)` and unique `(season, player_id)` where `player_id` is set (`2026_10_24_player_id_keys.py`, which re-linked the existing rows from the refreshed roster and merged a player's duplicate rows, larger value per stat); trigger `season_stats_fill_player_id` as for `player_stats`.
- **Capture stat categories** (`2026_10_23_capture_loader.sql`; hybrid layout, docs/PIPELINE_MIGRATION.md phase 3): every category the Season Stats / game player-stats capture has beyond offense, in a season table (`season`, `player_id`, PK both) and a game table (`game_id` FK ON DELETE CASCADE, `player_id`, PK both), each with `team_id`, `position`: `season_stat_defense` / `game_stat_defense` (`solo`, `assists`, `tackles`, `tfl`, `sacks`, `ints`, `int_yds`, `int_avg`, `int_long`, `deflections`, `forced_fumbles`, `fumble_recoveries`, `fumble_yds`, `blocks`, `safeties`, `tds`), `_kicking` (`fgm`, `fga`, `fg_pct`, `fg_long`, `fg_blocked`, `xpm`, `xpa`, `xp_pct`, `xp_blocked`, `fgm_29` … `fga_50`, `kickoffs`, `touchbacks`), `_punting` (`punts`, `yards`, `average`, `net_yards`, `net_average`, `blocked`, `inside_20`, `touchbacks`, `longest`), `_kickreturn` / `_puntreturn` (`returns`, `yards`, `average`, `tds`, `longest`), `_blocking` (`sacks_allowed`), `_general` (`games_played`, `downs_played`; game level `downs_played` only). A row exists only where Madden lists the player (defense is listed by tackles, about the top 250 defenders; a missing row is unknown, not zero). Madden's Season Stats screens cover the regular season only (JR, Oct 14): in the postseason the season tables (and `season_stats`, `team_seasons` Team Stats totals) stop at Week 18, and playoff production exists only where a game's box score is captured (`games` mode). The writer tools read them through the `season_stat_lines` / `game_stat_lines` views (§7: `get_player_season_stats`, `get_game_box_score`, `get_top_rated_players`, `get_defense_special_teams_leaders`).
- **Views** `season_stat_lines` (one row per `player_seasons` row: offense from `season_stats` + every category, prefixed `blk_`, `def_`, `kick_`, `punt_`, `kr_`, `pr_`) and `game_stat_lines` (one row per player and game that has any game-level row).
- **`game_scripts`** (`2026_11_18_game_scripts.sql`, §2.14): `game_id` (PK, FK `games` ON DELETE CASCADE), `season`, `week`, `version` (`SCRIPT_VERSION`), `facts_hash` (the recorded facts the script was built from), `script` (jsonb), `generated_at`. Index on `(season, week)`.
- **`game_highlights`** (`2026_11_23_game_highlights.sql`, §2.14): `game_id` (PK, FK `games` ON DELETE CASCADE), `season`, `week`, `highlights` (jsonb array of real highlight plays: `quarter`, `clock`, `play_type`, `play`, `rating`), `created_at`. Index on `(season, week)`. Populated by `pipeline/load_capture.py` from `games/<NN>/highlights.json`.
- **`captures`**: one row per capture run: `folder` (unique), `mode` (weekly | roster | season_schedule | games | playoff | contracts; a folder with `game_scores.csv` and `schedule.csv` loads as `playoff`), `season`, `week`, `status` (captured | extracted | loaded | failed), `row_counts` (jsonb), Gemini token totals, `error`, `captured_at` / `extracted_at` / `loaded_at`.
- **`llm_usage`**: one row per Gemini call: `source` (extract | newsroom), `capture_id` (FK, nullable), `label`, `model`, `input_tokens`, `cached_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `thinking_tokens`, `created_at`.
- **`awards`**:
  - `id` (PK), `season` (int), `award_type` (varchar), `player_id` (FK), `player_name` (varchar), `team_id` (FK), `coach_name` (varchar), `coach_id` (FK `coaches`, COTY rows; `2026_10_30_coaches.sql`), `position` (varchar). Partial unique indexes (`2026_11_20_all_pro_awards.sql`): `UNIQUE(season, award_type, position, player_id) WHERE player_id IS NOT NULL` (supporting First/Second Team All-Pro multi-player and multi-slot honors while preventing duplicate selections), `UNIQUE(season, award_type)` for major player awards (`MVP`, `OPOY`, `DPOY`, `OROY`, `DROY`, `SBMVP`), and `UNIQUE(season, award_type) WHERE award_type = 'COTY'`. The historical import (`pipeline/batch_menus.py`) writes COTY with both `coach_name` and `coach_id`, creating the `coaches` row if the name is new; year-end major awards (`MVP`, `OPOY`, `OROY`, `DPOY`, `DROY`, `COTY`) are parsed and recorded from the Week 22 `AWARDS_TRACKER` (`AWARDS_WINNERS`) article by `newsroom/award_winners.py` (§7.6c); `SBMVP` is parsed and recorded from the post-Super Bowl `SUPER_BOWL_GAME_RECAP` article by `newsroom/award_winners.py` (§7.6d); year-end All-Pro teams are populated from `OFFICIAL_AP_ALL_PRO_ROSTER` by `newsroom/all_pro.py` (§7.6b).

### 3.3a Coaches & Staff (`db/migrations/2026_10_30_coaches.sql`, `2026_10_31_coach_baseline.sql`, `2026_11_01_coach_schemes.sql`, `2026_11_02_drop_coach_archetype.sql`, `2026_11_03_whitfield_2034.sql`, `2026_11_05_coach_tendencies.sql`, `2026_11_06_coty_scores.sql`, `2026_11_07_coach_2034_totals.sql`, `2026_11_09_coach_full_names.sql`, `2026_11_10_whitfield_tendencies.sql`, `2026_11_11_whitfield_ratings.sql`, `2026_11_24_coty_historical_records.sql`, `2026_11_25_coordinators_and_slants.sql`, `2026_11_27_whitfield_bears_coordinator_lore.sql`, `2026_11_28_panthers_bears_coordinators_update.sql`, `2026_11_29_panthers_shavon_bell_dc.sql`, `2026_12_01_coach_tenures_correction.sql`)
Head coaches and coordinator staffs (docs/COACH_SLANTS_AND_COORDINATORS_PLAN.md). `engines/coty_engine.py` reads `coaches` and `coach_tenures` (§2.11). The writer tools read coaches, coordinators, tenures, snapshots, and tendencies through `engines/coach_profile.py` (§7: `get_coach_profile`, `get_team_context` (`head_coach`, `offensive_coordinator`, `defensive_coordinator`) and `get_matchup_edges` (`coaching_matchup`)). They count only games the article may see: the latest `coach_seasons` snapshot before the article's season plus visible games, the same sum as `coach_records`, which has no cutoff and serves ad-hoc queries. `tests/test_coach_profile.py` and `tests/test_coordinators.py` cover staff totals, scheme inheritance, Carolina continuity (Canales HC 2024–2033 with Brad Idzik OC and Ejiro Evero DC; Marcus Whitfield HC from 2034 with Charles Grant OC and Shavon Bell DC 2034–present), and Chicago Bears continuity (Ben Johnson HC 2025–present, Thomas Brown OC 2026–2027, Marcus Whitfield OC 2028–2033 until hired by Carolina, Brett Vance OC 2034–present promoted from QB coach/college coordinator, Dennis Allen DC 2026–present). **Coach and coordinator changes are manual:** in the offseason, close old tenures (`end_season`) and add new tenures.
- **`coaches`**: `id` (PK), `coach_name` (varchar, unique), `slant` (`OFFENSIVE` | `DEFENSIVE` | `CEO`, default `OFFENSIVE`, `2026_11_25_coordinators_and_slants.sql`), `offensive_scheme`, `defensive_scheme` (varchar, nullable). Head coach slants set tactical specialization:
  - `OFFENSIVE`: Head coach imports offensive scheme, playbook, and pass-lean tendency; defensive scheme remains intact under DC.
  - `DEFENSIVE`: Head coach imports defensive front (`3-4` or `4-3`), blitz rate, and man coverage; offensive scheme remains intact under OC.
  - `CEO`: Program manager focusing on fourth-down aggressiveness and game management; unit schemes delegated to OC and DC.
- **`coordinators`**: `id` (PK), `coordinator_name` (varchar), `side` (`OFFENSIVE` | `DEFENSIVE`), `scheme` (varchar), `playbook` (varchar), `pass_lean`, `blitz_rate`, `man_coverage` (each `Low`/`Medium`/`High`), `origin_notes` (text). Seeded by `engines/coordinator_seeder.py` from 2026 baseline with procedural fallbacks.
- **`coordinator_tenures`**: `id` (PK), `coordinator_id` (FK `coordinators`), `team_id` (FK `teams`), `role` (`OC` | `DC`), `start_season` (int), `end_season` (int, NULL = active). `UNIQUE(coordinator_id, team_id, role, start_season)`. Every active franchise maintains exactly one OC and one DC per season.
- **`coach_tenures`**: `coach_id` (FK), `team_id` (FK), `start_season`, `end_season` (NULL = current). `UNIQUE(coach_id, team_id, start_season)`. A coach's per-season record in the franchise comes from joining this to `team_seasons`. Originally all seeded with `start_season = 2026` in `2026_10_31_coach_baseline.sql`, tenures were corrected in `2026_12_01_coach_tenures_correction.sql` to reflect accurate historical hiring years: Shanahan (SF, 2017), McVay (LAR, 2017), LaFleur (GB, 2019–2035), Taylor (CIN, 2019), Campbell (DET, 2021), Sirianni (PHI, 2021–2035), O'Connell (MIN, 2022), Bowles (TB, 2022), Ryans (HOU, 2023), Steichen (IND, 2023–2035), Canales (CAR, 2024–2033), Harbaugh (LAC, 2024), Macdonald (SEA, 2024), Quinn (WAS, 2024), Johnson (CHI, 2025), Schottenheimer (DAL, 2025), Coen (JAX, 2025), Moore (NO, 2025), and Glenn (NYJ, 2025–2035). Historical prior stints are also recorded for veteran coaches: Harbaugh (Ravens 2008–2025), McCarthy (Packers 2006–2018, Cowboys 2020–2024), Stefanski (Browns 2020–2025), Vrabel (Titans 2018–2023), Saleh (Jets 2021–2024), Bowles (Jets 2015–2018), Quinn (Falcons 2015–2020), and Harbaugh (49ers 2011–2014). Carolina: Dave Canales 2024–2033, Marcus Whitfield from 2034. The Broncos' and Chiefs' coaches since 2026 are Peter Gaffney and Ron Leonard (Madden coaches with no earlier career; full names from `2026_11_09_coach_full_names.sql`), not Sean Payton and Andy Reid, who were removed (`2026_11_07_coach_2034_totals.sql`). They took over those teams' schemes and pass/blitz/man tendencies (playbooks unchanged since 2026), with Medium fourth-down aggressiveness.
- **`coach_seasons`**: `coach_id` (FK), `team_id` (FK), `season`, and the Madden coach screen's career counters as of that season: `career_wins`, `career_losses`, `career_ties`, `winning_seasons`, `playoff_wins`, `playoff_losses`, `super_bowl_wins`, `super_bowl_losses`. `UNIQUE(coach_id, season)`. These totals include pre-franchise NFL careers, so they are stored as shown rather than rebuilt from `games`. **Baseline row:** season 2025 holds each 2026 coach's real NFL head-coaching career through 2025, the starting point of the franchise (`2026_10_31_coach_baseline.sql`; source: Wikipedia's current-coaches list as of 2026-07-12, plus each coach's year-by-year record for winning seasons (W > L, partial and interim seasons included) and Super Bowl results), with `team_id` linked to their active franchise as of 2025 (`2026_12_01_coach_tenures_correction.sql`). Whitfield had no head-coaching career before 2034, so he has no baseline row. His one franchise-season row is 2034 (Carolina): 13-4, a #1-seed bye, 3-0 in the playoffs and a Super Bowl win (`2026_11_03_whitfield_2034.sql`, per JR; the 2034 regular season and early playoff rounds aren't in `games`). No other franchise-season rows (2026–2035) yet. **End-of-2034 rows** (`2026_11_07_coach_2034_totals.sql`): all 32 active coaches' totals at the end of 2034, from Madden's coach screen at 2035 Week 18 minus each team's 2035 record at that point (Carolina 17-0, the rest 16 games). The view matched the screen for every coach except Whitfield, one win short until Carolina's Week 18 game is loaded. Whitfield's 2034 row equals the earlier hand entry. Madden's careers start from different numbers than the real ones for Saleh (no Jets record) and Bowles (one more loss), so the 2025 rows are real-life history, not Madden's starting point. Dave Canales's latest row is still 2025.
- **View `coach_records`**: each coach's current career totals, the latest `coach_seasons` row (totals at the end of that season) plus every completed game his team played in later seasons while he held the job (`coach_tenures`). Regular-season games add wins, losses and ties; any non-`REGULAR_SEASON` game adds playoff wins and losses; `SUPER_BOWL` games add Super Bowl wins and losses. A winning season counts once the team's 17-game regular season is complete and wins exceed losses (Madden adds winning seasons only at season's end). Columns: `coach_id`, `coach_name`, `snapshot_season`, `career_wins`, `career_losses`, `career_ties`, `winning_seasons`, `playoff_wins`, `playoff_losses`, `super_bowl_wins`, `super_bowl_losses`. No coach-screen capture is needed: the weekly game captures keep it current.
- **`coty_scores`**: `coach_id` (FK), `team_id` (FK), `season`, `coty_score`, `coty_rank`, `win_pct`, `wins_above_expected`, `roster_outperformance` (nullable: no `team_ovr`), `win_pct_change` (nullable: no previous regular season), `first_year_with_team` (bool), `historical_bonus` (double precision, default 0.0), `historical_notes` (text[]). `UNIQUE(coach_id, season)`. The Coach of the Year race (§2.11), rewritten per season by `python3 -m engines.coty_engine [--season N]`; 32 rows for 2035.

### 3.3b Injuries (`db/migrations/2026_11_08_injuries.sql`)
- **`injuries`**:
  - `id` (SERIAL PK), `season` (int), `week` (int), `team_id` (FK `teams` ON DELETE CASCADE), `player_id` (FK `players` ON DELETE SET NULL), `player_name` (varchar), `position` (varchar), `ovr` (int), `injury_type` (varchar, diagnosis e.g. 'TURF TOE', 'BROKEN COLLARBONE', 'NONE'), `duration` (varchar, e.g. 'WEEK TO WEEK', '4 - 8 WEEKS', '0 WEEKS'), `weeks_remaining` (int, default 0), `is_ir` (bool, default false), `captured_at` (timestamptz, default now()). `UNIQUE(season, week, team_id, player_name)` and unique partial index on `(season, week, player_id) WHERE player_id IS NOT NULL`.
  - Populated weekly by `pipeline/load_capture.py` from `captures/<run>/injuries.csv` (extracted via on-device Apple Vision OCR across all 32 teams). Players on IR or with `weeks_remaining > 0` are factored into `engines/fantasy_projection_engine.py` (projected points zeroed out, omitted from `pos_rank`, discounted in `ros_total_points`). Napper reads the same rows through `engines/napper_injuries.py` (OUT/IR badges, IR-slot eligibility, AI lineups), with the same countdown rule.

### 3.3c Draft (`db/migrations/2026_11_19_draft_board.sql`, `2026_11_26_draft_selections.sql`, §2.10)
- **`draft_prospects`**: `id` (SERIAL PK), `draft_season` (int, the class: league season + 1), `projected_pick` (int, nullable for unprojected off-board picks), `projected_round` (varchar, '1'–'7' or 'UDFA'), `round_pick` (smallint), `full_name` (varchar), `position`, `college`, `height_in`, `weight`, `age`, `ovr` (scouted), `archetype`, `notes`, `source_capture` (the `captures/<run>` folder), `player_id` (FK `players` ON DELETE SET NULL; set by the rookie link), `loaded_at`; actual draft selection results (`2026_11_26_draft_selections.sql`): `actual_round` (varchar), `actual_round_pick` (smallint), `actual_pick` (smallint 1–300), `drafted_team_id` (FK `teams`), `selection_notes` (text). Unique index on `(draft_season, actual_pick) WHERE actual_pick IS NOT NULL` and index on `(draft_season, drafted_team_id)`. Populated pre-draft by `pipeline/draft_board.py` (board scan) and post-draft by `pipeline/draft_results.py` (actual results across all rounds, default 224 picks up to 300, or Round 1 loaded via CLI or `--mode draft-results`). Enables direct comparison of projected vs actual selection (risers, fallers, reaches).
- **Pick ownership** for future drafts is `draft_picks` (§3.3f): who owns each team's round 1–7 pick after trades.
- **`mock_draft_picks`**: `season` (league season of the order and needs), `week`, `pick`, `team_id` (FK `teams`), `prospect_id` (FK `draft_prospects` ON DELETE CASCADE, so a re-scanned board drops its old mocks), `locked` (bool), `selection_status`, `fit_score` (numeric), `rationale`, `generated_at`. PK `(season, week, pick)`. Written by `MockDraftEngine.save` (`pipeline/draft_board.save_mock`, replacing the week's earlier run).

### 3.3d Contracts & Cap (`db/migrations/2026_12_07_contracts.sql`, docs/TEAM_MANAGEMENT_PLAN.md §2.1)
Scanned from Manage Roster > TEAM SALARY once a season (the Season scan, after rosters) and kept current between scans from League Transactions (§3.3e; no automatic per-team re-scans; `--mode contracts --teams` re-scans by hand). **Money is `bigint` whole dollars** (`$16.8M` → 16800000), always parsed through `pipeline/money.parse_money`.
- **`team_cap_seasons`**: `team_id` (FK `teams`), `season`, `cap`, `rollover`, `adjusted_cap`, `cap_space` (can be negative), `salaries`, `cap_penalty`, `total_spend`, `next_rookie_reserve` (bigint, nullable), `scanned_at` (timestamptz), `capture_id` (FK `captures` ON DELETE SET NULL). PK `(team_id, season)`; the latest scan wins.
- **`player_contracts`**: `id` (SERIAL PK), `player_id` (FK `players`), `team_id` (FK `teams`), `season` (the league season it was scanned in), `length`, `years_remaining` (int), `total_value`, `bonus`, `cut_savings`, `cut_penalty` (bigint), `no_trade_clause` (bool, default false), `incentive` (text; the Team Salary table shows YES/NO: NO or NONE → NULL, YES → `'YES'`; the incentive's name is only on the player card), `status` (`ACTIVE` | `SUPERSEDED` | `RELEASED` | `TRADED`, default ACTIVE), `source` (`SCAN` | `TRANSACTION`, default SCAN), `scanned_at`, `capture_id`. Partial unique index `player_contracts_one_active (player_id, season) WHERE status = 'ACTIVE'`; index `(team_id, season, status)`. **Re-scan semantics:** a team's new scan marks that team's earlier `ACTIVE` rows for the season (and any other ACTIVE row of a player it lists) `SUPERSEDED` and inserts fresh rows; rows are never otherwise updated in place, so history shows what changed. Step 2 marks `RELEASED` and inserts `source = 'TRANSACTION'` rows; step 3 marks `TRADED` when JR confirms a trade.
- **`player_contract_years`**: `contract_id` (FK ON DELETE CASCADE), `year` (calendar year, e.g. 2036), `cap_hit` (bigint, NOT NULL). PK `(contract_id, year)`. One row per year column on screen (five: season … season+4); a $0 year is stored as 0. The contract ends in `season + years_remaining − 1`, not where the first $0 falls.
- **View `vw_team_cap_sheet`**: one row per `ACTIVE` contract: `team_id`, `abbreviation`, `season`, `contract_id`, `player_id`, `player_name`, `position` (`player_seasons` for that season), `length`, `years_remaining`, `contract_end_season`, `total_value`, `bonus`, `cut_savings`, `cut_penalty`, `no_trade_clause`, `incentive`, `cap_hit_y0` … `cap_hit_y4` (years season … season+4), and the team's `cap`, `adjusted_cap`, `cap_space`, `total_spend`, `next_rookie_reserve` (LEFT JOIN `team_cap_seasons`). GM agents (step 3) and newsroom cap tools (step 5) read this view.

### 3.3e League Transactions (`db/migrations/2026_12_08_transactions.sql`, `docs/team_management/02_LEAGUE_TRANSACTIONS_PLAN.md`)
Madden's CPU signs and releases (master plan D1); Mu's trades are made by hand (D2/D3). The League > LEAGUE TRANSACTIONS list is scanned weekly and loaded here.
- **`transactions`**: `id` (SERIAL PK), `season`, `week` (games.week; NULL for preseason/offseason headings), `txn_date` (text, the cleaned heading, e.g. `2036 SEASON - WEEK 4`), `type` (`SIGNED` | `RELEASED` | `TRADED` | `RE_SIGNED` | `EXTENDED` | `RESTRUCTURED` (`2026_12_09_transactions_restructured.sql`) | `OTHER`), `player_id` (FK `players` ON DELETE SET NULL: a signed depth player can be deleted by the next roster scan), `player_name` (as the row spells it), `position`, `from_team_id` / `to_team_id` (FK `teams`; release/trade: from; signing/trade: to; re-sign/extension: to = his team), `contract_years`, `contract_value` (bigint dollars), `raw_text`, `row_key`, `seq`, `parse_ok`, `flags` (comma-separated), `applied` (bool, default true; false = history only), `trade_group`, `gm_proposal_id` (int, no FK) (both for step 3 if it records confirmed trades here; the scan skips trade rows), `capture_id` (FK `captures` ON DELETE SET NULL), `created_at`. `UNIQUE (season, txn_date, row_key, seq)`; indexes on `(season, week)`, `player_id`, `trade_group`.
  - **Key** (`pipeline/transactions_parse.py`): the list has no finer date than its heading and identical rows are legitimate, so `row_key` is built from the parsed fields (`type|team|to_team|pos|full_key(name)|years|amount`; trades: sorted leg keys), so two OCR readings of one row collide; an unparsed row's key is `raw:` + its folded text. One trade row becomes one transactions row per player/pick leg (`row_key` + `#P1`, `#A1`, …). `seq` counts equal `(heading, row_key)` rows from the oldest row of the heading group up, so it is stable when newer rows appear at the top.
  - **Headings**: `clean_heading` applies the week-label digit fixes (T/I/L→1, O→0); `heading_ordinal` = `(year, phase, week/stage)` with preseason < season < post season < offseason (offseason rows carry the year of the season just finished, JR 2026-10-19; offseason stages share one ordinal, so a `--since` in the offseason re-reads the offseason part of the list; in season the list only goes back to the season's start); `heading_week` gives games.week (postseason via `capture_matching.db_week`).
- **Load** (`pipeline/load_transactions.py`, called by `pipeline/load_capture.py` in its one DB transaction): rows are inserted `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; effects run only for rows inserted now, oldest first, so a reload changes nothing. Checks (any stops the load, nothing written): a heading year after the season, an unreadable heading, a parsed team missing from `teams`, a heading group with fewer rows than already stored (the scan missed rows). A `--since` scan that didn't reach the list's end drops rows older than `since` (its oldest group is partial; they were loaded before). Rows that aren't roster moves (`is_move`: no move verb and no `(POS) NAME`, e.g. the draft-class notice) are skipped, not stored, and listed in the report's `skipped_not_moves` (JR, 2026-10-19). Rows before `<season> SEASON - WEEK 1` are skipped, not stored (`skipped_before_week_1`; JR, 2026-10-19: the Week 1 roster already has every preseason move); the season's own offseason rows order later and are kept. With `--apply-from`, rows between Week 1 and that heading are stored `applied = false` with no effects.
  - **Effects** (players matched by full name, list name and position; several hits = ambiguous: `player_id` NULL, flag `ambiguous`, no move): `SIGNED` → resolve among this season's released players, then this season's rows, then players with no row this season (full name + position); none → create `players` (`player_name` "D. Taylor", `full_name` "Devonta Taylor") + `player_seasons`; `team_id` = new team, `released_week` NULL; with `player_contracts` present, an old ACTIVE row → SUPERSEDED and a `source = 'TRANSACTION'` ACTIVE row (length = years remaining = N, `total_value` = parse_money(amount), bonus/savings/penalty NULL, cap hits total/N for N years then 0, at least five year rows). `RELEASED` → `released_week` = the week (preseason 0, offseason 24), `team_id` kept; ACTIVE contract → RELEASED. Trade rows are skipped and listed in `skipped_trades` (JR, 2026-10-19: the GM agents' approval flow, step 3, moves the players and contracts when JR confirms a trade). `RESTRUCTURED` ("TEAM HAVE RESTRUCTURED THE CONTRACT OF (POS) NAME GENERATING $X IN ADDITIONAL CAP SPACE") → this season's cap hit − X, the deal's later years + X spread evenly (`projected`), team `salaries` − X. Practice-squad signings ("SIGN (POS) NAME TO THEIR PRACTICE SQUAD", flag `practice_squad`) move the player with no contract; "SIGNED FROM OTHER TEAM PRACTICE SQUAD …" is a normal signing. Releases and re-signs also match a player with no row this season by full name + position. Moves for a player with no ACTIVE contract are listed in `no_contract`. `RE_SIGNED` / `EXTENDED` → this season unchanged (D8): the ACTIVE contract gets `extension_years`, `extension_value`, `extended_transaction_id`, and `projected = true` year rows for the new deal, starting `season + years_remaining` (the season after the current deal ends), hits = amount × `year_shares(N)`. No Team Salary screen is re-scanned automatically (JR, 2026-10-19). Report: `transactions_seen`, `transactions_new`, `unparsed`, `ambiguous`, `created_players`, `not_applied`, `older_seasons`, `contract_ops`, `cap_deltas`, `no_contract`, `cap_rows_missing`, `apply_from`, `skipped_not_moves`, `skipped_trades`, `skipped_before_week_1`.
  - **Cap deltas** (`team_cap_seasons` for the season; a team with no row is listed in `cap_rows_missing`): a signing adds its season cap hit to `salaries`; a release removes the hit from `salaries` and adds the stored `cut_penalty` (NULL = 0) to `cap_penalty` as dead money. `total_spend` moves by the sum and `cap_space` by minus it, and the row gets `derived_at` / `derived_transaction_id` (derived when `derived_at` is later than `scanned_at`). The next Season scan overwrites everything.
  - **Re-signing shares** (`average_shares` / `year_shares` / `projected_hits`, pure): from the latest Season scan's ACTIVE `SCAN` contracts whose every year is visible (`years_remaining = length ≤ 5`), each length's average share of the total per year; fewer than 10 contracts of a length (or none) → an even split; rounding goes to the last year so the hits sum to the amount.
  - `load_roster` clears `released_week` for every player on a scanned roster (the roster scan stays authoritative).
- Columns on step 1's tables (same migration): `player_contracts.extension_years`, `extension_value` (bigint), `extended_transaction_id` (FK `transactions` ON DELETE SET NULL); `player_contract_years.projected` (bool, default false: a re-signing's projected hit, not read off the screen); `team_cap_seasons.derived_at` (timestamptz), `derived_transaction_id` (FK `transactions` ON DELETE SET NULL).
- `player_seasons.released_week` (§3.1).

### 3.3f Front Office: GM agents (`db/migrations/2026_12_10_gm_agents.sql`, `docs/team_management/03_GM_AGENTS_PLAN.md`)
Mu's GM agents decide trades (master plan D2): they propose, JR approves and makes them by hand in Madden, then confirms on `#debug`. Madden's CPU keeps signings and releases (D1).
- **`gm_profiles`**: one row per GM tenure (the `coach_tenures` shape): `id`, `team_id`, `gm_name`, `bio` (short, invented), `voice` (style notes for the rationale writer), `bio_source` (`LLM` | `TEMPLATE`), `start_season` (invented, ≥ 2030), `end_season` (NULL = current), `end_reason`, traits `risk_appetite`, `aggressiveness` (0–1), `pick_affinity` (0.6–1.4), `youth_bias` (−1 veterans … +1 youth), `position_bias` JSONB (`{group: multiplier}`, ≤ 3 groups, each within ±15%), `seed`, `created_at`. Partial unique index: one open tenure per team. Rows are never regenerated; replacing a GM closes the tenure and seeds a new row.
  - **Seeding** (`engines/gm_personas.py`): traits are deterministic (`random.Random(sha256("gm:" + abbr))`) and stratified across the league (each trait's 32 strata dealt out by a seeded permutation; aggressiveness spans 0.12–0.88, so 9–10 GMs sit at ≥ 0.65 and 9–10 at ≤ 0.35; the plan's bound is 6–10). A replacement GM (`--replace`, seed `gm:<abbr>:<season>`) is redrawn until both bands still hold. Name, bio and voice: one Gemini call per GM (`engines/gm_llm.py`, label `gm_persona`) validated by `parse_persona` (2–3-word invented name not already used or the head coach's; no number but the start year; no claims about picks or trades), else the seeded name-pool template (last names unique across the league).
- **`gm_team_settings`**: `team_id` PK, `user_controlled` (JR runs the team in Madden: its GM only advises), `suggestions_on` (JR can switch an advisory GM's suggestions off per team), `updated_at`. The migration seeds CAR and IND as user-controlled (JR, 2026-10-20). No row = CPU-run, suggestions on.
- **`gm_team_seasons`**: PK `(team_id, season)`, `gm_id`, `direction` (`CONTEND` | `RETOOL` | `REBUILD`), `direction_score`, `direction_week`, `direction_inputs`, `needs` (ranked JSON), `needs_week`, `surplus`, `urgent_need` (injury trigger), step 4's `fo_quality` (NUMERIC(4,2), default 1.00, CHECK 0.5–1.6; step 3 never writes it after the default), `fo_quality_inputs`, `fo_quality_at`, `hot_seat_score`, `hot_seat_inputs`, `updated_at`. Seeding creates the season's rows.
- **View `vw_gm_profiles`**: the open tenure per team with `league_state.current_season`'s `gm_team_seasons` row and `gm_team_settings` (`user_controlled`, `suggestions_on`): master §2.4's single "GM profile".
- **`draft_picks`**: PK `(draft_season, round, original_team_id)`, `owner_team_id`, `overall_pick` (step 4 fills it), `source` (`SEED` | `TRADE` | `JR`), `acquired_proposal_id` (FK `gm_trade_proposals`), `used`.
