   - **Times** are the simulated `posted_at`: relative ("12m", "5h") against the feed's newest post (`as_of`), else the date ("Dec 31"); the post page shows the full time, date and views.
   - Loading, error ("Agent Wire couldn't load…") and empty states on every view.
5. **Social NFL Container (`SocialNflWidget`)**:
   - Embedded in the Agent Wire right rail.
   - Replaced generic CSS text shield with the official ESPN NFL shield logo.
   - Displays the top 3 most exciting matchups of the active week ranked by Excitement Index ($E$).
6. **Team Hub Beat Coverage & Article Feed (`TeamPage`)**:
   - Queries `GET /api/team/{abbr}/news?season={season}` to populate the team spotlight hero card and a horizontally scrollable feed displaying all beat reports and national articles covering the franchise across the season, strictly excluding opposing teams' beat reporters, with seamless fallback to positional template beats.
7. **Live Newsroom Front Page (`App.tsx`)**:
   - Fetches `GET /api/news/week/{week}` to bind the Hero Slot story, secondary analysis cards, feature trios, and trending local coverage primers directly to autonomous newsroom outputs. The sidebar Power Rankings card shows the top 3 from `GET /api/power_rankings` (latest stored week), or a placeholder when none is stored.
8. **Team & League Logos**:
   - Pulls high-resolution official SVG/PNG logos from ESPN's global CDN (`https://a.espncdn.com/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/${abbr}.png`), normalized across all 32 franchises.
9. **Napper Interactive Multi-League Experience (`NapperPage`, `src/components/napper/`)**:
   - Sleeper-inspired dark theme interface (`src/napper.css`: `#121926` background, `#1a2332` panels, `#00ceb8` cyan accents) featuring a 3-panel layout:
     - **Left Rail**: Single "Back to Home" button above direct navigation, league switcher with user/opponent live scores, league records, and user profile card (`@jrereyi2000`, 3 trophies, pending trades badge).
   - **Center Stage**: 5 interactive tabs:
       - `MATCHUP`: After a player's game is final, his card shows the result line and box score under his name instead of kickoff/opponent (`PostGameLine`, starters and bench; also on the `TEAM` tab), and his projection turns teal when he beat it, red when he fell short (`projClass`). Split head-to-head comparison from the selected league's saved rosters, with slots derived from `roster_format`, actual weekly points, active-season projections, NFL opponent/kickoff/status, season records, team points share (projections before games begin, actual points once scoring starts), and configured bench slots. Standard leagues omit `SUPERFLEX`. Selecting a player opens the same hero and sticky anchor tabs as the Mu Fantasy Analytics player modal with continuous Game Logs, Season Stats, and Outlook sections (no Latest News tab/section), recolored for Napper and populated by `GET /api/player/{player_id}`.
       - `TEAM`: The active user's actual league roster with player-specific headshots, game details, and weekly points, without projected-point labels or a lineup optimizer. Starters, bench, then an Injured Reserve section (empty IR slots included; a healthy player left in IR is labeled "Healthy"). An amber banner lists starters who won't score that week (injured or on bye) and suggests a swap or IR move; the lineup is never changed automatically. Click-to-swap starter/bench/IR assignments use atomic server validation (IR moves only for IR-eligible players); a settings icon next to the team name opens a persisted rename dialog.
       - `PLAYERS`: Available current-week free agents with player-specific headshots, actual opponent/kickoff details, stored weekly projections, season averages, search, and Add/Drop claims. Selecting a player opens the same Mu Fantasy Analytics modal structure and real player detail data, styled with Napper's dark navy and teal palette.
       - `LEAGUE`: Compact weekly scoreboard cards styled to match the Matchup banner's navy gradient, muted metadata, bold team typography, and teal points-share gauge, with team records, projections, and actual starter scores. Clicking a card opens its full starter/bench comparison in a modal using the Matchup view. Standings calculate PF/PA from season starter points, W-L-T from completed matchups, and seed by record percentage then PF. Includes the playoff bracket from `GET /playoffs`: quarterfinals (week 15) with the seeds 1–2 byes, semifinals (16) and championship (17) with seeds, scores (projections before kickoff), winners highlighted, the champion, and a status pill ("Projected · if the season ended today" until week 14 is final). Scoreboard cards in playoff weeks carry a round tag with both seeds; an empty playoff week shows the server's note. The Matchup tab keeps a week picker under its empty state, so a bye or eliminated week can be left. The separate Scores tab is removed. Manager strategy controls appear only before the draft.
       - `TRADES`: Active trade market, incoming AI proposals with rationale breakdown, and proposal creation modal. The header shows the trade deadline ("end of Week 11", amber; red once passed), and the propose button is disabled after it.
       - `CHAT`: Full-screen league stream tab on narrower viewports.
     - **Right Rail**: Autonomous League Chat and awards stream featuring automated bot cards ("RB/WR/TE Benchwarmer of the Week", "High Scorer of the Week", "Trade Alert", "Waiver Claim") alongside contextual banter from AI managers across 4 distinct archetypes (`TRASH_TALKER`, `ANALYTICS_NERD`, `WAIVER_HAWK`, `DIE_HARD`), plus interactive user chat input.

## 6. The Automated Pipeline Workflow (End-to-End)

### 6.0 Weekly run (capture pipeline, `docs/PIPELINE_MIGRATION.md` phase 5)
The capture pipeline runs alongside the old stream ingestion below until phase 6 retires that.
1. **Start** (JR, Oct 14: on demand; the broadcast trigger that watched for Advance Week was too brittle and was removed): after Advance Week, JR taps a button on the `#debug` page (phone or Mac). JR knows the run is going because his controller disconnects when Remote Play takes the console. Buttons: **Scan week** (the full run below), **Backfill previous week** (the same, `--no-articles`), **Game scan** (`--mode games`: every game of last week, box scores + player stats loaded with `--replace` over the backfill's lines, ~7 min a game, never articles), **Season scan** (`--mode season --no-articles`: start-of-season cadence run capturing the 18-week schedule, all 32 team rosters, all 32 Team Salary screens (contracts, right after rosters), the 32 team overalls and rankings cards, the 32-team injury report, and the draft board line-by-line in that exact order with prospects after schedule, rosters, contracts, team overalls, and injuries; capture timeout 2 × 75 min + 150 min for contracts; automatically rolls over `league_state` to `(current_season + 1, 1)` if `current_week != 1`; extracts, loads schedule, players, contracts (after the roster load; stops unless TEAM SALARY's year is the new season), team overalls/ratings into `team_seasons`, prospects, and Week 1 injuries; runs preseason calibration engines: 100-league simulated ADP drafts, scheduled game excitement projections, and Week 1 fantasy projections, then Napper week 1 AI lineups (`run_napper_week(season, 1, trades=False)`; a failure only logs a warning); publishes Week 1 preseason magazine unless `--no-articles`, shrinks media), **Team overalls scan** (`--mode team-overalls --no-articles`: captures all 32 team overalls and rankings cards from Options > User Teams > Triangle into `team_overalls.csv`, loading `team_ovr`, `off_ovr`, `def_ovr`, and ranks into `team_seasons`, no articles), **Roster scan** (`--mode roster --no-articles`: captures all 32 team rosters, extracting cards, loading players, shrinking media, no articles), **Contract scan** (`--mode contracts --no-articles`: every team's Team Salary screen, extracted and loaded into `player_contracts` / `team_cap_seasons` (§3.3d), ~40 min, no articles; a by-hand `--teams CAR,NO` re-scans only those teams), **Draft prospect scan** (`--mode prospects --no-articles`: captures the scouting draft board line-by-line, extracts prospects via Apple Vision OCR, loads them into `draft_prospects`, shrinks media, no articles; normally part of the start-of-season cadence after schedule and rosters); each can resume a stopped run folder.
2. **Weekly run** (`pipeline/weekly_capture.py`, a job of `server/debug_api.py` or run by hand; one at a time via `captures/.weekly_run.lock`, log in `captures/<run>/weekly_run.log`): notice → `madden_capture.py --connect` → `madden_capture.py --out <run>` (Season Stats, Team Stats, last week's schedule, and the Injury Report; from Week 19 a playoff run instead: `--only playoff`, last round's schedule, the injury report, and every game's box score and lines, ~7 min a game, plus the coming round's matchups; Week 22 is an articles-only run for the Week 23 slate; at Week 23, after playing the Super Bowl, Scan week captures the game directly from the in-game postgame stats screen via `madden_capture.py --only postgame` without advancing Madden to the offseason or navigating backward through the schedule) → League Transactions (the weekly/playoff capture reads the list from `--since` = the newest heading loaded this season, `<season> SEASON - WEEK 1` if none; optional: a failure only logs) → notice to turn the controller back on and restart the broadcast → `extract.py` (Gemini vision for tables + on-device Apple Vision OCR for injuries and League Transactions (`transactions.csv`, flagged rows re-read by Gemini one cropped row at a time); during postseason weeks, injury extraction filters screenshots to only the teams playing games that week) → `pipeline.load_capture` (loads games, then `transactions.csv` (League Transactions, §3.3e) before Season Stats so a moved player's line finds his new team, then injuries; in postseason weeks, `load_injuries` validates against `games` to only commit injuries for teams playing that week; maps postgame stat labels and formats into standard box score fields; `extract.py` `postgame_columns` renames the postgame screen's labels (PLAYER→NAME, YDS→YARDS, passing AVG→YPA, defense ASSIST/TOTAL/PDEF/FF/FR/FYDS/BLOCKS/TDS, kicking LONG/BLOCKS/XP BLK, punting BLOCKS/IN 20) to the box score's via `POSTGAME_RENAME`, and writes box-score columns it lacks (passing SACKS) blank via `POSTGAME_MISSING`) → `generate_team_and_player_stats(season, strict=True)` (an engine error raises) → `newsroom.weekly_run.run_weekly_swarm` → shrink (PNG → WebP q90 full resolution, `stop_debug` / `scope_debug` / `week_debug` / `team_debug` / `probe` / `explore` deleted, CSVs kept; ~1.7 GB → ~46 MB) → notice. Any failed step stops the week (nothing after it runs, so a failed load means no backfill, engines or articles) and sends an urgent notice with the `--resume` command. Resume starts at the first step not done: capture resumes and preserves completed categories (`stats/done.txt`), completed teams (`roster/done.txt`, `injuries/`), finished games (`games/<NN>/DONE`), completed Team Salary teams (`contracts/done.txt`, `contracts/targets.txt`), and completed season scan phases (`season_schedule`, `roster`, `contracts`, `team_overalls`, `injuries`, `prospects`) while discarding only partial frames of incomplete categories/teams; capture is done when the run wrote `.capture_done` (older weekly runs: `week.json`), extract when the mode's CSVs exist (weekly: `schedule.csv` + `stats_*.csv` + `injuries.csv` if `injuries/` present; playoff/postgame: `game_scores.csv` + `schedule.csv` if `schedule/` present + `injuries.csv` if `injuries/` present; games: `game_scores.csv`; contracts: `contracts.csv` + `team_cap.csv`, also required in a season run that captured `contracts/`), load when the `captures` row is `loaded`; the backfill and articles are safe to rerun.

### 6.1 Stream ingestion (retired in phase 6)
The entire intelligence apparatus is triggered autonomously via an optimized, concurrent pipeline designed to ingest streaming video and output localized AI content in real time.

1. **Ingestion (Live Stream & Worker Pool Concurrency):**
   - `pipeline/ocr_extractor.py` and `TemplateClassifier` monitor the Madden video stream at 1 FPS.
   - Dynamic batch jobs and single-frame stats are queued to an asynchronous **`ApiWorkerPool`** (default 3 concurrent workers, scalable via `--workers`).
   - Multiple extraction jobs run simultaneously against Gemini (`gemini-3.8-flash`), with thread-safe synchronization of game IDs, session state, and real-time database UPSERTs.
   - When the user scrolls Post-Game stats, box scores are instantly UPSERTED into `player_stats`.
   - When the user scrolls the Weekly Schedule, it logs final CPU-simmed scores and win/loss records.
   - When the user scrolls Season Stats, it overwrites the `season_stats` ledger.
2. **Mathematical Backfill:**
   - `engines/game_stat_generator.py` (regular-season games only: playoff games get lines only from a games capture, and captured playoff lines are never subtracted from the regular-season totals) is invoked for `league_state.current_season` (no season is hardcoded in the backfill or the pipeline's calls). It subtracts the player's lines already stored this season (matched by `player_id`, on any team, so a traded player's old-team games count; a `season_stats` row without one falls back to name + team + position) from the `season_stats` and distributes the remaining stats into the missing CPU-simmed games, so season totals match `season_stats`. Each player's remainder is split across those games in proportion to each game's combined score (weight $\max(1, \text{total points}/40)$), so per-game box scores for CPU-simmed games are synthetic: only season totals and final scores are real (see the caveat in 2.6). The exception is a team with exactly one missing game, the normal weekly case: its lines are that week's exact change in the season totals and are tagged `line_source = 'WEEKLY'`; lines spread over several games are tagged `DISTRIBUTED` (§2.13 uses only real lines). It inserts with the line's `player_id` and skips a row that hits either unique key. Team rows are the sums of those player lines, with third/fourth-down fields left NULL; right after the insert it runs `engines/team_box_estimator.run(season)` (§2.6), which fills every still-NULL per-game conversion, red-zone and penalty cell from the `team_seasons` remainder (an estimator failure stops a strict run).
   - In addition to offensive skill positions, `generate_defense_and_special_teams_stats(season_year, games_df)` backfills per-game lines for kicking (`game_stat_kicking`), defense (`game_stat_defense`), punting (`game_stat_punting`), blocking (`game_stat_blocking`), punt returns (`game_stat_puntreturn`), and kick returns (`game_stat_kickreturn`) from their respective `season_stat_*` tables for completed games missing game lines. In a weekly run where a team has 1 missing game, the game line equals that week's exact change in season totals. Multi-game distributions use domain-specific weights (kicking: points scored, defense: defensive stop score $\max(1, (50 - \text{pts\_allowed})/10)$, punting: lower points $\max(1, (40 - \text{pts})/10)$, blocking: even, returns: opponent score/punts), with sacks allocated in 0.5 half-sack steps and rate metrics (`fg_pct`, `xp_pct`, `average`, `net_average`, `tackles = solo + assists`) computed automatically. CLI flag `--no-dst` allows skipping defense and special teams backfill if desired.
3. **Culture Engines (Dependency-Ordered Execution & Batch Updates):**
   - Appended to the end of the backfill generator, `engines/game_stat_generator.py` first runs `gotw_engine.batch_evaluate(season, only_missing=True, game_ids=...)` to score the Game Excitement Index ($E$) for newly completed games (those with no `excitement_index`) and any games it just backfilled. Previously scored games are left unchanged. This must precede TWI, which only counts games with a non-null `excitement_index`.
   - Right after $E$ it runs `game_script_engine.run(season)` (§2.14): every completed game without a script, or whose recorded facts changed, gets its canonical story in `game_scripts` (the story's drama follows $E$).
   - It then runs `TWIEngine().backfill_twi()` followed by `PopularityEngine().calculate_popularity()`, sequentially in dependency order, because franchise popularity reads the `watchability_index` that TWI writes. These two used to run in parallel, so popularity could read stale or partially updated TWI.
   - Between TWI and franchise popularity it runs `GameCreditEngine().calculate(season)` (Section 2.4) `TeamEfficiencyEngine().calculate(season)` (Section 2.6), `TendencyEngine().calculate(season)` (Section 2.12), `CotyEngine().calculate(season)` (Section 2.11) and `PlayoffCloutEngine().calculate(season)` (Section 2.7). They read only `games` / `team_stats` / `player_stats` / `awards` (and, for COTY, `team_seasons.team_ovr` and the coach tables), so their position is not a dependency.
   - Once franchise metrics are computed, `PlayerPopularityEngine().calculate_popularity()` executes vectorized batch updates using PostgreSQL `executemany` (reducing DB execution time from ~1s to ~0.16s).
   - `RecordsEngine().calculate(season)` (`engines/records_engine.py`) evaluates the season against the record book and stores newly broken or tied franchise records and NFL all-time top 10 entries into `franchise_records` and `league_record_entries` (source `LEAGUE`, §2.9).
   - Then `project_scheduled_games(season)` refreshes `games.projected_excitement` for every still-scheduled game, so the next assignment brief and primer picks see current projections.
   - Then `project_upcoming_week(season)` (`engines/fantasy_projection_engine.py`) writes fantasy projections for the first week that still has scheduled games. It reads only `games`, `player_seasons`, `player_stats`, `game_stat_kicking`, `game_stat_defense` and `injuries`, so it has no dependency on the culture engines.
   - Finally, `simulate_season(season)` (`engines/fantasy_league_sim.py`) re-simulates the 100 fantasy leagues through that week (rostered %, start %, standings). It needs the season's stored draft (from `fantasy_adp_engine`) and skips with a message if there is none.
   - Then `run_napper_week(season)` (`engines/napper_weekly.py`) updates every IN_SEASON Napper league of the season for that same week: the playoff bracket (`sync_playoffs`: the round for that week, the champion after week 17), AI lineups (IR moves, starters), the waiver run from week 2 (`engines/napper_waiver_engine.run_waivers`: the user's queued claims and AI pickups, once per league and week), AI trade proposals (skipped if the league already has AI proposals that week, so re-runs add no duplicate offers or banter), decisions on pending offers to AI teams (both only through the week 11 trade deadline; from week 12 pending offers expire instead), AI lineups again (waiver adds and traded players arrive on the bench), and `napper_leagues.current_week` = the week. Each league commits on its own; a Napper failure only logs `[WARN]` and never fails the stats sync. Offers to the human team stay pending.
4. **The Swarm Trigger & Editorial Commissioning:**
   - The swarm is a separate step from the stats sync. `game_stat_generator.py` only syncs stats and runs the engines; the video pipeline (`pipeline/main.py`, both live and file modes) calls `newsroom.weekly_run.run_weekly_swarm(season)` right after it on the same thread. The admin dashboard's `season_stats` edits and a direct `python3 -m engines.game_stat_generator` run no longer publish anything.
   - **Publication week:** a Week N slate is published before Week N kicks off (recaps Week N−1, previews Week N). `run_weekly_swarm` picks N = the latest *fully completed* week + 1, where fully completed means every non-`BYE` game that week is `COMPLETED`; Week 1 if none. A partly played week doesn't count, so a mid-week Season Stats capture maps to an already-published slate. After Week 21 it publishes Week 22; Week 23 (Super Bowl) and the Week 1 preseason slate must be run by hand with `--week`. Once the Super Bowl week is finished it publishes nothing.
   - **Once per week:** it skips the week if `news_feed` already has a national-slot article for it (`slot` = `Hero Slot`, `Standalone`, or `Slot N`; local wakeups use `Local Beat N` and don't count), unless `--force`. It holds a PostgreSQL advisory lock on (season, week) for the run, so two triggers can't generate the same week at once.
   - **Memory refresh:** in a live run, before the brief, `agent_memory.refresh_season_summaries` rewrites the "season so far" summary of every agent with at least 3 new takes since its last summary (§7.7).
   - `run_weekly_swarm` compiles a dual-horizon Weekly Slate Digest:
     - **`season` / `calendar_week` / `recap_week`**: labels for the publication week and the week being recapped.
     - **`recap_slate`**: Completed scoreboard from `week - 1` (score, margin, excitement, team IDs), most exciting first, for retrospective recaps. Each game carries `game_story` (§2.14: storyline, halftime, the winning score and two key moments from its game script).
     - **`standout_performances`**: The top 5 individual stat lines from `week - 1`, ranked by passing, rushing and receiving yards and touchdowns.
     - **`preview_slate`**: Upcoming scheduled matchups for `week`, ordered by projected excitement. `projected_excitement` comes from `games.projected_excitement` on the 70–100 display scale, for prospective previews.
     - **`excitement_scale_note`**: Tells writers that Excitement Index values are on the 70–100 scale (≈70 dull, ≈80 typical, 90+ classic) and that projections are rough pre-game estimates. `recap_slate` values and the writer DB tools (`get_weekly_slate`, `get_team_season_schedule`, box score and advanced analytics) use the same display scale.
     - **`season_landscape`**: The league as it stood entering the week. `division_standings` gives every division's teams, best record first, as one line each (`City Team (ID: n) W-L[-T], PF, PA, diff`), counted from completed **regular-season** games before the publication week by `newsroom/season_landscape.division_standings` (playoff games excluded; `standings_through_week` is `week - 1`, capped at 18; `tests/test_season_landscape.py`). `most_watchable_teams` and `most_popular_teams` are the top 5 by `team_seasons` watchability and popularity, labelled with the same standings records.
     - **`league_transactions`** (omitted in a week with no new moves; `roster_moves.brief_block`): the moves visible to this week's articles that last week's couldn't see (`new_this_week`), as `notable_moves` (at most 12 move lines, notable first, never an OVR), `counts` by type, `cap_watch` (teams under $2M in cap space, most squeezed first, each with its basis) and a `note` (use a move only when it bears on the subject, most articles carry none; terms only from these lines or the contract tools; invent colour, never a term). Built last, from `db_tools.fetch_moves` / `fetch_cap_rows` with the brief's (season, week).
     - **`curatorial_guidance`**: Tells writers they may choose their own games, teams and players, except that an `EDITORIAL DESK SUBJECT ASSIGNMENT` in the prompt fixes the subject; then the freedom applies only to angles within it.
     - The brief is identical for every article in a run (Agent Wire posts get their own facts packets, §7.8). (The former `slate_manifest`, a duplicate of `recap_slate`, and `league_leaders`, now inside `season_landscape`, were removed.)
   - It then fires `SwarmDispatcher().dispatch_weekly_national(live=True, save_to_db=True, max_workers=5)`, which commissions the full 10-slot Editorial Matrix slate (`docs/EDITORIAL_MATRIX.md`) via the autonomous Editorial Assignment Desk. The optional `article_count` argument truncates the slate and is for tests/debugging only.
   - Local on-demand team wakeups (`POST /api/news/team/{team_id}/wakeup`; only `live=true` calls Gemini and saves, `live=false` is a dry run) also utilize `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=5)` to generate the team's local beat portfolio, then (live, saved wakeups only) the team's Agent Wire posts through `newsroom/social_publish.run_team_social`, which also shares each new local article from its reporter (§7.8). The portfolio is planned by `newsroom/local_portfolio.plan_local_portfolio` (see §7.2).
   - The Editorial Desk commissions a structured 10-article matrix (Hero, Slot 1, Slot 2, Slots 3–5 Feature Trio, Slots 6–8 Local Matchup Primers, and Standalone Power Rankings), matches each format to an eligible national writer or beat reporter, and dispatches them with custom curatorial directives and explicit temporal framing (`RETROSPECTIVE`, `PROSPECTIVE`, or `CUMULATIVE`). Other local beat formats (`LOCAL_POSTGAME_REPORT_CARD`, `LOCAL_PLAYER_SPOTLIGHT`, `LOCAL_SEASON_PROGRESS_REPORT`) are not part of the weekly national run; they are produced only by on-demand team wakeups.
   - **Only real output is saved:** `_execute_agent` writes to `news_feed` only when `live=True` and the model returned text. A missing API key, a Gemini exception, an empty response, or offline mock text (`live=False`) is logged as `[NOT SAVED]` and returned with an `error` field instead. A weekly run where every article failed therefore leaves the week unpublished, and the next trigger retries it (`tests/test_article_saving.py`).
   - **Agent Wire posts:** the old reaction tweets (random pundits and fans prompted with the full brief) are gone. After the national and fantasy slates, `run_weekly_swarm` calls `newsroom.social_publish.run_social_week`, which plans and publishes the week's ~100 Agent Wire posts; team wakeups post through `run_team_social` (§7.8). `--social-only` / `--skip-social` run or skip that step; `--fantasy-only` skips it; `--skip-fantasy` keeps it. The result dict gains a `social` status.
   - **Parallel Multithreaded Execution:** Dispatcher leverages Python `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (default 5 workers), collapsing total weekly generation time from ~90s sequential down to ~12–15s parallel.
   - **API Connection Pooling:** FastAPI backend (`server/api.py`) utilizes `psycopg_pool.ConnectionPool` (min 4, max 20 connections) with sub-3ms query latency across all REST endpoints.
   - **Persistence & Entity Tagging:** Articles are parsed for creative headlines (`# Headline`) and teaser excerpts (`> **EXCERPT:** ...`), then saved to `news_feed` with explicit `article_type` and `slot` identifiers alongside relational tags in `article_teams` and `article_players`.

## 7. Media Swarm Prompting, Curatorial Autonomy & Historical Anchoring
To eliminate editorial homogenization and ground long-form journalism in authentic football culture, prompts for the National Writers chain strict analytical constraints:
- **Core Model Standard:** All autonomous writer and reaction agents utilize Google's **`gemini-3.8-flash`** with autonomous ReAct database function calling (`automatic_function_calling` with a 50-call cap).
- **Mandatory Headline & Excerpt Directives:** Prompts enforce strict extraction markers:
  ```markdown
  # [Your Creative, Engaging Headline]
  > **EXCERPT:** [A sharp, 1-to-2 sentence summary / teaser for the front page]
  ```
  Both fields are parsed upon generation and stored directly in `news_feed(headline, excerpt)`.
- **Temporal Publishing Alignment (`TEMPORAL_ARTICLE_MODES`):**
  - **`RETROSPECTIVE`** (`MACRO_SLATE_RECAP`, `SINGLE_GAME_SCHEMATIC_AUTOPSY`, `LOCAL_POSTGAME_REPORT_CARD`, `PLAYOFF_AUTOPSY`): Publishes during calendar `week` evaluating the concluded action from `week - 1`.
  - **`PROSPECTIVE`** (`SLATE_LOOKAHEAD_PREDICTIONS`, `LOCAL_MATCHUP_PRIMER`, Super Bowl Previews): Previews upcoming scheduled matchups for `week`.
  - **`CUMULATIVE`** (`POWER_RANKINGS`, `AWARDS_TRACKER`, `PLAYER_DEEP_PROFILE`, `FRANCHISE_STATE_OF_THE_UNION`, etc.): Evaluates hierarchy entering `week` based on cumulative results through `week - 1`.
- **Curatorial Autonomy & Slate Digest (`newsroom/config/curatorial_autonomy_directives.json`):** Writers are fed both full scoreboards (last week's results and this week's schedule) and the division standings in the brief's `season_landscape`, rather than a narrow pre-filtered Top 5 list. Subject selection is layered: the article type's own curatorial autonomy block (`article_types.json`, or a playoff override's) comes first, and the writer's directive from `curatorial_autonomy_directives.json` is appended as "YOUR SPECIFIC WRITER SELECTION FILTER". If only one exists, it is used alone. An Editorial Desk subject assignment (§7.3) overrides both. Thirteen writers have a directive:
  - *Barnwell:* regression candidates: lucky one-score winners, poor point differentials, favorites exposed by upsets.
  - *Sando:* front-office pressure: aging, mediocre rosters (GM job security) or young blue-chip rosters (coaching development).
  - *Joyner:* schematic complexity and play-level efficiency: extreme tempo, boom-or-bust performances, tactical mismatches, or a defense that did the breaking (sacks, tackles for loss, takeaways from the box score's defensive lines and `get_defense_special_teams_leaders`; his data table charts a defense's recorded numbers, never pressure rates, snaps or targets).
  - *Rosenthal:* entertainment and vibes: high-tempo, high-excitement chaos and electric young quarterbacks.
  - *Chadiha:* the human element and historical stakes: aging stars, adversity, historic franchises under pressure.
  - *Yates:* statistical anomalies, milestones and fantasy volume that support factual lists.
  - *Simmons:* the "wonkiest" game: upsets, bad beats, historic franchises over- or under-achieving the eye test.
  - *Acho:* exposed flaws and public overreactions: popular favorites that lost, high-tempo offenses shut down (shown in the box score's defensive lines and the defenders' season numbers; his column format names those plus `get_team_efficiency_profile` as the defensive and situational evidence).
  - *The Athletic:* process, scheme and roster trajectory; young-core surges and schedule-adjusted winners; avoids garbage-time inflation.
  - *Skip Bayless:* superstar drama and the "clutch gene": prime-time chokes or game-winning drives by marquee players.
  - *Kimes:* games where efficiency tells a different story than the score, and smart defensive schemes, evidenced by the box score's defensive lines and `get_defense_special_teams_leaders` (coverage rotations and pressures are her read of them; her column format credits unheralded defenders with their recorded numbers).
  - *Orlovsky:* the most extreme quarterback performances only, good or bad.
  - *Clark:* locker-room dynamics, player sacrifice and leadership under pressure.
- **Historical Comparisons (optional, 1990–2023):** National writers may compare a team or player to a real NFL team or season from 1990–2023 when the parallel genuinely sharpens the argument. It isn't required; the prompt says most articles need none, allows at most one, forbids inventing a historical team, and gives no canned examples (the old ones, e.g. the 2000 Ravens, got reused everywhere). Beat reporters don't receive this instruction.
- **Invented detail vs. data (JR, Oct 15: invent freely what the data can't contradict):** Scores, records, stat lines, injury statuses and rankings must come from the brief or the tools. How each game unfolded comes from its game script (§2.14), which the shared `natural_language_rule` block calls canon (`GAME SCRIPTS ARE CANON`): quarter scores, scoring plays and their order, scorers, clock, lead changes, the winning score, key moments, adjustments and defensive standouts are shared by every writer and never contradicted. Beyond the script writers invent the texture no data records (snaps between the scores, play calls, pressure and coverage described in words, blocks, drops, sideline scenes, crowd, quotes), plus anonymous sources such as Sando's "NFC executive" quotes; a defender with no recorded line for a game is praised in words, not numbers. Betting lines and non-existent injury speculation are never used: prompts forbid citing unverified medical timetables or inventing injuries. **Contracts and moves** (JR's R1/R13, `docs/EDITORIAL_MATRIX.md` *Roster Moves & Contracts*): that a move happened (who signed, released, re-signed or restructured whom, which team, which week), its terms (years, total, this season's cap hit, bonus, cut savings, dead money, no-trade clause), team cap space, spend and dead money, rosters and free-agent status, and the year a contract runs through come only from the contract tools or the brief and are never contradicted; they appear only when they matter to the article's subject (most articles carry no cap numbers). A projected yearly figure (`est.`) is hedged in prose. Never invented even though untracked: guaranteed money, incentive details, option years. Free to invent: negotiation colour, anonymous front-office or agent quotes, motives, locker-room reaction, depth-chart meaning, a reason for a cut that doesn't contradict data (no invented injury, no "cut for cap space" when the team has room unless framed as opinion), and unnamed, unpriced interest from other teams. A trade is done only when executed; anything else is a rumor. `article_structure_directives.json`'s "do not invent … contract details" stays as the policy (R11). Official weekly injuries from the league's capture are integrated across newsroom tiers: national writers cover marquee injuries ($\ge 88$ OVR, $\ge 50$ Pop, starting QBs), local beat reporters cover all injured starters, fantasy analysts cover skill starters and handcuffs, and Agent Wire covers beat reports and fan shock. Madden ratings are never cited: writers may use OVR and team ratings from their tools to judge talent, but the shared `natural_language_rule` block (appended to every article and tweet prompt) forbids rating numbers, "OVR"/"overall" wording and any mention of Madden or the video game in published text.
- **Regular season vs. playoffs rule:** both research blocks say season numbers (player season stats, the defense and special-teams leaders, team situational rates, standings) are the regular season only, playoff numbers come only from playoff box scores and `postseason_stats`, and the two are never added together. `tests/test_writer_config.py` checks both blocks.
- **Team situational rule:** the national `DEFENSIVE DATA` block and the beat research block also say season third-down, red-zone and penalty rates come from `get_team_efficiency_profile` and a game's only from its `team_situational` box-score line (none = don't invent the game's totals), that third-down defense has no season total, and that down-and-distance splits aren't recorded. The directives that ask for situational football point at those tools: the regular-season and postseason lookahead Stat to Know, the matchup primer (its Defensive Mandate may be a red-zone target against the opponent's red-zone TD rate), the schematic autopsy and its Weeks 20–22 override, the postgame report card, the season progress report, the contender blueprint, the playoff autopsy, the QB duel (its blitz-conversion line is replaced by team rates plus the QBs' interceptions and sacks taken), the Super Bowl comprehensive preview, masterclass and Tale of the Tape. `tests/test_writer_config.py` checks them.
- **Player splits rule:** both research blocks list `get_player_scheme_splits` and say to cite a split with its game count, as a tendency, and to leave splits out when its `verdict` says there aren't enough real games. Seven article types end their `curatorial_autonomy` with a `SPLITS DATA:` line: `LOCAL_PLAYER_SPOTLIGHT`, `PLAYER_PROGRESS_REPORT`, `QUARTERBACK_DUEL_TAPE_BREAKDOWN`, `LOCAL_MATCHUP_PRIMER`, `FANTASY_START_SIT`, `FANTASY_MATCHUP_EXPLOITS` and `FANTASY_PLAYOFF_STREAMERS` (their `data_queried` list the tool; fantasy types by tool name). `tests/test_writer_config.py` checks the registration and hookups.
- **Coaching tendencies rule:** both research blocks (`COACHING TENDENCIES:`; the beat block also tells reporters to query their coach and, before a game, the opponent's) say the schemes and Low / Medium / High tendencies from `get_coach_profile` and `get_matchup_edges` frame scheme talk. Pass lean and fourth down are measured in this league (§2.12) and can be stated as what the team does; man coverage is the staff's real-world philosophy and is presented as reputation, never as what the defense has done; there is no blitz data, so writers never put a number on how often a defense blitzes or call it a season-long habit. Never a percentage, never a count or rate of how often a team blitzed or played man in a game; describing in words what a defense did in one game is fine when it fits the game script. Thirteen article types end their `curatorial_autonomy` with a `COACHING DATA:` line naming the tool and what to use it for: `PLAYOFF_AUTOPSY` (seat temperature), `COACHING_HOT_SEAT_AUDIT` (every eliminated coach it sorts), `FRANCHISE_STATE_OF_THE_UNION`, `LOCAL_SEASON_PROGRESS_REPORT`, `SEASON_PROGRESS_AUDIT`, `SINGLE_GAME_SCHEMATIC_AUTOPSY`, `QUARTERBACK_DUEL_TAPE_BREAKDOWN`, `SUPER_BOWL_COMPREHENSIVE_PREVIEW`, `SLATE_LOOKAHEAD_PREDICTIONS`, `LOCAL_MATCHUP_PRIMER`, `LOCAL_PLAYER_SPOTLIGHT`, `LOCAL_POSTGAME_REPORT_CARD` and `SUPER_BOWL_MEDIA_INTERVIEW_AUDIT` (their `data_queried` list the profile or the coaching matchup), as do the playoff overrides of the schematic autopsy and the slate lookahead. `tests/test_writer_config.py` checks the registration, both rule blocks and the hookups.
- **Defensive data rule:** the national writers' research block (`DEFENSIVE DATA`, between the tool list and the optional historical comparisons) and the beat reporters' research block tell writers that defense and special teams have real season numbers (and game lines where captured) and to back defensive claims with named players, with three limits: a player Madden doesn't list has unknown numbers, not zeros; listed defenders never sum to team sacks or takeaways; pressures, blitzes, coverage, snap counts and targets aren't recorded, so they are never given as numbers or rates, but may be described in words when it fits the box score and the game script (a pass rush that lived in the backfield, a corner who blanketed his man). `tests/test_writer_config.py` checks the tool is registered and the Week 19 directives point at it; `tests/test_defense_tools.py` covers the line formatting.
- **Cap and move data lines:** nine article types end their `curatorial_autonomy` with a `CAP DATA:` line naming the contract tool and what it's for, each with the relevance rule ("Only when it bears on the story: most … need one or two cap figures or none"), the basis and `est.` hedging: `FRANCHISE_STATE_OF_THE_UNION` (from Week 15 next season's space as the rebuild runway), `SEASON_PROGRESS_AUDIT`, `PLAYOFF_AUTOPSY` (no invented trade proposals), `SUPER_BOWL_RUNNER_UP_POSTMORTEM` (grounds its Championship-Window Outlook's contract/cap pressures), `FRANCHISE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT` (no raw trade packages), `DRAFT_BLUEPRINT_ROSTER_AUDIT` (the rookie reserve; no speculative trades), `PLAYER_DEEP_PROFILE` and `LOCAL_PLAYER_SPOTLIGHT` (`get_player_contract`), `LOCAL_SEASON_PROGRESS_REPORT`. Four end with a `ROSTER MOVES:` line (`get_league_transactions` / the brief's blocks; a move only when it bears on the subject, never an invented one): `POWER_RANKINGS` (never rank a team on an invented move), `SEASON_PROGRESS_AUDIT`, `LOCAL_MATCHUP_PRIMER` (a new signing who may play), `LOCAL_POSTGAME_REPORT_CARD` (the next man up after a release). Their `data_queried` list the tools; `tests/test_writer_config.py` checks the lines and the brief hookups.
- **Contract facts rule:** both research blocks list the three contract tools (below) with a `CONTRACT FACTS RULE:` (moves, terms and cap figures only from the tools or the brief and only when they matter to the subject, most articles carrying no cap numbers; `est.` figures hedged; "not known" never filled in; colour invented, never a term, a figure or a move). `tests/test_writer_config.py`: the tools are registered and named in both blocks; `test_contract_asks_name_the_cap_tools` (JR's R14) lets a type ask for cap or contract content only if its `data_queried` or `curatorial_autonomy` names `get_team_cap_sheet` or `get_player_contract`, and no persona may ask; `test_rumor_and_trade_wording` fails on any sentence in the article types, personas, research blocks, `SOCIAL_RULES` or the two matrices that lets a trade be reported as done without saying it was executed.
- **Database Context & Research Tools:** Agents have autonomous access to query live simulation context via ReAct AFC tools:
  - `get_weekly_slate`: Returns the complete scoreboard for all games in a given week with final scores, victory margins, excitement indices, and team IDs.
  - `get_season_standings`: Returns full NFL division standings with wins, losses, win %, points scored (PF), points allowed (PA), and point differential, from regular-season games only.
  - `get_top_popular_players`: Returns the top 20 most popular players across the league with Madden OVR, age, experience, and popularity indices.
  - `get_top_rated_players`: The highest-rated players by Madden OVR at every position (default 200, capped at 300; long snappers excluded), one compact line each with team, OVR, age, experience and season totals: passing/rushing/receiving, tackles/sacks/INT/forced fumbles for listed defenders, FG for kickers, net average for punters. This is the pool for league-wide player rankings such as the Top 100. Ratings run high (this season the 100th-best player is OVR 92 and 733 players are 80+), and the popularity list holds only 41 of the 108 highest-rated players, so it can't serve as the pool.
  - `get_all_team_popularities`: Returns all 32 teams ranked by popularity index, watchability rating, and overall roster quality.
  - `get_player_context` / `get_player_season_stats`: Resolves player by name or ID (season stats are the regular season; when the player has captured playoff lines, a separate `postseason_stats` line, summed from the season's completed non-regular-season games in `game_stat_lines` before the article's week, with a note to keep the two apart); returns age, years of experience, physical measurements (height/weight), career awards list, Madden OVR, cumulative season stats, stored popularity components (SpikeIndex, $R_{\text{rating}}$, $F_{\text{platform}}$, $T_{\text{success}}$, $A_{\text{awards}}$, $E_{\text{exposure}}$), player Playoff Clout, and season touch/yard share for RB/FB/WR/TE. Season stats add sacks taken for passers and, from `season_stat_lines`, `games_played` plus defense (tackles with solo/assisted, sacks, TFL, INT and return yards, pass deflections, forced fumbles, recoveries, defensive TDs, safeties), kicking (FG with long and 50+, XP), punting (average, net, inside the 20), kick/punt returns and sacks allowed, only the categories the player is listed in. Defenders, kickers and punters drop the all-zero offensive lines; a defender (or K/P) with no listed line gets a note that the numbers are unknown, not zero.
  - `get_team_context`: Resolves team by name, abbreviation, or ID; returns win-loss record, watchability, popularity, Madden team ratings, top 3 players, and a one-line `head_coach` (name, season with the team, schemes) pointing to `get_coach_profile`.
  - `get_team_season_schedule`: Returns a team's complete season schedule and game log: week-by-week opponents, scores, win/loss outcomes, victory margins, one-score game breakdown, excitement ratings, and game IDs.
  - `get_game_box_score`: Returns team box score and top individual game contributions, plus `team_situational` per team with only real values (captured, or listed in `exact_fields`; estimated backlog values are never shown): third/fourth-down and two-point conversions (with attempts when known), red-zone TDs and FGs (with trips when known), penalties or penalty yards, quarter-by-quarter scoring, first downs and time of possession (captured box scores); plus, from `game_stat_lines`, `defensive_performances` (defenders with a sack, INT, forced fumble, recovery, defensive TD, safety, 7+ tackles or 2+ TFL; biggest playmakers first) and `kicking` lines. Games the capture didn't cover get a `defense_note` telling the writer not to cite individual defensive stats. It ends with `game_script`, the game's rendered script (§2.14), which the tool description tells writers not to contradict.
  - `get_defense_special_teams_leaders(category, team_identifier, limit, season, rookies_only)`: season leaders from `season_stat_lines`, league-wide or for one team (limit capped at 50); each line shows `rookie` or `exp N` from `player_seasons.years_of_experience`, and `rookies_only=True` keeps first-season players only (for DROY). Categories (`LEADER_CATEGORIES`): sacks, interceptions, tackles, tackles_for_loss, pass_deflections, forced_fumbles, takeaways (INT + fumble recoveries), defensive_tds, field_goals, punting (net average), kick_returns, punt_returns, sacks_allowed (fewest, linemen). Each line carries the player's full defensive or special-teams line, and the note warns that listed players don't sum to team totals and that pressures, snaps, targets and coverage aren't recorded.
  - `get_team_injury_report(team_identifier, week, season)`: Official weekly injury report for a team. Resolves depth-chart starter status and flags `is_key_injury: true` if OVR $\ge 88$, Pop $\ge 50$, or starting QB. National preview slates inject key injuries (`preview_slate['all_games'][i]['key_injuries']`), and beat briefs inject the team's injured starters (`team_injury_report`).
  - `get_advanced_team_analytics`: Reads the stored TWI components (σ_top, P_draft, Y_prod, Purgatory Penalty), TWI, franchise POP, team Playoff Clout, starter age (top 22 by OVR), and $B$ / $L$ / $D$ / $N$ / Legacy Floor from `market_constants`.
  - `get_advanced_game_analytics`: Completed games return pace/production ($P$ from `gotw_engine.production_score`), Upset Leverage ($U$), excitement, and both teams' Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit. Scheduled games return projected $U$ (`excitement_projection.expected_upset`) and the underdog's win probability.
  - `get_opponent_adjusted_credit`: Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit (Section 2.4). No team → all 32 ranked with credit, recent-form credit, Trajectory Multiplier and label, strength of schedule, raw point differential per game, expected wins and win luck; with a team → that summary plus a game-by-game credit log.
  - `get_coach_of_the_year_race(limit, season)`: Section 2.11. Head coaches ranked by COTY score (default top 10), each with record and win%, wins above expectation and expected wins, `team_ovr` and its rank, win% rank, roster outperformance, last season's win% and the change (null unless the team's full previous regular season is recorded), and the first-season flag; the note says when improvement is left out. Registered for every writer and named in the national research block.
  - `get_clutch_profile(team_identifier, quarterback, season)`: Section 2.15. Late-game records from the game scripts for a team or a quarterback (his starts): fourth-quarter comebacks, game-winning drives, blown double-digit and fourth-quarter leads, records by halftime and three-quarter state, overtime record, points per game by quarter, the biggest comeback win and lead lost, and the games behind each count; regular season and postseason separate. Neither = the league leaders. Named in the national research block and in the beat block (the reporter's team and quarterback); the note tells writers to cite the counts as fact and never compare them with real-NFL career totals.
  - `get_matchup_edges`: Section 2.8. Pass, rush and ball-security edges plus Trench Edge for both offenses in a matchup; with no opponent, uses the team's next scheduled game. A `coaching_matchup` block gives, for each offense against the other defense, the offense's coach, scheme, pass lean and fourth-down tendency and the defense's front and coverage tendency, each tier marked `measured` or `reputation` (§3.3a, §2.12).
  - `get_player_scheme_splits(player_identifier, seasons=2)`: Section 2.13. A QB, back, receiver or tight end's per-game production against Strong / Average / Weak scoring defenses, pass rushes and coverages over this season and last: overall per-game line, each group's games, blended per-game averages and change from his average, real and skipped synthetic game counts, a `verdict` when no group has 3 real games, and a note on citing splits.
  - `get_coach_profile(team_identifier, season)`: the team's head coach that season: season with the team and start year, who he replaced, offensive scheme and defensive front, three tendencies (pass lean, fourth down, man coverage; no blitz rate) in words with their source (measured this season, measured last season, real-world tier, or real-world staff philosophy), this season's regular-season and playoff record, `record_with_team` (regular season over every season of this tenure) and `playoff_record_with_team` (postseason W-L with this team over the same tenure, Super Bowls included; `0-0` when he has none), both null when an earlier season of the tenure doesn't have its full regular season in `games` (the `coach_seasons` snapshots are career totals, not per team, so they can't fill the gap), career line (regular season and win%, winning seasons, playoffs, Super Bowls), Super Bowls with the team, Coach of the Year seasons, and a note on citing tendencies. Records count only games the article may see (§3.3a); the article's own season's awards are hidden while an article is being written.
  - `get_team_efficiency_profile`: Section 2.6. Pythagorean wins and pyth luck, pace & production, pass/rush yards and giveaways per game; all 32 sorted by pyth luck, or one team. Each team also carries `situational` (`db_tools.situational_profiles`) from the `team_seasons` Team Stats totals through the latest loaded weekly capture (not cut off by the article's week): third- and fourth-down conversions and rate, two-point tries, red-zone trips (and per game), TD and scoring rate, red-zone trips and TD rate allowed, penalties and penalty yards per game, and league ranks (`third_down_rank`, `fourth_down_rank`, `red_zone_rank`, `red_zone_defense_rank`, `discipline_rank`; 1 = best). Third downs allowed appear only from games where the opponent's `team_stats` row is real (captured, or listed in `exact_fields`), with the game count; there is no season third-down defense total.
  - `get_league_leaderboards`: Returns top 5 teams by watchability and popularity.
  - `get_draft_order`: Round 1 order (Picks 1–32) with playoff-exit brackets (§2.10), each pick with `locked` and `selection_status`, plus `locked_picks`, `playoff_field_known` and `rounds_complete`; respects the run's week cutoff.
  - `get_team_draft_needs`: A team's roster needs ranked by urgency, from starter rating deficits, positional depth, age cliffs and statistical phase rankings.
  - `get_team_roster_depth_chart`: A team's full depth chart by position group (QB, RB, WR, TE, OT, IOL, EDGE, DT, LB, CB, S) with ratings, age tiers and roster demographics.
  - `get_draft_trade_value`: Jimmy Johnson trade-chart points and typical trade packages for any pick (1–260).
  - `get_franchise_rebuild_status`: A franchise's lifecycle stage (Championship Window, Youth Ascent, Roster Purgatory, Ground-Up Rebuild) and recommended draft posture.
  - `get_mock_draft(season, limit)`: this week's saved Round 1 mock draft (`mock_draft_picks`; run and saved on first call), pick by pick with team, prospect profile (blank fields null), fit, rationale and `locked`; `limit` keeps the first N picks (24 at Week 20); `source` is `saved` or `generated` (§2.10).
  - `get_scouted_draft_board(season, limit, position)`: the scouted board for class season + 1 from `draft_prospects` (`projected_pick`, `projected_round`, `round_pick`, `name`, `pos`, `college`, `ht`, `wt`, `age`, `ovr`, `archetype`, `notes`; null where the scan was blank), default 32, filtered by position or position group (QB, OT, EDGE, CB, LB…).
  - `get_team_articles`: Returns recent editorial articles and analysis referencing a specific team (resolves by name, city, abbreviation, or ID).
  - `get_player_articles`: Returns recent editorial articles and profiles referencing a specific player (resolves by name or ID).
  - **Roster moves, contracts and the cap** (step 5 of `docs/TEAM_MANAGEMENT_PLAN.md`, `docs/team_management/05_NEWSROOM_INTEGRATION_PLAN.md`; each a `fetch_*` in `db_tools.py` plus the pure formatters in `newsroom/roster_moves.py`). Every money figure is printed with its basis: `scanned Week N` (the Team Salary scan's capture week), `from the signing` (a CPU signing's contract: bonus, cut savings and dead money print as "not known", never $0), `estimated: last cap scan … plus N roster moves since; dead money from in-season signings is unknown` (a `team_cap_seasons` row whose `derived_at` is later than `scanned_at`), and projected years of a re-signing or restructure, which are shown and always labelled `est.` (JR, 2026-10-20; e.g. `2037: $14.2M est.`). Money is prose (`$16.8M`, `$3.93M`, `$200K`, `-$29.5M`). The docstrings tell writers to call them only when a move or contract bears on the story.
    - `get_team_cap_sheet(team_identifier, season, limit)`: with a team, `cap_basis`, cap, adjusted cap, cap space with its league rank, salaries, dead money (`cap_penalty`), total spend, next rookie reserve, `top_contracts` (largest cap hits this season with through year, total, bonus, cut savings, dead money if cut, no-trade clause, incentive flag, basis and future hits), `expiring` (contract-year players, `years_remaining = 1`), `committed_by_year` (scanned hits for the next four seasons, plus projected new-deal hits as `est.` with their count) and a count of the team's visible moves; no team = all 32 by cap space.
    - `get_player_contract(player_identifier, season)`: team (or "free agent (released by the X, Week N)"), the ACTIVE contract as above with this season's and future hits, the `new_deal` line for a re-signing/extension (`new deal: N years, $X total, starts YYYY`), the season's replaced/released/traded contract rows and the player's visible moves; "No contract on file. Don't invent one." when there is none.
    - `get_league_transactions(team_identifier, week, types, position, notable_only, limit, season)`: visible moves newest week first, one line each (`Week 4 · SIGNED · Dolphins signed CB Matt Beason (free agent): 1 year, $4.49M · now CB3 on the depth chart · notable: yes`; releases add dead money and "free agent since Week N" or "since signed by the X (Week N)"; re-signings the headline terms and the new deal's start when known; restructures the space freed), `counts` by type and `rows_with_no_player`; a week the article can't see yet is refused. Trades appear only once step 3 records them.
    - **Notability** (`roster_moves.notability`, §2.3 of the sub-plan): a starter at his position (`is_starter_rank`, shared with the beat injury block), popularity 50+, a re-signing or extension of $20M+, a signing of $5M+, a restructure freeing $5M+, a release leaving $5M+ dead money, or OVR 88+ (ranks only, never printed). On 2036 Weeks 1–4, 128 of 181 moves pass (124 as starters), above the 15–35 the plan aimed for; the thresholds are module constants for JR to tune.
  - `get_record_book(category, team_identifier, season)`: Section 2.9. Queries the canonical all-time NFL record book and franchise records (matching category names/labels flexibly like `season_pass_tds`, `Passing TDs in a season`, `season_pass_yards`, `season_rec_tds`, `game_pass_tds`, `season_team_points`, `season_team_wins`). Returns all-time NFL record holders, previous record holders, all-time top 10 leaderboards, franchise records for a specified team, and any all-time records broken or tied this season.
- **Fantasy research tools** (`newsroom/fantasy_tools.py`, `FANTASY_TOOLS`): given **only to `FANTASY_*` article types** (`SwarmDispatcher.tools_for(article_type)`), so other prompts don't carry their descriptions. Full PPR, RB = HB + FB. Same week rule as the other tools: a Week N article sees box scores from weeks before N only; projections (`fantasy_projections`) and simulated-league rosters (`fantasy_sim_player_weeks`) up to and including week N are visible (a later week is refused). Depth slots are OVR order within the team's position group; a player released by the article's week keeps his old team (his past lines are with it) but has depth `released` and is out of the depth order (`_roster`, `roster_moves.on_roster`).
  - `get_fantasy_rankings(position, week, sort, limit)`: a week's (default: the article's) projections with opponent, matchup stars, PPR/g, ROS rank and points, ADP rank, rostered/started %, and teams on bye; `sort='ros'` for rest of season. Weeks 2–18 only.
  - `get_fantasy_week_results(week, position, limit)`: a completed week's (default: latest visible) top scorers, biggest beats and biggest misses (projected 8+) vs. the pre-game projection, with stat line, positional finish and rostered %.
  - `get_fantasy_usage(team_identifier, recent_weeks, limit)`: carries, receptions and touches per game with team shares, last N games vs. the rest of the season; no team = league-wide touch risers and fallers (3+ games). Receptions stand in for targets (not tracked).
  - `get_fantasy_waiver_wire(week, position, max_rostered_pct, limit)`: players rostered in ≤ X% of simulated leagues ordered by ROS projection, with rostered % change, recent PPR and touches, plus most added / most dropped, and `nfl_moves` (`nfl_move_lines`: skill players the NFL teams signed in the article's week or the one before, with new team, depth slot and rostered %; then released players still rostered somewhere, as drop candidates projecting 0 until they sign; `FANTASY_WAIVER_WIRE`'s `NFL MOVES:` curatorial line points the writer at it). `tests/test_fantasy_tools.py` covers `nfl_move_lines` on hand-built rows.
  - `get_fantasy_defense_vs_position(position, view)`: PPR allowed per game to each position, season and last 4 games, rank 1 = most generous; `view='playoff_schedule'` = each offense's Weeks 15–17 opponents and average opponent rank.
  - `get_fantasy_player_profile(player_identifier)`: weekly log (opponent, PPR, projection, stat line, rostered/started %), scoring range (mean, best, worst, SD, 20+ boom and sub-8 bust weeks), touches/g, ADP with range, current ROS rank, `team_moves` (his visible moves this season, as move lines) and, for a released player, a `status` saying he has no team and projects 0 until he signs.
  - `get_fantasy_draft_board(position, limit, rookies_only, strategy)`: simulated ADP board with range and draft %, OVR, age, experience, depth slot and team offense ranks; optional ADP among one strategy's managers; once games are visible, season PPR and positional finish so far.
  - `get_fantasy_mock_draft(league, rounds)`: one simulated league's draft pick by pick with each team's strategy.
  - `get_fantasy_season_leaders(position, first_week, last_week, limit)`: PPR leaders over a week range (1–17 season, 15–17 playoffs) with games, per-game, positional finish, ADP and draft %.
  - `get_fantasy_projection_accuracy()`: projection MAE, correlation and guess-the-average MAE for projected starters (weekly top 12 QB/TE, 30 RB/WR), by position, plus the biggest over- and under-projections.
  - `get_fantasy_injuries(week, position, season)`: Weekly injury report for skill positions (QB, RB, WR, TE). Returns injured starters and role-players with depth chart rank, IR status, and the immediate `handcuff_beneficiary` inheriting vacated volume.
- **Season & week scope of the research tools (`newsroom/run_context.py`):** `_execute_agent` sets the article's season and publication week for its thread before calling Gemini (automatic function calling runs the tools on that thread) and clears it afterwards.
  - **Season:** tools take `season: int = 0`, meaning "current": the article's season inside a run, otherwise `league_state.current_season`, otherwise the latest season in `games` (`db_tools.resolve_season`). No tool hardcodes a season.
  - **Week cutoff:** a Week N article can't see results from Week N onward. `get_game_box_score` refuses those games; `get_advanced_game_analytics`, `get_team_season_schedule` and `get_weekly_slate` show them as scheduled with no score; `get_season_standings` counts the record and points from earlier games only; `get_opponent_adjusted_credit`, `get_coach_of_the_year_race`, `get_team_efficiency_profile`, `get_matchup_edges` and the credit inside game analytics use earlier games only (`fetch_team_game_rows(..., before_week=)`); `get_team_articles` / `get_player_articles` return only articles from earlier weeks, which also hides the article's own week. Earlier seasons are fully visible.
  - **Released players** (`roster_moves.on_roster`, Napper's has-game rule): a player is on his team for a Week N article iff `released_week IS NULL OR released_week > N` (N = the cutoff week; with no article context every release counts). `get_team_roster_depth_chart`, `get_team_context`'s top 3, `get_team_draft_needs`, `get_franchise_rebuild_status` and the starter age in `get_advanced_team_analytics` leave released players out; `get_player_season_stats` / `get_player_context`, `get_top_rated_players` and `get_top_popular_players` label their team "free agent (released by the X, Week N)" (`db_tools.team_label`). A CPU signing's new player has no rating or age until the next roster scan: the depth chart counts him with no age in the average, and the draft-needs analysis skips him. The brief's `key_injuries`, the beat brief's `team_injury_report` and the Agent Wire's injuries (§7.8) rank and list on-roster players only.
  - **Transactions** (`roster_moves.txn_visible`): a row headed `WEEK N` happened before Week N's games, so a Week N article sees it (like the injury report) when the capture that loaded it was taken at week ≤ N (`captures.week`); a late `WEEK N` row loaded at N+1 stays out of a regenerated Week N article, and a row with no capture (a confirmed trade) counts as loaded in time. Earlier seasons are visible, later ones hidden. Offseason rows (`week` NULL) are visible only to a Week 24 article once the `signings` offseason event is out. `new_this_week` = visible to Week N but not to Week N−1.
  - **Not cut off:** stored season totals with no per-week history (`season_stats`, `team_seasons` records and indices, player popularity components, ratings, rosters, contracts and team cap rows (except that `get_player_contract` hides a re-signing's terms and projected years while its transaction isn't visible), the draft board and team needs; `get_draft_order` does apply the cutoff, and `get_mock_draft` saves under the run's week). In normal forward runs these already stop at the previous week; when an old week is regenerated they reflect the latest data. `tests/test_run_context.py` covers the context.
- **Prohibited Internal Symbols:** Strict prompt directive banning internal mathematical symbols (`$P$`, `Y_prod`, `(88.17 W)`, `(64.84 POP)`) in favor of natural sports journalism prose.

### 7.1 Article Taxonomy & Writer Archetype Alignments (`newsroom/config/article_types.json`)
`article_types.json` defines 33 core article types: the 29 national/historic types below and the 4 local beat types in §7.2 (plus the 27 fantasy types). Each maps to its allowed writer archetypes and a writing category. A type either carries its own structure directive or, where noted, uses the writer's column format from `article_structure_directives.json`:

1. **The Macro Slate Recap** (`MACRO_SLATE_RECAP`)
   - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Barnwell, Rosenthal (`BARNWELL`, `ROSENTHAL`, `Weekly Recaps`).
   - **Granularity:** Week-level.
   - **Focus:** Cross-league trends, regression candidates, aesthetic watchability power rankings.
   - **Data Queried:** $E_{\text{game}}$, $P_{\text{pace}}$, league-wide point differentials, Pythagorean win expectations.
   - **Structure Source:** Writer format (Barnwell's column or Rosenthal's Weekend Notebook; `article_structure_directives.json`).

2. **The Player Deep Profile** (`PLAYER_DEEP_PROFILE`)
   - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Jeffri Chadiha (Narrative/Legacy), KC Joyner (Scientific Efficiency), Gregg Rosenthal (Aesthetic/Vibes) (`CHADIHA`, `JOYNER`, `ROSENTHAL`, `Player Season Overview`).
   - **Granularity:** In-Season Trajectory / Career Arc.
   - **Focus:** Prestige, longform player profile evaluating their season-to-date trajectory, breakout status, MVP credentials, or legacy implications.
   - **Data Queried:** Player Popularity Index ($Pop_{\text{player}}$), Awards Equity ($A_{\text{awards}}$), Playoff Clout ($S_{\text{playoff}}$), SpikeIndex, Franchise Platform ($F_{\text{platform}}$).
   - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Evaluates season-to-date leaderboards, cumulative stats, and popularity to select one player whose current campaign defines the narrative of the league—an aging legend defending legacy, an MVP frontrunner with massive cultural footprint, or a volatile breakout star, regardless of whether it is Week 3 or Week 17.
   - **Structure Directive:** 4-part profile (`# 1. The Defining Moment / The Hook`, `## 2. The Anatomy of Their Season`, `## 3. The Cultural Footprint & Legacy`, `### 4. The Horizon`).
   - **Prompt Hook:** Select a marquee player whose campaign currently defines the season. Deliver a definitive longform profile on their offensive engine, their cultural footprint, and their career horizon based on their season-to-date tape.

3. **The Franchise State of the Union** (`FRANCHISE_STATE_OF_THE_UNION`)
   - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Sando (`SANDO`, `Front-Office Columnists`).
   - **Granularity:** 3-to-5 year franchise trajectory.
   - **Focus:** General Manager job security, roster demographic distributions, draft hit rates, rebuilding vs. purgatory.
   - **Data Queried:** $P_{\text{draft}}$, $Y_{\text{prod}}$, Purgatory Penalty, Base Market Gravity ($B$), Roster Age, Head-Coach Profile (`get_coach_profile`), Round 1 Draft Order (`get_draft_order`), Round 1 Mock Draft Simulation (`get_mock_draft`).
   - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Evaluates multi-year franchise data, demographic distributions, and standing trends. Identifies a franchise at an existential fork in the road: aging roster in purgatory, high-gravity giant collapsing, or young rebuilding core on the verge of contention. Evaluates coaching hierarchy via `get_coach_profile`. Includes the Coach-Rookie Alignment Test: for early-lottery teams (Picks 1–5), cross-references draft slot (`get_draft_order`) and primary positional need (`get_team_draft_needs`) against the head coach's tenure, scheme, pass lean, and career win percentage (`get_coach_profile`) to interrogate whether to trust the incumbent staff to groom an incoming franchise QB or foundational tackle or if drafting a blue-chip asset necessitates a clean coaching break; may name the projected prospect from `get_mock_draft`.
   - **Structure Source:** Writer format (Mike Sando's Pick Six with anonymous executive quotes).
   - **Prompt Hook:** Analyze a franchise in transition. Ignore the single-game box score. Evaluate their front office's 3-to-5 year trajectory using the Purgatory Penalty, aging roster constraints, and draft equity.

4. **The Single-Game Schematic Autopsy** (`SINGLE_GAME_SCHEMATIC_AUTOPSY`)
   - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** KC Joyner (Scientific Efficiency), Mina Kimes (Metric-Driven Schematic Breakdown), Dan Orlovsky (QB Film Room / Telestrator) (`JOYNER`, `KIMES`, `ORLOVSKY`, `Film Analysts`).
   - **Granularity:** Single-game play-by-play.
   - **Focus:** Point-of-attack wins, run-pass splits, specific receiver coverage isolations, quarterback processing mechanics, and the defensive playmakers who swung the game.
   - **Data Queried:** Advanced Game Analytics (Offensive Pace, Single-Game Excitement), Game Box Scores, Touch Share & Scrimmage Yard Share (season), game defensive lines and kickers where captured.
   - **Curatorial Autonomy:** the most extreme tactical anomaly on the slate; a game decided by defense qualifies only if its box score has defensive lines, and scheme is the writer's read of the box score (no pressure, blitz or coverage rates, snaps or targets).
   - **Structure Source:** Writer format (Joyner's tape-charting breakdown, Kimes's analytical breakdown or Orlovsky's film-room telestrator; `article_structure_directives.json`).

5. **The Power Rankings** (`POWER_RANKINGS`)
   - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Mike Sando (Executive Tiers & Front-Office Context), Gregg Rosenthal (Vibe & Watchability Rankings) (`SANDO`, `ROSENTHAL`, `32-Team Thematic Consensus`).
   - **Granularity:** Week-level / Full League 1-32 Hierarchy.
   - **Focus:** Comprehensive publication-ready 32-team Power Ranking evaluating every team 1-32 through an explicit weekly editorial theme, underlying metrics, and movement indicators.
   - **Data Queried:** Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit, Team Watchability Index ($W$), Trajectory Multiplier ($T$), Purgatory Penalty, Base Market Gravity ($B$).
   - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Evaluates full 32-team standings and metrics to separate paper tigers from true contenders using Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit, rewards trajectory surging teams, penalizes middle-tier stagnators, and establishes a unifying Weekly Editorial Theme.
   - **Structure Directive:** 4-part 32-Team Power Ranking (`# 1. The Macro Overview & Weekly Theme`, `## 2. The 32-Team Enumeration 1 to 32`, one header per team formatted `## [Rank]. [City] [Team] ([W-L]) (Previous: N | Change: ±N)`, the same format as the continuity block in §7.6, `3. The Thematic Blurbs with bold kickers`, `### 4. The Walk-Off / The Drop-Off`).
   - **Prompt Hook:** Rank all 32 NFL franchises from 1 to 32 under an explicit weekly editorial theme. Separate paper tigers from true contenders using opponent-adjusted credit and trajectory, provide a 3-to-4 sentence analytical blurb for every team with movement indicators, and diagnose the widening gap between tiers.

6. **The Awards Tracker** (`AWARDS_TRACKER`)
   - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Field Yates (Objective/Factual), Bill Simmons (Narrative/Historical Debate) (`YATES`, `SIMMONS`, `The Hardware Race`).
   - **Granularity:** Season-level / Hardware Campaign.
   - **Focus:** Active MVP, OPOY, OROY, DPOY, DROY and Coach of the Year races, evaluating true contenders vs. empty-calorie stat accumulators, narrative momentum, rookie learning curves, the defenders driving the defensive races, and which head coaches are winning more than their rosters should.
   - **Data Queried:** Awards Equity ($A_{\text{awards}}$), Player Popularity ($Pop_{\text{player}}$), Team Success ($T_{\text{success}}$), SpikeIndex, defense season leaders (rookies flagged), player season stats, the Coach of the Year race (§2.11).
   - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Aggressively applies the "Empty Calories" Suppressor on sub-.300 teams, pinpointing the intersection of elite explosive production ($SpikeIndex$) and contender team success ($T_{\text{success}}$). A "Defensive Awards Evidence" block (also appended to both postseason overrides) builds the DPOY race from `get_defense_special_teams_leaders` (sacks, TFL, interceptions, takeaways first; forced fumbles, pass deflections, defensive TDs as tiebreakers) and the DROY race from the same tool with `rookies_only=True` (years of experience 0), confirmed with `get_player_season_stats`; a defender with no recorded line has unknown numbers and can't be a candidate; pressures, snaps and coverage numbers are never cited; team record weighs less than for MVP. For Coach of the Year it starts from `get_coach_of_the_year_race` and credits the coach getting the most beyond the roster, the schedule and last season, not simply the best record. The **COACHING DATA CITATION** constraint strictly forbids explicit mentions of internal engine mechanics or labels ("COTY score", "coach scores", "Coach of the Year metric", "historical bonus"); coaches are framed solely through real-world football accomplishments (win-loss record, undefeated seasons, all-time/franchise records broken, margin of victory, roster talent overachievement).
   - **Structure Directive:** 5-part Awards Watch, approx. 1,100–1,400 words (The MVP Frontrunner / The Heavy Crown `#` / `##`, The Contenders / The Chasers `##`, The Coach of the Year Race `##`, The Offensive Rookie Race `##`, The Defensive Races `##`: DPOY frontrunner and chasers with their lines, then a `###` DROY race). Section 3 forbids internal COTY scores or bonuses.
   - **Postseason:** Week 21 runs the `AWARDS_FINALISTS` override and Week 22 `AWARDS_WINNERS` (§7.4).
   - **Prompt Hook:** Analyze the active MVP, OPOY, OROY, DPOY and DROY races across the league. Apply the empty-calories suppressor to eliminate meaningless stats, examine the frontrunner vs. the chasers, rank the Coach of the Year race, break down the offensive rookie race, and settle the defensive races on recorded sacks, tackles for loss and takeaways.
   - **Award records:** single-winner regular-season awards in the `awards` table (`MVP`, `OPOY`, `DPOY`, `OROY`, `DROY`, `COTY`) are parsed, validated, and recorded from the Week 22 `AWARDS_TRACKER` (`AWARDS_WINNERS`) article by `newsroom/award_winners.py` (§7.6c); `SBMVP` is parsed and recorded from `SUPER_BOWL_GAME_RECAP` by `newsroom/award_winners.py` (§7.6d); year-end All-Pro First and Second Team honors are parsed and recorded from `OFFICIAL_AP_ALL_PRO_ROSTER` by `newsroom/all_pro.py` (§7.6b). Historical seasons (2026–2034) were filled from Madden's award screens (`pipeline/batch_menus.py`).
   - `tests/test_writer_config.py` checks that the type and both overrides cover Coach of the Year and point at the tool, and that the tool is registered.

7. **The Weekly Slate Lookahead** (`SLATE_LOOKAHEAD_PREDICTIONS`)
   - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Bill Barnwell (Efficiency & Statistical Modeling), Emmanuel Acho (Contrarian Matchups & Narratives), Gregg Rosenthal (Aesthetic Matchups & Watchability) (`BARNWELL`, `ACHO`, `ROSENTHAL`, `ESPN Multi-Angle Matchup Preview`).
   - **Granularity:** Week-ahead / Matchup Preview Guide.
   - **Focus:** Comprehensive, modular weekly preview guide modeled after ESPN's multi-perspective game capsules covering team context, key stats, watchability, tactical predictions, fantasy spotlights, and projected final scores.
   - **Data Queried:** Upcoming Matchup Schedule, Single-Game Excitement Index ($E$), Offensive Pace ($P$), Upset Leverage ($U_{\text{projected}}$), Team Watchability ($W$), SpikeIndex, Base Market Gravity ($B$).
   - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Evaluates the full upcoming schedule across high-tempo clashes ($P$, $W$), upset leverage shockers ($U$), and skill-player mismatch volatility (SpikeIndex) using an integrated multi-analyst format.
   - **Structure Directive:** 4-part Modular Lookahead (`# 1. The Slate Overview & Complete Matchup Jump List`, `2. The Full Slate Enumeration for every single game on schedule`, `## 3. Modular Matchup Capsules with Team Context, Stat to Know, Why Watch, Tactical Angle, Fantasy Spotlight, and Official Projected Final Score`, `4. Data Integration Rule`).
   - **Prompt Hook:** Preview the upcoming weekend slate using ESPN-style modular game capsules. For each featured matchup, break down locker-room buzz, a key statistical split, why fans should tune in, a bold tactical forecast, fantasy spike spotlight, and an official final score prediction.

8. **The NFL Top 100 & Positional Value Hierarchy** (`NFL_TOP_100_HIERARCHY`)
    - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** The Athletic Staff (Consensus Roster Building), Field Yates (Factual List-Maker) (`THE_ATHLETIC`, `YATES`, `The Preseason / Offseason Hierarchy`).
    - **Granularity:** Preseason / League-Wide Player Hierarchy.
    - **Focus:** Prestige top 100 player countdown from No. 100 to No. 1: grouped tiers for 100-11, an individual top 10 countdown, positional value and legacy vs. projection.
    - **Data Queried:** Baseline Rating ($R_{\text{rating}}$), Positional Multiplier ($M_{\text{pos}}$), Career Awards Equity ($A_{\text{career}}$), Explosive Ceiling ($SpikeIndex$).
    - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Builds the pool with `get_top_rated_players` (top 200 by OVR, every position, with stats; `get_player_season_stats` only for close calls) and ranks on baseline talent, production and positional value, not popularity, including linemen and defenders. Weighs aging veterans with championship pedigree against young players with elite traits. Every player must be a real player in the league database, listed once with his current team and position.
    - **Structure Directive:** Countdown from No. 100 to No. 1, modelled on ESPN's NFL Rank and NFL Network's player-voted Top 100, which both count down and end on No. 1 with no snubs section (`# headline` + 2-paragraph methodology with team and position counts; 3–4 `##` tiers in countdown order, e.g. `## The Elite Specialists (100-61)`, each with 1–2 paragraphs of analysis and a numbered `**[Rank]. [Player]**, [Pos], [City] [Team]` line per player from highest number to lowest, no individual blurbs; `## The Top 10` with one `### [Rank]. [Player], [Pos], [City] [Team]` per player from 10 to 1, 3–4 sentences each; the article ends on No. 1 with no snubs, honorable mentions or wrap-up). Exactly 100 different players, no gaps or repeats.
    - **Prompt Hook:** Count down the top 100 players in the league from No. 100 to No. 1. Explain your methodology, group Nos. 100-11 into named tiers listed in countdown order, break down the top 10 individually from No. 10 to No. 1, and end on No. 1.

9. **The Playoff Predictor** (`PLAYOFF_PREDICTOR`)
    - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Gregg Rosenthal (Chaos/Wild Card brackets), Bill Barnwell (Efficiency-based pathing) (`ROSENTHAL`, `BARNWELL`, `Bracketology & Postseason Tiers`).
    - **Granularity:** Late-Season / Playoff Race.
    - **Focus:** Projected postseason bracket mapping, identifying fraudulent division leaders ripe for first-round upsets, and dangerous Wild Card gatecrashers.
    - **Data Queried:** Trajectory Multiplier ($T$), Watchability Volatility ($\sigma_{\text{top}}$), Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit, Base Market Gravity ($B$).
    - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Maps the projected postseason bracket; exposes fraudulent division leaders with inflated records ripe for first-round upsets; highlights terrifying surging Wild Card teams.
    - **Structure Directive:** 4-part Playoff Picture (`# 1. The Postseason Landscape`, `## 2. The Bracket Locks`, `## 3. The Dangerous Gatecrashers`, `### 4. The First-Round Exit Warning`).
    - **Prompt Hook:** Map out the projected postseason bracket. Expose a fraudulent division leader on upset alert, identify the terrifying Wild Card gatecrashers, and establish the true conference heavyweights.

10. **The Super Bowl Contender Blueprint** (`SUPER_BOWL_CONTENDER_BLUEPRINT`)
    - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Mike Sando (Front-Office Windows), KC Joyner (Statistical Profiling), Jeffri Chadiha (Legacy & Clout) (`SANDO`, `JOYNER`, `CHADIHA`, `The Championship Litmus Test`).
    - **Granularity:** Championship / Postseason Arc.
