29. **The Black Monday Mandate (Coaching Hot Seat Audit)** (`COACHING_HOT_SEAT_AUDIT`, Week 19 Slot 6)
    - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Mike Sando (Front-Office Insider & Executive Verdicts), Bill Barnwell (Expected Wins & Roster-Adjusted Accountability) (`SANDO`, `BARNWELL`, `Front-Office Columnists`).
    - **Granularity:** League-wide Black Monday sweep of the completed regular season.
    - **Focus:** Head-coach job security for the teams that missed the playoffs: wins against expected wins, results against baseline roster talent, and the draft slot each staff hands its front office.
    - **Data Queried:** Baseline Roster Talent ($R_{\text{rating}}$), Draft Order (Picks 1–18, `get_draft_order`), Coach of the Year / Wins Above Expectation (`get_coach_of_the_year_race`, called with limit 32), Head-Coach Profile (`get_coach_profile`).
    - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Only coaches of teams holding Picks 1–18. Sorts each into the Firing Line (wins above expectation below −1.5 with a top-half roster), the Bubble (milder shortfall or a scheme that no longer fits) or the Safe Seats (bottom-half roster at or above expectation, or a first-year coach). The Editorial Desk's subject leads the Firing Line. Verdicts are the writer's read, never a reported firing or hiring.
    - **Structure Directive:** 4-part Black Monday Mandate (`# The Black Monday Verdicts`, `## The Firing Line: Severe Underperformance vs. Talent`, `## On the Bubble: Ultimatum Years & Scheme Mandates`, `### The Safe Seats: Talent Deficit Pardons`).
    - **Prompt Hook:** It is Black Monday. Sort every head coach whose season ended in Week 18 into the Firing Line, the Bubble or the Safe Seats, judging each staff by its wins against what its roster and schedule said it should win.

30. **The Historic Record Breaker** (`HISTORIC_RECORD_BREAK`)
    - **Allowed Writers / Archetypes:** Jeffri Chadiha (Narrative/Legacy), Field Yates (Factual & Milestones), Bill Simmons (Historical / Pop Culture), Bill Barnwell (Statistical Context), Dan Orlovsky (Film Room) (`CHADIHA`, `YATES`, `SIMMONS`, `BARNWELL`, `ORLOVSKY`, `Historic Milestones`).
    - **Granularity:** Historical / Event Milestone (Retrospective recap week).
    - **Focus:** Definitive coverage celebrating an all-time NFL record being shattered or tied, examining the historical weight, the fallen record standard, the mechanics of how it was broken, and its standing in NFL lore.
    - **Data Queried:** All-Time NFL Record Book (`get_record_book`), Player Season Stats (`get_player_season_stats`), Game Box Scores (`get_game_box_score`), Team Context (`get_team_context`), Player Context (`get_player_context`).
    - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Focuses exclusively on the NFL-wide record-breaking or record-tying event assigned by the Editorial Desk. Queries `get_record_book` to retrieve canonical all-time leaderboards, previous holders, the year it was set, and how long the record stood. Combines game box scores and season totals to ground the achievement in authentic football history.
    - **Structure Directive:** 4-part Milestone Celebration (approx. 1,000–1,400 words): `# [Epic Headline]` with `> **EXCERPT:**`, `1. The Historical Moment (The Coronation)`, `## 2. Toppling the Pantheon: The Old Standard`, `## 3. The Anatomy of History: How the Record Fell`, `### 4. The Pantheon Verdict: Where This Campaign Stands`.
    - **Prompt Hook:** Celebrate an all-time NFL record being shattered or tied. Anchor the piece in historical weight: honor the legendary standard that previously stood, dissect the mechanics and scheme that enabled the milestone, and deliver a definitive verdict on where this feat ranks in pro football lore.

### 7.2 Local Beat Reporting Taxonomy (`LOCAL_BEAT_REPORTER`)
Local Beat Reporters cover their assigned franchise exclusively. Their research directive keeps them on their own team's games, and they don't receive the national writers' historical-comparison instruction. The beat research directive points them at `get_defense_special_teams_leaders` for defensive/special-teams leaders and `get_team_injury_report` for active roster injuries. Roster moves: when the team has moves new this week, the beat brief adds `team_transactions` (`roster_moves.team_block`: `moves_this_week` as move lines, notable first, a `cap_snapshot` line with its basis, and `reporting_guidance`: a notable move gets a paragraph on the role it leaves or fills; terms only from the lines or `get_player_contract`; invent the negotiation colour, never a figure; the cap only if it bears on the story). Moves feed the existing beat articles: there is no new wakeup trigger or beat article type (JR, 2026-10-20). Starting injuries are injected directly into the beat assignment brief (`team_injury_report`; starters are ranked among on-roster players with `roster_moves.is_starter_rank`, and released players are left out). The `BEAT INJURY REPORTING RULE` requires beat reporters to cover all injured starters across offense, defense, and specialists, framing them through tactical fallout ("next man up", protection shifts, scheme adjustments) while barring speculation on non-starters or unverified medical returns.

**Weekly portfolio (`newsroom/local_portfolio.py`):** every team wakeup plans the whole portfolio, however many beat reporters the team has: the Postgame Report Card if the team played last week, the Matchup Primer if it plays this week (aimed at that game via an Editorial Desk subject assignment), the Player Spotlight always, and the Season Progress Report from Week 6. Types the team already has that week (e.g. a national Slot 6–8 primer) are skipped. Articles are dealt round-robin across the team's reporters, starting at a different reporter each week, so one reporter writes all of them and no reporter is stuck on the postgame report. Each is saved under the team with slot `Local Beat N` (`tests/test_local_portfolio.py`).


1. **The Postgame Report Card** (`LOCAL_POSTGAME_REPORT_CARD`)
   - **Beat Archetype:** Local Beat Writer / Postgame Columnist (2–4 hours post-whistle).
   - **Focus:** Immediate unit execution versus expectations, unit letter grades (A+ through F), offensive line protection breakdowns, third-down stalls, and coaching decisions.
   - **Data Queried:** Unit Offensive Pace ($P$), Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit, Giveaways per Game (ball security), Pass Rush Rating (Madden rank).

2. **The Local Matchup Primer & Game-Plan Blueprint** (`LOCAL_MATCHUP_PRIMER`)
   - **Beat Archetype:** Senior Beat Reporter (Friday / Saturday morning preview).
   - **Focus:** Home-team perspective matchup reality, 3 hyper-specific micro battles, game-plan victory blueprint, and final score prediction.
   - **Data Queried:** Matchup Edge (pass, rush, ball security), Upset Leverage ($U_{\text{projected}}$), Trench Edge (O-line vs. pass rush ratings), both teams' defense & special-teams leaders.
   - **Defense:** each of the three micro-battles is anchored in both players' season numbers where they exist (sacks and TFL vs. sacks allowed, catches and yards vs. INT and pass deflections), with no invented pressure rates, snaps or targets; the Defensive Mandate is a sack or takeaway target tied to named defenders (it was a third-down stop percentage, which isn't recorded).

3. **The Local Player Spotlight** (`LOCAL_PLAYER_SPOTLIGHT`)
   - **Beat Archetype:** Franchise Beat Reporter / Film Analyst.
   - **Focus:** Single local player's role evolution, developmental leap, tape & assignment execution, coaching trust, and roster implications.
   - **Data Queried:** SpikeIndex, Touch Share & Scrimmage Yard Share (season), Positional Multiplier ($M_{\text{pos}}$), Blue-Chip Pedigree ($P_{\text{draft}}$), Under-25 Production Share ($Y_{\text{prod}}$), defense & special-teams lines.
   - **Defense:** Defenders and specialists are eligible: the curatorial block points at `get_defense_special_teams_leaders` and `get_player_season_stats`, with the unknown-not-zero, no-team-sums and no pressure/snap/target rules (JR, Oct 14). The whole roster is in play (a pass rusher piling up sacks, a ball-hawking corner, a kicker or returner), and usage is described from recorded numbers rather than target volume or snap rate.

4. **The Local Season-to-Date Progress Report** (`LOCAL_SEASON_PROGRESS_REPORT`)
   - **Beat Archetype:** Senior Beat Columnist (Midseason / Season Audit).
   - **Focus:** Comprehensive evaluation of the home team's campaign, offensive/defensive identity check, structural strengths, lingering flaws, coaching staff seat temperature, and second-half win benchmark.
   - **Data Queried:** Trajectory Multiplier ($T$), Opponent-Adjusted Game Credit, Youth Production Share ($Y_{\text{prod}}$), Purgatory Penalty, Base Market Gravity ($B$), Round 1 Draft Order (`get_draft_order`).
   - **Curatorial Autonomy:** Evaluates franchise trajectory through local expectations. When diagnosing teams in the Purgatory Penalty, weighs their remaining schedule against their projected draft capital (e.g. escaping the middle tier vs securing a top-5 pick).

### 7.3 The Editorial Assignment Desk & Article-First Commissioning Architecture
Rather than waking up a fixed list of writer agents who generate generic articles, the Swarm operates under an **Article-First Editorial Commissioning Architecture** (`commission_weekly_editorial_slate`):

1. **Weekly Default Distribution (Full 10-Slot Editorial Matrix):**
   - `docs/EDITORIAL_MATRIX.md` is the source of truth for the weekly slate. Every weekly run (`dispatch_weekly_national`) commissions all 10 matrix slots for the week: Hero, Slots 1–8, and Standalone.
   - `article_count` / `target_articles` truncate the slate and are reserved for tests and debugging (`tests/test_wakeups.py --article-count`, `tests/test_editorial_flow.py --count`); production never passes them.
   - The weekly national run does not wake up any extra beat reporters. Slots 6–8 come only from the matrix. Local beat portfolios (`LOCAL_POSTGAME_REPORT_CARD`, `LOCAL_PLAYER_SPOTLIGHT`, `LOCAL_SEASON_PROGRESS_REPORT`, plus team-specific `LOCAL_MATCHUP_PRIMER`) come from on-demand team wakeups (`dispatch_team_local`).
   - **Slot Targeting (`newsroom/slot_targets.py`):** Before writers are assigned, `commission_weekly_editorial_slate` resolves the subject of every targeted slot per the matrix's *Slot Targeting Rules*. Matchup primers go to the week's most interesting scheduled games, ranked by `rank_games_by_interest`: projected $E$ (§2.1) plus division-game and playoff-race stakes (byes excluded, 2-week team rotation in the regular season, covering the side with fewer primers this season), and in Super Bowl week to the AFC and NFC champions. In regular-season Weeks 2–18, Slot 6 is converted into a Crisis Beat / Hot Seat Accountability primer targeting teams with the worst win-differential vs. expectation ($\Delta W = \text{Wins} - \text{Expected Wins} \le -2.0$) or active losing streaks with sub-.350 win percentages, while Slots 7 and 8 remain marquee matchup primers. Phase 4 `FRANCHISE_STATE_OF_THE_UNION` goes to a mathematically eliminated team. Week 19 `COACHING_HOT_SEAT_AUDIT` (Slot 6) goes to the team that missed the playoffs with the steepest shortfall below −1.5 wins above expectation (`team_seasons.wins − expected_wins`, `hot_seat_team_ids`; the note lists the other eliminated teams below −1.5; untargeted if none qualifies or expected wins aren't computed), resolved before the Slot 7 Exit State of the Union, which goes to a team that missed the playoffs, and W20–22 `PLAYOFF_AUTOPSY` slots go to the prior round's losers. All of these are distinct teams, most popular first.
   - Each primer is written by a beat reporter for its subject team. Every targeted article gets an `EDITORIAL DESK SUBJECT ASSIGNMENT` block in its prompt, which overrides subject-selection instructions, and is saved under the subject's `team_id`. If league data can't be loaded, slots fall back to writer-chosen subjects.
   - **Dynamic Weekly Slate Expansion for Record-Breaking Events:** In any week where an all-time NFL single-game or single-season record is broken or tied in the recap week (`week - 1`), `commission_weekly_editorial_slate` detects the milestones via `find_records_broken_in_week(cur, season, recap_week)` and dynamically expands the weekly editorial slate by appending dedicated `Record Breaker Slot` (or `Record Breaker Slot 2`, etc.) assignments beyond the default 10-slot matrix. Each record slot is commissioned with `HISTORIC_RECORD_BREAK`, assigned to an eligible writer (`CHADIHA`, `YATES`, `SIMMONS`, `BARNWELL`, `ORLOVSKY`), and injected with a mandatory `EDITORIAL DESK ASSIGNMENT (MANDATORY)` brief containing the broken record details, old record holder and value, game matchup, and historical context. Weekly national run filter `NATIONAL_SLOT_FILTER` includes `OR slot LIKE 'Record%%'`. On the frontend (`Mu_Network/src/App.tsx`, `index.css`) and API (`server/api.py`), record breaker articles are served in `historic_events` and featured with a commemorative gold/amber badge ("ALL-TIME NFL RECORD BROKEN") and `.record-breaker-card` styling.
   - **Postseason week numbering:** 19 = Wild Card, 20 = Divisional, 21 = Conference Championships, 22 = Super Bowl bye, 23 = Super Bowl. Historical imports (`pipeline/batch_menus.py`) store the Super Bowl at week 23. `db/migrations/migrate_super_bowl_week.py` moves older week-22 Super Bowl rows to week 23.
2. **Matrix-Driven Editorial Slate Planning (`docs/EDITORIAL_MATRIX.md`):**
   - Implements the definitive **18-Week Regular Season & 5-Week Postseason Editorial Matrix** to commission a structured portfolio of article types per week:
     - **Regular Season (Weeks 1–18):** Fixed Hero Slot (`PRE_SEASON_ROSTER_TIERS` in W1, `MACRO_SLATE_RECAP` in W2–18), Slot 1 (`SLATE_LOOKAHEAD_PREDICTIONS`), Slot 2 (`NFL_TOP_100_HIERARCHY` in W1, `SINGLE_GAME_SCHEMATIC_AUTOPSY` in W2–18), Slots 3–5 (Feature Trio selected dynamically from 4 distinct seasonal phase pools), Slots 6–8 (Slot 6 Crisis Beat primer in W2–18 / 3x `LOCAL_MATCHUP_PRIMER` in W1, Slots 7–8 `LOCAL_MATCHUP_PRIMER`), and Standalone (`POWER_RANKINGS`).
     - **Postseason (Weeks 19–23):** Custom boards tailored for Wild Card Eve (W19), Divisional Eve (W20), Championship Eve (W21), Super Bowl Bye (W22; Slot 8 features the joint HC-QB legacy column `SUPER_BOWL_HISTORICAL_STAKES`), and Super Bowl Week (W23). W19 keeps the Standalone `POWER_RANKINGS` (the season's final ranking, see 7.6); W20–W23 have no Standalone slot (9 articles).
   - Dynamically rotates national columnists (`SIMMONS`, `KIMES`, `ACHO`, `SKIP_BAYLESS`, `YATES`) into `NATIONAL_OPINION_COLUMN` slots during regular-season feature trios.
3. **Qualified Writer Matching & Load Balancing:**
   - For each commissioned article type, the Editorial Desk queries `allowed_writers` from the taxonomy (`newsroom/config/article_types.json`).
   - Picks each writer at random from the least-used allowed writers so far this week (`writer_assignment_counts`). This spreads work but doesn't prevent repeats: a writer can get more than one article in a week when the allowed lists overlap. Rotating-columnist slots name their writer directly and skip the balancing.
   - **Writer records:** every writer name in `allowed_writers` and the columnist pools is an archetype key in `agent_personas.json`. `compile_agent_prompt` uses the archetype's `archetypes` persona, except for articles by writers listed under `article_personas` in `agent_personas.json` (long-form voices for writers whose persona is written for tweets: YATES, SIMMONS, KIMES, ACHO, CLARK, ORLOVSKY; `tests/test_writer_config.py` fails if a national writer has only a tweet persona), and `FANTASY_*` articles, which use `fantasy_writers.json`. `get_agent_record` loads the `agent_memories` row whose `archetype` matches exactly (oldest first); old shorthand names are mapped first by `WRITER_ARCHETYPE_ALIASES` (`BAYLESS` → `SKIP_BAYLESS`). Tweet-only accounts such as `SKIP_TWEET` are separate agents and are never used for articles. `tests/test_writer_config.py` checks that every article writer has a persona, every opinion columnist has a curatorial directive, and no columnist pool or article type uses an alias.
   - Binds each writer to their specific commissioned `article_type`, ensuring prompt injection of custom curatorial lenses, structure directives, and analytical parameters.

### 7.3.1 The Mu Fantasy Desk (`newsroom/fantasy_desk.py`, `docs/FANTASY_EDITORIAL_MATRIX.md`)
- **When:** `weekly_run` calls `dispatch_weekly_fantasy(season, week)` after the national slate, Weeks 1–18 (W19–23: no slate; the page keeps W18). A week is skipped when it already has `Fantasy …` slots (unless `--force`).
- **Slots** (`fantasy_slate(week)`): `Fantasy Hero`, `Fantasy 1`–`3` (front page) and `Fantasy Shelf N` ("View all analysis").
  - W1: `FANTASY_DRAFT_TIERS` ×4 (WR, RB, QB, TE, position given as a desk note) + Sleepers & Busts, Mock Draft, Rookie Rankings.
  - W2–14: Start/Sit, Waiver Wire, Weekly Reactions, Card 03 from `CARD3_SCHEDULE` (W2 Overreaction, W3 DvP, W4 Overreaction, W5 Overreaction, W6 Buy/Sell, W7 ADP Checkpoint, W8 ROS Tiers, W9 DvP, W10 Buy/Sell, W11 ROS Tiers, W12 Trade Deadline Special, W13 Playoff Schedule Planner, W14 Must-Win Week); shelf: Rankings & Tiers, Box Score & Usage, 10 Things, Matchup Exploits (W3+).
  - W15–17: Start/Sit (playoff/championship editions), Streamers, Reactions, Playoff Schedule Planner (W15) / Ceiling Plays; W18: Season in Review, Awards (with All-Fantasy Team), Draft Retro, Projection Report Card, Dynasty Early Look.
  - K/D/ST (`FANTASY_STREAMING_DST_K`) is gated by `KDST_ENABLED = False` until migration phase 3.
- **Writers:** 26 `FANTASY_*` types in `article_types.json`, written by BERRY, YATES, BARNWELL and JOYNER (spread by least-used, like the national desk). Yates writes all four W1 Draft Tiers; Joyner writes 10 Things. Length targets are minimums ("at least N words; longer is fine"). For `FANTASY_*` articles `compile_agent_prompt` swaps in the writer's fantasy voice and selection filter from `newsroom/config/fantasy_writers.json` (tweet personas and national directives are untouched), uses `FANTASY_DESK_GUIDELINES` instead of the national research-tools block (full PPR, all numbers from tools, "rostered in X% of leagues" with no mention of simulations, official skill injuries via `get_fantasy_injuries` to explain vacated volume and handcuffs, NFL roster moves (a released player has no game and projects 0 until he signs; a signing is a pickup only if his depth slot gives him a role; moves only from `nfl_moves` / `team_moves`, never invented), clear verdicts, never invent past calls), a fantasy-calendar temporal block (`temporal_mode = "FANTASY"`), the fantasy tools, and the desk note as an `EDITORIAL DESK ASSIGNMENT (MANDATORY)` block. Fantasy articles are saved with `team_id` NULL.
- **Brief:** a small fantasy brief (season, publication week, fantasy phase, week label, results-through week, scoring), not the national assignment brief.

### 7.4 Playoff-Specific Dynamic Overrides Architecture (`newsroom/config/playoff_overrides.json`)
During the single-elimination tournament and Super Bowl run (Weeks 19–23), standard regular-season directives are dynamically superseded by targeted postseason overrides. The orchestrator inspects `target_weeks` at compile time and overrides the baseline article directive, curatorial autonomy filter, and structure specification without mutating baseline schemas:

- **Lookup & validation (`newsroom/playoff_overrides.py`):** `find_active_override(overrides, article_type, week)` picks the override; `normalize_temporal_mode` reads `temporal_mode` case-insensitively and accepts only `RETROSPECTIVE`, `PROSPECTIVE` or `CUMULATIVE` (anything else falls back to the article type's mode in `TEMPORAL_ARTICLE_MODES`). `SwarmDispatcher` runs `validate_playoff_overrides` on load and prints a warning per problem: unknown article type or temporal mode, target weeks outside 19–23, a week claimed by two overrides of the same type, or a misused `preview_week`. `tests/test_playoff_overrides.py` also validates the shipped config.
- **`preview_week` (optional, `PROSPECTIVE` only):** the week whose games the override previews, when it isn't the publication week. The prompt then names that week and tells the writer to fetch it with `get_weekly_slate`, since the brief's `preview_slate` only holds the publication week.

1. **`SINGLE_GAME_SCHEMATIC_AUTOPSY` Playoff Overrides:**
   - **Week 19:** no override. It looks back at regular-season Week 18, so the base article type applies.
   - **Weeks 20–22 (`PLAYOFF_ELIMINATION_ROUND_AUTOPSY` | `RETROSPECTIVE`):**
     - **Focus:** High-leverage situational football, tightened personnel packages, blitz counters, and scheme-exposure points from the elimination round just completed.
     - **Curatorial Filter:** Isolates the single clash with highest tactical leverage, third-down aggression, or where fourth-down game management swung win probability; a defense exposing an offense counts when the box score shows it (sacks, takeaways, a defensive score).
     - **Defense grounding:** the schematic collision is built from the box score (sacks, TFL, takeaways, pass deflections and the offense's lines), and the high-leverage moments are ones the box score supports (strip-sack, interception, stalled red-zone trip, long or missed field goal), with no invented down-and-distance, pressure rates, coverage percentages, snaps or targets.
     - **Structure:** 4-part Tape Autopsy (`# 1. The Decisive Blueprint`, `## 2. The Core Schematic Collision`, `## 3. The High-Leverage Breakdown`, `### 4. The Elimination Takeaway`).
   - **Week 23 (`SUPER_BOWL_SCHEMATIC_MASTERCLASS` | `PROSPECTIVE`):**
     - **Focus:** Exhaustive pre-game tactical blueprint previewing the Super Bowl clash through an X's and O's coaching lens (disregarding celebrity/media week storylines).
     - **Curatorial Filter:** Trench warfare leverage (pass rush vs. slide protection), safety disguise rotations against primary passing concepts.
     - **Structure:** 5-part Masterclass (`# 1. The Grand Chess Match`, `## 2. Trench Warfare & Point-of-Attack Leverage`, `## 3. Coverage Shells vs. Passing Architecture`, `## 4. The Critical Situational Battle`, `### 5. The Coordinator Verdict`).

2. **`SLATE_LOOKAHEAD_PREDICTIONS` Playoff Overrides:**
   - **Weeks 19–21 (`POSTSEASON_ROUND_LOOKAHEAD` | `PROSPECTIVE`):**
     - **Focus:** High-stakes single-elimination preview capsules for every game in the upcoming playoff round (Wild Card, Divisional, Conference Championship). Strict ban on betting lines/spreads.
     - **Curatorial Filter:** Mandatory full enumeration covering every scheduled tournament matchup. Mismatches use both sides of the ball: each team's defensive leaders from `get_defense_special_teams_leaders` against the quarterback's interceptions and sacks taken, never summed into team totals.
     - **Capsules:** Stat to Know may be offensive, defensive or special teams; Tactical Flashpoint names the players on both sides with their season numbers (no pressure rates, coverage percentages, snaps or targets); the X-Factor can be a defender or specialist.
     - **Structure:** Modular Postseason Capsules (`# 1. The Round Overview`, `## 2. Matchup Enumeration with kickoff time from the slate (no invented broadcast network)`, `3. Structured Sub-Sections: What We're Hearing, Stat to Know, Tactical Flashpoint, The X-Factor Player, Official Prediction`).
   - **Week 22 (`SUPER_BOWL_EARLY_TALE_OF_THE_TAPE` | `PROSPECTIVE`, `preview_week: 23`):**
     - **Focus:** Comprehensive bye-week Tale of the Tape comparing both Super Bowl finalists from top to bottom.
     - **Curatorial Filter:** Contrasts cumulative efficiency profiles across every unit and neutral-site dome variables.
     - **Structure:** 6-part Tale of the Tape (`# 1. The Championship Tale of the Tape`, `## 2. The Macro Profile & Efficiency Baselines`, `## 3. Unit Clash 1: Team A Offense vs. Team B Defense`, `## 4. Unit Clash 2: Team B Offense vs. Team A Defense`, `## 5. The Volatility & Spike Audit`, `### 6. The Bye-Week Installation Mandate`).

3. **`AWARDS_TRACKER` Playoff Overrides** (both `CUMULATIVE`; award voting covers the regular season only, so writers judge Weeks 1–18 and don't credit playoff games; both overrides strictly enforce the **COACHING DATA CITATION** constraint prohibiting explicit mentions of "COTY score", "coach scores", "Coach of the Year metric", or "historical bonus", framing cases in real-world records and achievements):
   - **Week 21 (`AWARDS_FINALISTS`):** three finalists each for MVP, OPOY, OROY, DPOY, DROY and Coach of the Year (coaches from `get_coach_of_the_year_race`), with the projected winner (approx. 1,400–1,700 words; defenders' cases on sacks, TFL, interceptions, takeaways); the snub may be a player or a coach. Structure: `# The Ballots Are In`, one `##` per award with three bolded finalists, `## The Projected Winners`, `### The Snub`.
   - **Week 22 (`AWARDS_WINNERS`):** the publication's winner and runner-up for each of the six awards (approx. 1,400–1,700 words). Structure: `#` headline naming the MVP, a paragraph announcing the six winners, one `##` per award in the order MVP, OPOY, OROY, DPOY, DROY, Coach of the Year (`## MVP: [Player], [Position], [Team]`; `## Coach of the Year: [Coach], [Team]`) with the case and the runner-up, `### The Season in One Line`. Grounded purely in on-field records, margins, and overachievement without engine scores or bonus numbers.

4. **`SUPER_BOWL_CONTENDER_BLUEPRINT` Playoff Overrides** (both `CUMULATIVE`; only teams playing that week's playoff games are in scope, judged on the regular season plus their playoff games so far):
   - **Week 20 (`DIVISIONAL_ROUND_CONTENDER_AUDIT`):** the eight divisional-round teams, three wins from a title. Structure: `# The Championship Standard`, `##` heavyweights (2–3 teams), `## The Vulnerable Favorite`, `### The Dangerous Underdog`.
   - **Week 21 (`FINAL_FOUR_CONTENDER_AUDIT`):** the four conference finalists ranked 1–4 by title chances. Structure: `#` hook, four numbered `## [Rank]. [City] [Team] ([W-L])` sections (path, case, fatal flaw), `### The Verdict` naming the champion.

*(Note: Week 22 Slot 8 commissions `SUPER_BOWL_HISTORICAL_STAKES` directly from `POSTSEASON_EDITORIAL_SLATES` (§7.1, #28) as the dedicated HC-QB legacy column rather than via a dynamic override.)*

### 7.5 Database Schema & Newsroom REST API Specifications
To support seamless frontend newsroom consumption, longform editorial content is persisted with full metadata and relational tagging:

1. **`news_feed` Database Schema:**
   ```sql
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS news_feed (
       id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
       season INT,
       week INT,
       team_id INT,                -- teams.id (no FK constraint in live schema)
       content_type VARCHAR(50),   -- 'ARTICLE' | 'TWEET'
       persona VARCHAR(50),        -- e.g. 'BARNWELL', 'SANDO', 'LOCAL_BEAT_REPORTER'
       headline VARCHAR(255),
       content TEXT,
       created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
       excerpt TEXT,
       article_type VARCHAR(64),   -- e.g. 'MACRO_SLATE_RECAP', 'POWER_RANKINGS'
       slot VARCHAR(32)            -- 'Hero Slot', 'Slot 1'..'Slot 8', 'Standalone', or 'Local Beat N'
   );
   ```

2. **Relational Entity Tagging:**
   - **`article_teams`**: Maps mentioned franchises (`PRIMARY`, `FEATURED`, `MENTIONED`) with mention frequencies. Team mentions come from `newsroom/entity_patterns.team_mention_pattern`: full name, nickname, city (except shared New York / Los Angeles) and uppercase abbreviation, all **case-sensitive**, so ordinary words ("was", "no", "ten", "bills") are not team mentions. Abbreviations that are English words (`NO`, `WAS`, `TEN`, `MIN`, `CAR`, `SEA`, `DEN`) are never matched, even in all caps; those teams are found by nickname or city (`tests/test_entity_patterns.py`). A team is `PRIMARY` if it's the article's assigned team, appears in the headline or a header, or is mentioned 5+ times; `FEATURED` at 3+; otherwise `MENTIONED`.
   - **`article_players`**: Maps mentioned players with their active franchise, position, mention tier, and mention frequencies.

3. **Newsroom REST API Endpoints (`server/api.py`):**
   - **`GET /api/news/week/{week}?season={season}`**:
     - Returns the complete weekly news slate partitioned into canonical display slots:
       - **`hero`**: Hero Slot article (top banner).
       - **`secondary`**: Slots 1 and 2 (e.g. Schematic Autopsy, Slate Lookahead).
       - **`features`**: Slots 3, 4, and 5 (seasonal feature trio).
       - **`local_primers`**: Slots 6, 7, and 8 only (the national desk's local matchup primers, with Slot 6 dedicated to the Crisis Beat in Weeks 2–18).
       - **`standalone`**: Standalone Power Rankings 32-team hierarchy.
       - **`other`**: Any other article that week (e.g. unslotted); the frontend shows these with the main stories.
     - Team-page beat coverage (slot `Local Beat N`, from on-demand team wakeups) is excluded; it appears only via `GET /api/team/{abbr}/news`.
     - Mu Fantasy articles (`article_type` `FANTASY_*`, slots `Fantasy …`) are excluded; they belong to the fantasy page.
     - Includes resolved author objects (`display_name`, `handle`, `archetype`) and attached entity tags (`tagged_teams`, `tagged_players`).
   - **`GET /api/news/article/{article_id}`**:
     - Returns full article text, headline, teaser excerpt, assigned slot, author metadata (`handle`, `display_name`, `archetype`, `bio`, `team_abbr`), `related_articles` (up to 6 articles written that same week powering the "Keep Reading" rail, newest first), and all tagged entities for reading view rendering.
   - **`GET /api/author/{author_key}`**:
     - Returns author profile (`handle`, `display_name`, `archetype`, `bio`, `team`) and full article archive across all weeks and seasons (`articles`, `total_articles`) matching the author's handle, archetype, or agent ID, with tagged teams for each article. Powers the dedicated `#author-{handle}` full-page view.

### 7.6 Stored Weekly Power Rankings (`newsroom/power_rankings.py`)
The Standalone `POWER_RANKINGS` article is the source of the stored weekly ranking:
1. **Continuity in the prompt:** `compile_agent_prompt` adds a "POWER RANKINGS CONTINUITY" block, which lists the latest earlier stored week's order (or says none has been published, so every team is "Previous: — | New") and restates the header format `## [Rank]. [City] [Team] ([W-L]) (Previous: N | Change: ±N)`. This means movement indicators come from real prior rankings. In Week 19 (`FINAL_RANKINGS_WEEK`) the block also tells the writer this is the season's final ranking, judged on the full Weeks 1–18 body of work.
2. **Parsing:** each numbered header that names a team (resolved by its unique nickname, or abbreviation) or carries a W-L record is a team entry. Other numbered headers (e.g. "## 2. The Drop-Off") are treated as section breaks. The bold lead-in is the `kicker`; the first paragraph after it is the `blurb` (a horizontal rule or blank line ends it, so the walk-off isn't included).
3. **Validation:** the article must rank all 32 teams exactly once with ranks 1–32. In a live run, a failing article gets one rewrite in the same chat, with the specific errors (missing/duplicate teams or ranks, unmatched headers).
4. **Storage:** on save to `news_feed`, a valid article replaces that week's `power_rankings` rows. Only the order, kicker and blurb come from the text. `record` is snapshotted from `team_seasons`, and `previous_rank` / `movement` come from the stored previous week, not the writer's indicators. An article that still fails validation is saved as an article but no ranking is stored (logged as `POWER RANKINGS NOT STORED`).
5. **No rankings after the regular season:** Week 19 (entering the Wild Card round, cumulative through Week 18) is the last ranked week (`FINAL_RANKINGS_WEEK = 19`). The postseason slates for Weeks 20–23 don't commission `POWER_RANKINGS`, and if one is generated anyway for a week after 19 (e.g. a targeted manual run) it is saved as an article but not stored (logged as `POWER RANKINGS NOT STORED`), so the Week 19 order stays the final ranking shown by `GET /api/power_rankings`.

### 7.6b Stored Year-End All-Pro Rosters (`newsroom/all_pro.py`)
The Postseason Week 19 `OFFICIAL_AP_ALL_PRO_ROSTER` article is the source of the stored All-Pro awards in the `awards` table:
1. **Formatting & Structure:** The article structure directive mandates Markdown bullets formatted `- **[Slot]**: [Player Full Name], [Team] — [1-sentence rationale]` across 4 sections: First-Team Offense (13 slots: QB, RB, FB, 3 WRs, TE, AP, 5 OL), First-Team Defense & Special Teams (12 defense + 6 special teams = 18 slots), Second-Team Offense (13 slots), and Second-Team Defense & Special Teams (18 slots). Total: 62 selections (31 First-Team, 31 Second-Team).
2. **Parsing & Resolution:** `parse_all_pro(text, roster_index)` resolves each bullet's player by matching Player Name + Team + Position compatibility against `load_roster_index(cur, season)` (from `player_seasons`, `players`, and `teams`). This disambiguates common surnames and players sharing a name on the same team (e.g. WR vs DT).
3. **Validation & In-Flight Retry:** Checks that all 31 First-Team and 31 Second-Team slots are filled, all players resolve to active roster players, no player is selected multiple times for the same slot, and no player holds multiple primary offensive or defensive positions (AP and Special Teams exceptions are permitted). If validation fails, `swarm_dispatcher` sends `all_pro_fix_prompt` for a one-shot rewrite within the active chat session before publishing.
4. **Storage & Downstream Exposure:** On save to `news_feed`, `store_all_pro_teams` deletes previous All-Pro rows for that season and inserts 62 rows into `awards` (`award_type` = `'First Team All-Pro'` or `'Second Team All-Pro'`, `position` = slot). These honors are immediately returned in `get_player_season_stats` / `get_player_context` (`career_awards`) with their position (e.g. `"2035 First Team All-Pro (QB)"`) and contribute 30 points (First Team) and 15 points (Second Team) to Awards Equity ($A_{\text{awards}}$) in `engines/player_popularity_engine.py`.
5. **Honorifics & Earned Hardware Directive:** `swarm_dispatcher.py` instructs all writers to check `career_awards` in player profiles and use earned honorifics in prose ("the reigning MVP", "two-time First-Team All-Pro cornerback", "former Rookie of the Year") while strictly prohibiting fabricated awards.

### 7.6c Stored Year-End Award Winners (`newsroom/award_winners.py`)
The Postseason Week 22 `AWARDS_TRACKER` (`AWARDS_WINNERS` override) article is the source of the six major regular-season award winners stored in the `awards` table:
1. **Formatting & Structure:** The article structure directive mandates six level-2 (`##`) headers announcing the winners in order: `## MVP: [Player], [Position], [Team]`, `## OPOY: [Player], [Position], [Team]`, `## OROY: [Player], [Position], [Team]`, `## DPOY: [Player], [Position], [Team]`, `## DROY: [Player], [Position], [Team]`, and `## Coach of the Year: [Coach], [Team]` (or `## COTY: ...`).
2. **Parsing & Resolution:** `parse_award_winners(text, roster_index, coach_index)` matches player recipients against `load_roster_index(cur, season)` by name, team, and position, and matches the coach recipient against `load_coach_index(cur, season)` by name and team.
3. **Validation & In-Flight Retry:** Checks that all 6 awards (`MVP`, `OPOY`, `OROY`, `DPOY`, `DROY`, `COTY`) are present and resolve to real active roster players and head coaches. If validation fails, `swarm_dispatcher` sends `award_winners_fix_prompt` for an in-flight rewrite within the active chat session before publishing, which also reminds writers never to cite internal engine mechanics (COTY scores, bonus numbers) in the Coach of the Year section.
4. **Storage:** On save to `news_feed`, `store_award_winners` deletes previous regular-season award rows for that season and inserts 6 rows into `awards` (`award_type` = `'MVP'`, `'OPOY'`, `'OROY'`, `'DPOY'`, `'DROY'`, `'COTY'`). `COTY` rows record `coach_id`, `coach_name`, and `team_id`; player award rows record `player_id`, `player_name`, `team_id`, and `position`. `SBMVP` is parsed and recorded from the post-Super Bowl `SUPER_BOWL_GAME_RECAP` article (§7.6d).
5. **Downstream Exposure:** Player awards immediately contribute to Awards Equity ($A_{\text{awards}}$: MVP 60 pts, OPOY/DPOY 40 pts, OROY/DROY 25 pts) in `engines/player_popularity_engine.py`, appear in player profile `career_awards`, and are cited by writer tools. Coach of the Year is visible in `engines/coach_profile.py` (`awards_visible`).

### 7.6d Stored Super Bowl MVP (`newsroom/award_winners.py`)
The post-Super Bowl `SUPER_BOWL_GAME_RECAP` article is the authoritative source of the Super Bowl MVP honor stored in the `awards` table:
1. **Formatting & Structure:** The article structure directive mandates an exact level-2 (`##`) header awarding the Pete Rozelle Trophy / Super Bowl MVP: `## Super Bowl MVP: [Player Full Name], [Position], [Team Name]`.
2. **Parsing & Resolution:** `parse_sb_mvp(text, roster_index)` extracts the recipient from the header (supporting flexible variations like `## Super Bowl MVP`, `### Super Bowl MVP`, `Pete Rozelle Trophy / Super Bowl MVP`, and `SBMVP`) and resolves the player against active rosters using `resolve_player_award`.
3. **Validation & In-Flight Retry:** Checks that the Super Bowl MVP header is present and resolves to a real active roster player. If validation fails, `swarm_dispatcher` sends `sb_mvp_fix_prompt` for an in-flight rewrite within the active chat session before publishing.
4. **Storage:** On save to `news_feed`, `store_sb_mvp` deletes any previous `SBMVP` row for that season and inserts a single row into `awards` (`award_type` = `'SBMVP'`, `player_id`, `player_name`, `team_id`, and `position`).
5. **Downstream Exposure:** Stored `SBMVP` honors immediately contribute 25 points to Awards Equity ($A_{\text{awards}}$) in `engines/player_popularity_engine.py`, 40 points to Playoff Clout ($S_{\text{playoff}}$) in `engines/playoff_clout_engine.py`, appear in player profile `career_awards` (`get_player_context`), and feed draft ADP bumps.

### 7.7 Evolving Agent Memory (`newsroom/agent_memory.py`)
Every agent (national writers, beat reporters, pundits and fans) keeps its hand-written identity line in `agent_memories.persistent_memory`, which never changes (the one exception: `2026_10_22_agent_wire_social.sql` swapped the name inside the line for agents it renamed). Three evolving layers sit on top of it, written by `db/migrations/2026_10_20_agent_memory.sql`'s tables:
1. **Takes log (`agent_memory_entries`).** After a live post is saved (`SwarmDispatcher._record_agent_memory`): a tweet is stored as a take as-is (no extra call); an article gets one extra model call (`takes_extraction_prompt`) that returns up to 3 first-person stances the writer committed to, each tagged with a team (resolved by nickname) and/or player.
2. **Prediction track record (`agent_predictions`).** For `PROSPECTIVE` articles, `parse_score_predictions` reads `Projected Final: Team N, Team N` lines (and, for the Super Bowl Roundtable, `Panellist: Team N, Team N` lines, labelled by panellist) and stores each pick against the matching game in the article's preview week (`preview_week` for overrides). Picks are graded when a prompt is built: correct, wrong, push (a predicted or actual tie) or pending.
3. **Season summaries (`agent_season_summaries`).** At the start of each live weekly run, agents with at least 3 new takes since their last summary get it rewritten through last week (one call each, ≤120 words, first person). Each rewrite is a new row per `through_week`, so older weeks keep the summary they saw.

**In the prompt:** under "PERSISTENT WORLDVIEW & MEMORIES", after the identity line, `build_memory_block` adds the latest summary, the 6 most recent takes ("stay consistent, or say plainly that you've changed your mind"), up to 3 earlier takes about the article's subject team, and the track record: the season's winner-picking record plus the 5 most recent graded picks with the final score, telling the writer to own misses in their own voice. Only memories from before the article's publication week are used, and graded results only count games from earlier weeks, so regenerating an old week can't see the future. If the memory tables don't exist yet, prompts simply have no memory block.

**Failure handling:** memory recording runs after the article is committed and never affects it; failures are logged as `MEMORY NOT RECORDED`. Deleting `news_feed` rows cascades to their takes and predictions; `db/clear_news_feed.sh` also clears the summaries. Tests: `tests/test_agent_memory.py`; `tools/verify_newsroom.py` builds memory blocks for each kind of agent against the live database, and exercises the whole write path (parse and store a prediction, store takes and a tweet take, check the week cutoff, gather summary inputs, store a summary, read the graded block back) inside a transaction it rolls back.

### 7.8 Agent Wire Social Desk (`newsroom/social_desk.py`, `docs/SOCIAL_MATRIX.md`)

**Status:** build steps 3–6 are built: planning, the post writer (JR chose Gemini 3.8 Flash for every post kind after the side-by-side test), publishing from the weekly run and team wakeups, and the `/api/social/*` endpoints (§4). Step 7, the Agent Wire frontend on live data, is built (§5). First live week: 2035 Week 18 (Oct 14), 100 posts and 20 trends. After it, JR asked for a player-first mix and 20 ex-player accounts (Oct 14; `db/migrations/2026_10_25_former_players.sql`), described below.

- **What it does:** `load_week_context(cur, season, week)` reads one publication week (including each recap game's script, `ctx["game_scripts"]`, §2.14; a game's facts carry it as `how_it_happened`: storyline, halftime, winning score, key moments); `plan_social_week(ctx)` returns about 100 assignments, each an account, a post kind, a subject and a **facts packet** (no model call, nothing written). Week N looks back at Week N-1's completed games and ahead to Week N's games; standings and season totals count only games before Week N, and a game in Week N or later never shows a score, so replanning an old week can't leak results.
- **Buckets (player-first mix, JR Oct 14):** shares (one per article stored for the week, from the writer's account; Skip's column shares from `SKIP_TWEET`, the roundtable panel never shares; local beat articles from one of that team's reporters); **reactions only from die-hards, one per team that played, no extras** (only die-hards post about a team as a team); **performances 14** (top full-PPR offensive lines, with big defensive and kicking games mixed in: the 2nd, 5th and 8th ordinary (non-buzz) performance post goes to the next of up to `DEF_PERFORMANCE_MAX` = 3 lines from the recap week's captured box scores (`ctx["defense_lines"]` from `game_stat_lines`, games with no captured defensive or kicking lines contribute none) that score `DEF_PERFORMANCE_MIN` = 12+ on `defense_play_score` (6/sack, 8/INT, 4/forced fumble, 3/recovery, 12/defensive TD, 6/safety; kicking 3/FG made, +3 per 50+ make, +12 for a 55+ yarder; the bigger of defense and kicking), biggest first; e.g. 2 sacks, an INT plus a forced fumble, a defensive TD, 4 field goals or a 55-yarder. Their facts carry the game line (`defense_stat_line`), game, result and opponent but no season totals or fantasy points; pundit preferences `DEF` and `K` in `PUNDIT_AFFINITY`. Buzz posts come first and a busy week trims the bucket to buzz only (2035 Week 18), so defensive days get posts only in weeks with ordinary slots), written by fantasy accounts, pundits, ex-players and bandwagon fans in rotation, never die-hards; **injuries (`BEAT_INJURY`, `INJURY_SHOCK`; `load_week_context` ranks starters among on-roster players and drops players released by the publication week):** beat accounts post factual reports on injured starters with recovery timetables; die-hard fan and meme accounts react in shock, panic or gloom when marquee superstars ($\ge 88$ OVR, $\ge 50$ Pop, starting QBs) suffer significant injuries; **bad games 8** (`BAD_GAME`: players who averaged 12+ full-PPR points over 3+ earlier games and scored at most 65% of that, biggest drop first, then QBs with 3+ interceptions; players with no line are skipped, since they may have been inactive), mostly bandwagon fans clowning the flop, plus pundits, an ex-player and @GridironMemes; **comparisons 10** (`COMPARISON`): 5 die-hards argue their team's best player beats last week's opponent's best at the same position, 3 ex-players rank the league's top five at their old position with their old team's best player alongside, pundits fill the rest with top-five debates; beat notes 5; standings 2 (from Week 3: a pundit and a bandwagon fan); previews 6 (fans of the top projected games, plus 2 fantasy start/sit posts in Weeks 2–17); memes 2; replies 10–15 (placeholders; parents are picked after writing). Replies flex to land the week near 100; if a full slate with a big article week is still over, performances shrink to no fewer than 10. Player names stored in capitals ("JERRY RAYFORD") are posted in normal case, and season ranks say RB, not HB.
- **What the league is talking about (JR, Oct 14; `Week.player_buzz`, `Week.team_stories`; frequencies checked against the 2035 season):**
  - **Player games**, tier 1 ("the league is talking"): 400+ passing yards, 5+ passing TDs, 150+ rushing or receiving yards (TE 90+), 3+ total TDs or 40+ full-PPR points. Tier 2 ("historic"): 6+ passing TDs, 200+ rushing or receiving yards (TE 150+), 4+ total TDs, 50+ PPR, or a tier-1 game that is the season's best by full-PPR points at its position (from Week 4). Season context in the regular season: round numbers crossed that week (5,000 passing yards, 50 passing TDs, 1,500/2,000 rushing yards, 1,500 receiving yards and 20 total TDs make news alone; 4,000 passing yards, 40 TDs, 1,000 yards and 15 TDs are only mentioned when the game is already news), NFL single-season records (breaking one is tier 2; being on pace for or already past one is tier 1 from Week 12, context before that). **Record book (§2.9):** when the record tables exist, `load_week_context` loads the book as it stood before the season (`load_record_book`: franchise records and all-time entries; league-wide records and top 10s only for categories with full real-NFL coverage, so the record book's partial categories never claim an NFL rank), and the desk uses it instead of the hardcoded `SEASON_RECORDS` / `TEAM_POINTS_RECORD` (kept as the fallback). A season total that breaks the player's franchise record that week is tier 1 ("the most passing TDs in a season in Panthers history: 49 (old record 47, George Crawford, 2034)"), one already past it is context, a tie of the NFL record is tier 2, and a season that climbs into the NFL top 10 is context (tier 1 from Week 12 for a top-3 season). Single games are checked against the NFL record and top 10 (tie or break tier 2, top 10 tier 1) and the franchise's single-game record where the book has one (tier 1). This season's other players and earlier games count too, so a teammate who already passed a record keeps it. Team storylines add franchise records for wins and points ("Most points in a season in Steelers history: 468 (old record 436, the 2014 Pittsburgh Steelers)") and take the NFL points record from the book. Ties among many holders read "shared with 9 players, most recently Sam Darnold, 2029". Tier 1 gets three performance posts (pundit, ex-player preferring the player's team and position, bandwagon fan), tier 2 four (plus a fantasy account) and a comparison on where the game ranks among the season's best at the position. Facts carry `why_people_are_talking` (up to 3 reasons, with "the 2nd-most in a game this season" ranks) and `how_big`; each post gets its own angle. These posts sit at the front of their buckets; ordinary performances fill the rest. QBs with 2+ interceptions in a loss count as bad games (no QB threw 3 in a 2035 game).
  - **Team storylines (`TEAM_STORY`, regular season, teams that played last week; up to 8 posts):** unbeaten or winless from 5 games (tier 2 from 10, with real NFL history in `nfl_history_through_2025`: the 1972 Dolphins and 2007 Patriots, the 2008 Lions and 2017 Browns), winning or losing streaks of 6+ (tier 2 from 10), a 6+ game winning streak ending (tier 2 for the first loss after an 8-0 or better start), a first win after 0-5 or worse, wins by 35+, 50+ points, upsets (a 0.35+ gap in winning percentage before the game, both teams with 5+ games) and the points record (606, 2013 Broncos: on pace from 6 games, past it, or broken that week as tier 2). One story per team with up to 3 reasons. Good news: tier 1 a pundit and a bandwagon fan, tier 2 two pundits (the second takes the skeptic's case), the team's ex-player if it has one, and a bandwagon fan. Bad news: the meme page and pundits, plus bandwagon fans hopping off and an ex-player at tier 2. Filed under the team, so they show in its Team Hub. A team wakeup for a team with a storyline always gets at least one national voice, and that post is the storyline. Agent Wire labels them "Storyline".
  - **Busy weeks:** to stay near 100, the planner trims the last-added performances (to 10), comparisons (to 7), bad games (to 5), storylines (to 4) and move posts (to 1), in that order. In 2035 Weeks 2–18 that lands every week at 100 or 101.
  - **Engagement:** `buzz_heat` in `social_desk.json` multiplies a buzz post's subject heat (tier 1 ×1.5, tier 2 ×2.2), and `TEAM_STORY` has kind factor 1.3, so these posts draw the replies and lead the trends.
- **Roster moves (team management step 5; `load_moves`, docs/team_management/05_NEWSROOM_INTEGRATION_PLAN.md §2.8):** `load_week_context` adds `ctx["moves"]`, the moves new for the publication week (`roster_moves.new_this_week` over `db_tools.fetch_moves`, annotated with notability; each with its move line, the money strings in it, the acting team, the new depth slot and the player's seasons with that team) and `ctx["team_caps"]` (each team's cap line with its basis); missing tables mean no moves (a savepoint keeps the connection usable). Four kinds, window Mon – Wed, facts packets only (every term and figure is in the move or cap line):
  - `MOVE_NEWS`: the insiders (`SCHEFTER_TWEET`, `RAPOPORT`, `Accounts.insider`, under the pundit cap) break the week's most notable moves, at most `MOVE_NEWS_MAX` = 3, most notable first; planned right after shares so the insiders aren't used up first. With `CAP_TAKE` it fills the `MOVES` bucket.
  - `BEAT_MOVE`: a beat team with a notable move gets it instead of its plain `BEAT` note; a starter injury (`BEAT_INJURY`) still comes first. Facts: the team's move lines and its cap line. `plan_team_wakeup` gives the first beat reporter not filing the injury report a `BEAT_MOVE` the same way.
  - `MOVE_REACTION`: a die-hard of the acting team on each of the `MOVE_REACTION_MAX` = 2 most notable moves, appended to reactions like `INJURY_SHOCK` (`seasons_with_your_team` when he's had 2+ seasons there and is leaving, staying or restructuring).
  - `CAP_TAKE`: one pundit, only when a team is over the cap (the deepest one) or, failing that, a release this week left $10M+ dead money (`CAP_TAKE_DEAD_MONEY`). Facts: the team's cap line (and the release line).
  - Kind factors (`social_desk.json`): `MOVE_NEWS` 1.4, `BEAT_MOVE` 1.0, `MOVE_REACTION` 1.3, `CAP_TAKE` 1.1. `docs/SOCIAL_MATRIX.md` §2 doesn't list these kinds yet: the sub-plan's table text for it wasn't among JR's §2.6 approvals.
- **Ex-players (`FORMER_PLAYER`, 20 accounts):** real retired stars plus 2026 NFL Top 50 players who aren't on a 2035 roster (treated as retired before 2035), written "in the style of" like the pundits: Mahomes, Josh Allen, Justin Jefferson, Ja'Marr Chase, McCaffrey, Saquon, Micah Parsons, T.J. Watt, Derrick Henry, Dak, Maxx Crosby, Herbert, Brady, Marshawn Lynch, Cam Newton, Vick, Brees, Strahan, Calvin Johnson, Larry Fitzgerald. `assigned_team_id` is the old team and the identity line says "I played <POS> for the <team>"; the planner reads the position from it. Their posts are league-wide and never filed under the old team (outside its team wakeup). Every assignment by an ex-player, and by the ex-player pundits (`FORMER_TEAM_SLANT`: Ochocinco CIN, Shannon DEN, J.J. Watt HOU, RG3 WAS, Clark PIT, McAfee IND), carries `slant_team`, which the writer turns into a "soft spot for your old team" line. Followers 0.8–6M, like rate 0.004. Agent Wire labels them "Former <team>" with an orange badge.
- **Postseason (Week 19+):** a few extra reactions, then the room goes to `PLAYER` posts by fans of eliminated teams (their own team's top players, alternating with players still alive). Alive teams are this week's scheduled teams, or last week's playoff winners in the Super Bowl bye week. **Preseason** (no completed games): every team's fans preview their opener, then pundits. **Fantasy season over (Weeks 19–23 / playoffs / offseason):** the fantasy football season ends after Week 18 (`fantasy_phase(week) is None`). During playoffs and offseason, tweets about fantasy are never generated: fantasy accounts (`FANTASY_GURU`, `BERRY`, `YATES`) and the `fantasy` role are completely disabled in account selection, buzz assignments (tier 2 buzz uses a pundit rather than fantasy), performance rotations, and replies; fantasy article shares and previews are suppressed; facts packets omit `fantasy_points_ppr`, prior PPR averages, and PPR-based rankings (ranking by real yardage/TDs instead); prompts enforce a mandatory rule forbidding fantasy mentions; and `publishable` / `save_post` enforce hard blocking against fantasy terminology.
- **Accounts:** least-used first under weekly caps (pundit 3, fantasy 4, beat 2, fan 2, bandwagon 3, ex-player 3), with position affinities for pundits (e.g. QB → RG3, Cowherd, Stephen A). The pundit pool mirrors `TWEET_EXCLUDED_ARCHETYPES` and leaves out all fantasy accounts (`FANTASY_ARCHETYPES`: `FANTASY_GURU`, `BERRY`, `YATES`), memes, and draft accounts, so fantasy analysts are never drawn as general pundits. Deterministic for a season and week.
- **Dry run:** `python3 -m newsroom.social_desk --season 2035 --week 18 [--json plan.json]` prints the plan; when the week's slates aren't stored yet it plans placeholder shares, one per national and fantasy slot.
- **Writer (`newsroom/social_writer.py`, build step 4):** `build_post_prompt` puts the account's tweet persona (`agent_personas.json` `archetypes`, tuned for loose conversational perspective rather than formulaic catchphrases), its identity line and memory block, and `SOCIAL_RULES` (one post of at most 280 characters, numbers only from the facts packet, no invented injuries or betting lines; contract figures, signings, releases and trades only from the facts, a trade done only if the facts say it was executed, a rumor kept a rumor, never an invented contract figure (JR's R12), a game's `how_it_happened` never contradicted, facts treated as background boundaries rather than a stat-checklist, cadence/syntax variety guidelines forbidding repetitive LLM clichés like "expeditiously", "pure cardio", or formulaic jersey buying/trashing, and everything else free to invent the way real people post: what they saw, a play they can't stop thinking about, hot takes, jokes, rumors framed as rumors, grudges; no tools and no metric names even if the persona mentions them, no ratings or Madden, at most 2 hashtags; and in postseason/offseason, a strict prohibition on fantasy football or PPR mentions) in the system instruction, and a per-kind task plus the facts packet in the prompt (replies also get the parent post). For non-fantasy personas, `build_post_prompt` sanitizes raw fantasy metrics (`fantasy_points_ppr`, prior PPR averages, decimal points in buzz facts) so real-football accounts never quote decimal fantasy stats. `write_post` makes one Gemini call with no tools and asks once for a shorter version if the post runs over 280 characters; `clean_post` strips labels, quotes, markdown and links, keeps 2 hashtags and flags problems (length, ratings, fantasy mentions after season over, "expeditiously"). Facts packets describe a game's excitement in words ("an entertaining game") and never carry the index. Usage and cost come from `usage_metadata` (`PRICES`: 3.8 Flash $0.75/$3.75 per 1M until Dec 31, 2026; 3.5 Flash-Lite $0.30/$2.50).
- **Money guard (`social_writer.allowed_money` / `clean_post(..., allowed=)`):** every post is checked against the dollar amounts in its facts packet (and, for a reply, its parent's text), compared to the nearest $10K ("$118M" = "$118 million"); a dollar figure that isn't there is a problem (`MONEY_PROBLEM`). `write_post` asks for its one rewrite when a post runs long or names such a figure (one call covers both), and `social_publish.publishable` drops a post that still does (`[NOT SAVED]`).
- **Model comparison:** `python3 tools/social_model_compare.py [--week 9] [--models gemini-3.8-flash gemini-3.5-flash-lite]` writes the same 20 planned posts (6 reactions, 3 performances, 2 beat notes, 1 standings, 2 previews incl. a fantasy start/sit, 2 memes, 2 shares when the week has stored articles, and 2 replies from rival fans) with each model, prints cost per post, and saves posts, prompts and usage to `captures/social_compare/`. It reads the database and writes nothing to it.
- **Publishing (`newsroom/social_publish.py`, build step 5):**
  - `run_social_week(conn_info, season, week)` (called by `weekly_run` after the slates): skips a week that already has posts with slot `Agent Wire` (unless `force`), plans, writes the non-reply posts in parallel (5 workers), then plans and writes the reply pass, then refreshes trends. `dry_run` prints the plan only. Posts with an API error, no text, over 280 characters after the one rewrite, a ratings/Madden mention, or a fantasy mention after the fantasy season are logged `[NOT SAVED]` and skipped.
  - `run_team_social(conn_info, season, week, team_id, local_articles)` (called by `dispatch_team_local` on live, saved wakeups): `plan_team_wakeup` gives each of the team's beat reporters a distinct note cycling through `BEAT_ANGLES` (film notes, locker room vibes, coordinator adjustments, practice observations, injury context) to avoid duplicate reporter angles, each die-hard a reaction to last week's game or a preview of this week's (alternating), 3 pundits if the team's popularity is above 70 or 1 above 55 (each posts about one of the team's top lines last week, or a top-five debate at its best player's position), the team's ex-player, if it has one, on a top-five debate at his old position, and a share of each local article written in that wakeup by its reporter; agents who already posted in an earlier wakeup for the team and week are skipped. Saved with slot `Agent Wire Local`.
  - **Deduplication guards:** `_publish` inspects existing tweets via `find_existing_tweet_for_assignment` prior to generation, reusing already published posts for downstream reply planning and skipping redundant Gemini calls; `save_post` enforces content-level deduplication (`find_duplicate_content_tweet`, comparing normalized text for the author, season, and week) and assignment deduplication, returning `None` and logging `[DUPLICATE SKIPPED]` without writing duplicate rows to `news_feed`. In postseason/offseason, `save_post` also refuses any post with a fantasy archetype or fantasy terms.
  - **Saving:** each post is a `news_feed` row (`content_type` 'TWEET', `persona` = archetype, `agent_id`, `post_kind`, `posted_at`, `reply_to_id`, `linked_article_id` for shares, `subject_game_id`, `subject_player_id`, `likes`, `reposts`, `reply_count`, `views`, `hashtags`). Teams are tagged by `EntityTaggingService` from the text plus both teams of the post's game; a player subject is tagged `PRIMARY`. Every post except a share is stored as the author's take (`agent_memory.store_tweet_take`).
  - **Replies (`social_desk.plan_replies`):** the 20 replies go to the 8 most-liked posts (3, 3, 3, 3, 2, 2, 2, 2). A fan's post gets the other team's fans first, then its own team's fans and bandwagon fans; a pundit's or writer's gets a set foil (`CONTRARIAN`, e.g. Shannon ↔ Skip, Stephen A ↔ McAfee, Barnwell → Skip, Sando → Cowherd), then fans of the teams involved; a meme gets the roasted team's fans; a beat note gets that team's fans. Replies count against the weekly caps and carry the parent's facts and text.
  - **Variety:** posts receive dynamic creative perspectives framed as guidance rather than rigid templates. Dedicated angle pools include expanded `FAN_ANGLES` (legacy claims/debates, "best player in the league" / "best at position" / "best all-time", pre-game dread of opponent stars, attacking players / receipts, trauma/PTSD, apology forms, coaching hot seats), `BANDWAGON_ANGLES` (jumping on/off, front-runner flexing without repetitive jersey buying tropes), `BEAT_ANGLES` (film notes, locker room vibes, adjustments), `PREVIEW_ANGLES` (key matchups, matchup fear, legacy stakes), and `BUZZ_ANGLES`. Rules discourage stock fan phrases ("casuals", "real fans", "plastic franchise", "bleed … forever") and clichés. Replies to the same post take different stances in turn: push back, agree and add, joke. The first live week (Oct 14) showed nearly every fan post leaning on the same phrases and four near-identical bandwagon replies to one Barnwell share, which these rules fix.
  - **Simulated time (`posted_at`):** Week 1's Thursday is the first Thursday after Labor Day of the season year, each week 7 days later; kickoffs come from `games.game_time` (default Sunday 1:00 PM) and games last 3h10m. Reactions land 5–90 minutes after their game ends, game memes 30 minutes–10 hours after, performances and bad games between the game's end and Monday night, storylines and move posts Monday 9:00–Wednesday 20:00, comparisons Tuesday 9:00–Thursday 20:00, beat notes, shares and draft posts Tuesday morning–Wednesday evening, standings Tuesday, postseason player posts Tuesday–Saturday, previews from Thursday (at most two days before kickoff) until an hour before kickoff, fantasy start/sit Friday–Sunday morning, replies 3–240 minutes after their parent. Planned posts (everything except reactions, game memes and replies) that land between 1:00 and 6:59 move to 7:00–9:59 that morning. Seeded per post, so a rerun gives the same times.
  - **Engagement:** `followers` is set once per account (`agent_memories.followers`, seeded by `agent_id`): insiders (Schefter, Rapoport) 1–5M, other national voices 0.5–3M, The Flex Spot 250–600K, @GridironMemes 600K–1M, @MockDraftMarty 50–150K, beat reporters 20–150K and fans 200–25K scaled by their team's popularity, bandwagon fans 500–8K. Likes = followers × the role's like rate × subject heat (game excitement and team popularity, player popularity, or the shared article's slot) × a kind factor (memes 1.6, replies 0.25) × log-normal noise; reposts, reply counts and views are ratios of likes. Stored on the row, so they never change between loads.
  - **Trends (`refresh_trends`):** after every weekly run and team wakeup, the week's teams, players, games and hashtags are scored by summed engagement (likes + 2 × reposts + 3 × replies) with a recency weight, weighted by kind (players 1.3, games 1.2, teams 0.8, hashtags 0.5), and need a minimum number of posts (games 3, others 2). Generic hashtags (#NFL, #Week17 and the like) never trend. The top 20 replace the week's `social_trends` rows; titles are templates ("Bills–Chiefs", the player's name, "#BillsMafia") and the description is the top post's text.
  - **Settings:** `newsroom/config/social_desk.json` holds the model (and per-kind overrides), workers, follower ranges, like rates, kind factors, share-slot heat and the trend weights and stoplist. `db/clear_news_feed.sh` also backs up and clears `social_trends` when it deletes posts.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_social_api.py` (post shape, labels, For You order), `tests/test_social_publish.py` (calendar, post windows, followers, engagement, publishable posts, fantasy suppression after season over, trends, replies, team wakeups, tweet deduplication guards for normalized content and assignments, pundit pool vs the dispatcher), `tests/test_social_writer.py` (cleanup, prompts, old-team slant, cost) and `tests/test_social_desk.py` (synthetic league: 100 posts, one die-hard reaction per team and no one else reacting, the player-first mix, bad games, comparisons, ex-players not filed under their old team, name tidying, buzz tiers and their extra posts, common milestones not being news, a record broken, team storylines (perfect and winless runs, a first loss after a perfect start, team wakeups), no storylines before games are played, defensive and kicking performances mixed into the bucket (2nd/5th ordinary post, biggest first, ordinary lines and uncaptured games left out), share accounts, no score leaks, caps, fantasy weeks, complete fantasy suppression in playoffs and offseason (`test_no_fantasy_tweets_in_playoffs_or_offseason`), determinism, preseason, postseason and the Super Bowl bye).

### 7.9 On-Demand Custom Article Commissioning (`newsroom/custom_commission.py`)
Provides autonomous and interactive on-demand article generation from user prompts, accessible via CLI and the `#debug` Control tab ("Commission Custom Story"):
- **Two-Step Editorial Flow:**
  1. *Prompt Classification (`classify_prompt`):* A fast `gemini-3.8-flash` structured pass analyzes the user's prompt, publication season, and week. It resolves:
     - `target_desk`: `NATIONAL` (macro recaps, schematic autopsies, player deep profiles, columns, coach hot seats, beat coverage) or `FANTASY` (waiver wire, start/sit, rankings, sleepers/busts, draft tiers).
     - `article_type_id`: One of 33 national or 25 fantasy types from `newsroom/config/article_types.json`.
     - `writer_archetype`: Eligible writer matching the format and tone (e.g. Joyner for film, Barnwell for regression, Kimes for scheme, Sando for front office, Berry/Yates for fantasy, or Local Beat Reporter for team beats).
     - `team_id`: Target franchise ID if team-specific, else `None`.
     - `desk_note`: Mandatory directive specifying the exact angle, players, and matchups to investigate.
     - `rationale`: 1-sentence analytical reasoning for the proposed pairing.
  2. *Swarm Execution (`commission_custom_article`):* Dispatches the writer agent via `SwarmDispatcher` with the full ReAct database toolset (28+ tools for national, `FANTASY_TOOLS` for fantasy). National articles are saved to `news_feed` with `slot="Special Commission"`, routing to `slotted_response["other"]` on `GET /api/news/week/{week}` and appearing in the expanded "This Week's Stories" grid (`showAllStories` in `App.tsx`). Fantasy articles are saved with `slot="Fantasy Spotlight"` and appear in `/api/fantasy/articles` and on the Mu Fantasy hub.
- **CLI Usage:** `python3 -m newsroom.custom_commission --prompt "..." [--season N] [--week N] [--desk NATIONAL|FANTASY] [--type T] [--writer W] [--team ID] [--desk-note "..."] [--classify-only] [--dry-run]`
- **REST Endpoints (`server/debug_api.py`):**
  - `POST /api/debug/classify_article`: returns proposal and catalog dropdown lists.
  - `POST /api/debug/commission_article`: starts background job in `captures/jobs/` with live log streaming and attaches `url_hash` (`#article-{id}` or `#article-fantasy-{id}`) upon completion.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_custom_commission.py`.

### 7.10 The Offseason Editorial Package (Week 24: "Offseason") (`newsroom/offseason_service.py`, `newsroom/offseason_run.py`, `docs/OFFSEASON_EDITORIAL_SLATE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`)
Follows the franchise from the Super Bowl through the first round of the next NFL Draft. Published as a unified 6-article package under the concluded season and synthetic publication week 24 (labeled "Offseason" in `App.tsx` and the edition bar), while decoupling publication time from each story's narrative anchor date (`event_date + 1 week`) and enforcing an anti-leakage information cutoff:
- **National Desk Grid Slots (1 Hero, 2 Secondary, 3 Features):**
  - Stored under canonical newsroom slot keys (`Hero Slot`, `Slot 1`, `Slot 2`, `Slot 3`, `Slot 4`, `Slot 5`) in `news_feed`, with `server/api.py` categorizing `Hero Slot` into `hero`, `Slot 1` & `Slot 2` (and `Secondary *`) into `secondary`, and `Slot 3`, `Slot 4`, `Slot 5` (and `Feature *`) into `features`.
  - In `Mu_Network/src/App.tsx`, Week 24 ("Offseason") renders a full-width editorial layout (`gridTemplateColumns: "1fr"`) hiding the in-season sidebar (`Trending Local Coverage` and `Power Rankings`), featuring:
    - `Hero Slot`: `SUPER_BOWL_GAME_RECAP` (`BARNWELL`, `ROSENTHAL`, `CHADIHA`) — defining turn, box scores, turning points, official Super Bowl MVP; zero post-game event leakage.
    - `Slot 1` (Secondary): `SUPER_BOWL_CHAMPION_RETROSPECTIVE` (`CHADIHA`, `SANDO`, `ROSENTHAL`) — full-season title arc, regular-season identity translation to January. Displayed under `Secondary Slots (1 & 2) · Retrospective & Loss Analysis`.
    - `Slot 2` (Secondary): `SUPER_BOWL_RUNNER_UP_POSTMORTEM` (`SANDO`, `BARNWELL`) — tactical diagnosis of championship defeat, top offseason mandate.
    - `Slot 3` (Feature): `COACHING_CAROUSEL_AUDIT` (`SANDO`, `BARNWELL`) — grades the offseason hiring cycle; evaluates head coach slants (`OFFENSIVE`, `DEFENSIVE`, `CEO`), coordinator origins (e.g. ex-Panthers OC), and scheme inheritance rules (imported offensive scheme preserves existing defense under DC, and vice versa; `docs/COACH_SLANTS_AND_COORDINATORS_PLAN.md`); zero offseason free-agency, offseason trades or draft (in-season moves are season history, R3) leakage. Displayed under `Feature Slots (3, 4 & 5) · Offseason In-Depth Audits`.
    - `Slot 4` (Feature): `FREE_AGENCY_SIGNING_GRADES` (`YATES`, `BARNWELL`, `SANDO`) — grades curated marquee veteran additions (90+ OVR only); strict ban on numeric OVR (converted to qualitative tiers: 95+ "Tier 1 superstar / cornerstone", 90-94 "High-impact marquee starter / Pro Bowl caliber"); "All-Pro" descriptor is strictly forbidden unless verified in the database `awards` table; zero draft leakage.
    - `Slot 5` (Feature): `NFL_DRAFT_CLASS_RECAP` (`MEL_KIPER`, `YATES`, `BARNWELL`) — Round 1 deep-dive focus (picks 1–32) using actual results from `draft_prospects` (`actual_pick`, `drafted_team_id`, `actual_round`, `actual_round_pick`) comparing against `projected_pick` to highlight steals and reaches, backed by full multi-round draft class context across all 7 rounds (224+ picks) stored in `draft_prospects`.
- **Service & Manifest (`newsroom/offseason_service.py`):**
  - All-in-one manifest parser (`captures/offseason/<season>/manifest.json`) recording season, event dates, coaching moves, marquee signings, and draft results.
  - Transactionally applies coaching moves and coordinator tenures, enforcing exactly one active HC, OC, and DC per team.
  - **Signing candidates from League Transactions** (team management step 5, phase 7): `preview_manifest` adds `signing_candidates`, the season's offseason `SIGNED` rows (`transactions.week` NULL; `fetch_offseason_signings`) of players rated 90+ (`SIGNING_CANDIDATE_MIN_OVR`), most expensive first, each with player, position, new team, former team (the signing row's, else the team that last released him, else "unknown (fill in)"), OVR and qualitative tier for JR, and `terms` ("4 years, $96.4M total (from the signing)"). JR still picks the 2–3 marquee signings into the manifest. The signings facts packet gives each manifest signing `signing_terms` from its matching offseason `SIGNED` row (by player name) or "not recorded: state no contract terms" (R5). No transactions table or no offseason rows = no candidates, no failure. `tests/test_offseason_service.py` `TestSigningCandidates` covers the pure helpers.
  - Pre-packages facts bundles with strict temporal cutoffs (`newsroom/run_context.py` `set_offseason_cutoff`). The anti-leakage boundaries bar *offseason* moves only (JR's R2/R3): the Super Bowl stories and the Coaching Carousel may cite in-season signings and releases as season history, and the signing grades cite contract terms only from the signing terms or `get_player_contract`, never inferring a missing one (R5). The three Super Bowl types and the Carousel carry the same narrowing in `article_types.json`; `SUPER_BOWL_RUNNER_UP_POSTMORTEM` and `FREE_AGENCY_SIGNING_GRADES` list the contract tools in `data_queried`.
- **Madden Draft Recap Capture & Ingestion (`madden_capture.py`, `pipeline/extract_draft_results.py`, `pipeline/draft_results.py`):**
  - Live console Remote Play capture via `python3 madden_capture.py --only draft-results [--draft-picks N] [--row-by-row]`: navigates Weekly Hub → DRAFT RECAP tile → permitted Cross → held-L2 dropdown switch to NFL. Defaults to paged mode (10 rows per page across all rounds via `capture_list`), extracted into `draft_results.csv` and `draft_results.json` via `pipeline.extract_draft_results`; optional `--row-by-row` captures picks one by one via player card crops (`pick_NNN.png`).
  - Ingestion via `python3 -m pipeline.draft_results --season <season> --file <path> [--dry-run]` directly enriches `draft_prospects` across all rounds (`actual_round`, `actual_round_pick`, `actual_pick`, `drafted_team_id`, `selection_notes`). Also accessible via `python3 -m pipeline.weekly_capture --mode draft-results`.
- **CLI Runner (`newsroom/offseason_run.py`):**
  - `python3 -m newsroom.offseason_run --season <season> --input <manifest> [--dry-run] [--only super_bowl|coaching|signings|draft] [--force]`
- **REST Endpoints (`server/debug_api.py`):**
  - `POST /api/debug/offseason/preview`: validates manifest and returns structured preview with resolved teams, coaches, slants, qualitative tiers, `signing_candidates` and warnings.
  - `POST /api/debug/offseason/run`: runs the offseason package background job with live log streaming.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_offseason_service.py` (28 tests), `tests/test_offseason_catalog.py` (7 tests), `tests/test_coordinators.py` (14 tests), `tests/test_draft_results.py` (20 tests), `tests/test_extract_draft_results.py` (5 tests), `tests/test_weekly_capture.py` (21 tests).
